Unary PHP in odd-prime resolution over linear equations

Establish superpolynomial proof-line lower bounds for unrestricted DAG-like resolution over linear equations over each fixed odd prime field \(\mathbb F_p\), for ordinary unary \(\mathrm{PHP}^{N+1}_N\); track the functional variant separately.

Origin: starting research record.

Starting point

Created on 21 September 2026 at the user’s request to investigate extension of the ordinary unary-PHP resolution-over-parities candidate beyond characteristic two. This thread is separate from odd-field multiplicity coverings and the main Frege program. Setup was followed by a user-assigned Spin on 21 September 2026.

Where we stand

The unrestricted odd-prime PHP lower bound remains open. A route review parked the block-complex line: ceiling-\(D\) PC refutability depends only on the degree-\((D-1,D)\) quotient datum, so the complex and its filtration can only compute it. A functional-side closed moment tower removes the three-selector obstruction only by saturation, which reproduces the recorded mixed-moment system. Sparse and probe-sparse blocks have polynomial-inventory endpoints, and low- and dense-rank ternary blocks are excluded on separated graphs. A clique of heads lowers the ternary dense threshold to \(\Theta(N\log N)\) for blocks covering a clique of pairs, including random dense ones, leaving a window of about \(10^{12}\Lambda\) for them. Structured intermediate blocks remain open; for \(p\ge5\) no dense-rank criterion is recorded. Spin is paused; this cycle was run on request.

The remaining route

  1. Exclude the complete-board selector source using PHP structure. The direct compiler has degree \(4p-1\). The shared sparse-support case now also allows polynomially many global probes at a controlled degree cost. The direct fixed-size rank/profile dichotomy is refuted, and a dyadic random inventory defeats a common restriction across sizes (calibration argument). The marked homogeneous tensor presentation makes the alternative precise: control degree-local propagation into the old unit for arbitrary compiled tuples \(1-L^{p-1}\) and their coupled coefficient residuals, through the required PC window. The \(p\)-complex now has a general coded-row exactness range for its leading matching-correction layer; one-predicate compatibility and collectively wide ternary pair completion are proved, but arbitrary-family and full-source compatibility remain unproved. The wide three-selector control refutes arbitrary partial-moment extension. The block complex and its filtration are parked: PC refutability depends only on the degree-\((D-1,D)\) quotient datum. The open case is dense-support blocks of intermediate projected rank.
  2. Preserve the base and original-degree accounting. The ordinary compiler permits the weak base; use weak-only maps before adding functionality. No graph restriction is needed for its initial bridge. If a later normalization introduces one, justify its effect rather than importing complete-board stability afterward. Keep all original companions and coefficient fields; the rank-one representation is optional, not a free source change.
  3. Review and extend. Check any ternary mechanism against the ordering and mixed-interface controls, then establish every fixed odd prime. New working proofs are not Lean-verified or novelty-certified; the target remains standard syntactic Res(Lin), not characteristic zero or arbitrary semantic inference.

Proposed next step

Route item: odd-prime-clause-transfer. The user assigned the next cycle to the contracting-homotopy test: express one-block elimination as a perturbation of Razborov's reduction operator, then check whether the two-block series on historical Example 6.5 costs less than whole-level repair. On the intermediate window, the open steps are a Macaulay-quotient bound beyond degree-two heads for random dense blocks and a clique-or-probe dichotomy for structured blocks (clique entry).

Working mathematical context

Target and exact compiler

For every fixed odd prime \(p\) and \(K>0\), seek more than \(N^K\) proof lines for ordinary unary \(\mathrm{PHP}^{N+1}_N\) in standard syntactic DAG-like Res(Lin); track functionality separately. Variables remain Boolean. The rule audit distinguishes written size, proof lines and stronger semantic systems. Growing fields and characteristic zero are outside the target.

The weak-base compiler gives at most \(S\) complete accuracy-one selector blocks, at most \(\binom{v+p-1}{p-1}\) actual input positions per block, and PC degree \(4p-1\). Ordinary PHP needs only Booleanity, column collisions and linear row equations; its wide initial values cost \(2p-1\). Functional-CNF initials still require the functional compiler. Both retain every \(r^p-r\).

The optional rank-one bridge uses \(P=1-rL\), \(LP\) and \(r^p-r\), at comparison cost \(p+1\) and replaced-companion cost \(2p+1\). At \(p=3\), \(r=L\) returns the direct selector source. These names are not free affine inputs.

Moment tools and generic obstructions

The first-active NS lift and all-prime pairing supply low-degree moments and coordinates, not the coupled full-source lift. The pebbling and ordering controls rule out generic NS-only and PC-hardness-only lifting. These controls require PHP-specific structure.

Functional degree-two cleanup and affine boundary

The odd-characteristic classification identifies affine Boolean values modulo \(I_2\) as partial-row/column indicators or complements modulo rows; the older column-only statement used the weak base. At \(p=3\), apply it to \((L+1)/2\) to detect \(1-L^2\in I_2\), also in stable old \(C_B\). The affinization theorem makes \(1-L^2\) affine exactly for row/column-local \(L\) modulo rows. Its query corollary preserves companion cost five and compiler degree eleven. Mixed selector-span cancellations remain unclassified.

Certified nonlinear quotient rank

The complete-source reduction uses \(r_a=\dim(V_a/(V_a\cap\overline{P_{\le1}}))\) in \(Q_2=P_{\le2}/I_2\). Two-sided basis relations with old \(I_2\) certificates preserve companion degree five; the retained representation fits degree at least seven. The profile theorem excludes \(r_a\le N^\sigma\operatorname{polylog}N\), \(\sigma<1\), at polynomial inventory and polylog degree. High rank is necessary, not sufficient for hardness. The two-column family has rank \(N(N+1)\) but coefficient degree two: high-rank alternatives must allow cheap profiles.

High-polar extraction cannot be copied

The common-factor control has high Boolean-reduced polar rank but few disjoint heads under cube-preserving affine changes. Ordinary representatives become affine after a field-valued probe. Binary coordinate changes cannot be imported without their domain equations.

Dense heads without coordinate changes

The distinct-head count gives filtered reduction image at most \(4\binom sk\exp(k^2/s-qk/s^2)\) for \(q\le s(s-1)/4\), \(k\le s/4\). Overlap is allowed; heads must be distinct. Old degree-two relations supply explicit reduction certificates.

The ternary graph criterion adds affine-core maps and nonlinear prefixes: each block needs projected rank at most \(R\) or at least \(Q_*=O(N^{3/2}\sqrt{\log N})\), at polynomial inventory. Its coefficient degree is \(T=O(\sqrt{N\log N}+R)\), with \(TD+k\le N/10^6\). At \(D=11\), \(R\approx N/(4.4\times10^7)\) is allowed. The clique extension admits blocks covering all pairs of \(m_*=O(\sqrt{N\log N})\) coordinates.

Other available tools and their limits

The all-prime disequality compiler remains available, but its full-field conversion obstruction leaves Boolean-domain equation conversion unproved.

The relative matching count still requires compatible separation density and actual head structure. The torus, reserve-coordinate and absolute-budget controls exclude the tested shortcuts, not the whole route.

The reader audit retains unmet global-type, span-count and fresh-moment hypotheses.

The marked presentation keeps original homogenized generators: \(z^D=0\) records NS degree, while \(W_{j+1}=W_j+\sum_y y(W_j:z)\), starting at \(J_D\), records PC closure. Active torsion can be harmless for old targets. Nontrivial positive-degree blocks are nonflat over the old algebra.

The odd-prime amplitude theorem on \(K_s=(A_0/(z))_s^*\) gives \(\ker D^a=\operatorname{im}D^{p-a}\) at degree \(d\) if \(t\ge2d+p-a\) active rows retain constant/predicate rank after \(3d+2(p-a)\) label deletions. Forced coded-row insertion proves it over any characteristic-\(p\) field. The two-form consequence needs collective width \(2d+2p-1\) and robustness through \(3d+3p-1\), over the algebraic closure. It outputs \(K_{d+p-1}\); auxiliary arrays can reach \(d+p+1\). Supplied polarized compatibility and full-source PC closure remain unproved.

The one-predicate theorem for \(q_A(L)=0\), \(a=|A|\), gives prescribed-moment extension, stable NS filtration and PC=NS through \(B\), when \(t\ge2B-a\) and evaluation robustness reaches \(3B-a\) deletions. The ternary pair theorem completes source endpoints from \(D_i z_i=0\) and \(D_1^2z_2=D_2^2z_1\). Two nonzero-value predicates have stable filtration through \(B\) under collective width \(2B+1\) and robustness \(3B+2\). Arbitrary-family, higher-prime and raw coefficient compatibility remain open.

The filtered obstruction quotient identifies reduced-complex \(H_1\), modulo liftable cycles, with \(\ker z\). Identical marked barcodes can have different PC degrees. Ceiling-\(D\) refutability depends only on the marked degree-\((D-1,D)\) datum. Strengthening to the functional base is legitimate after any weak-only maps.

Other removal endpoints

The two-size dense control refutes a fixed-board low-probe/dense-rank dichotomy but enters the dense endpoint after restriction to \(\sqrt N\) holes. No general restriction dichotomy follows.

The later thinning theorem improves the row exponent to every fixed \(\alpha<1\), retaining \(O(\log n)\) probes per block. Compress to polynomially many actual selectors, then leave \(n^\beta\) rows with \(0<\beta<1-\alpha\). Each support has \(u\)-survival probability at most \(n^{-u(1-\alpha-\beta)+o(1)}\); a fixed \(u\) handles the entire inventory. Apply the constant-row endpoint afterward, so the original support size creates no degree factor.

The weak occupancy application permits \(L=c+\sum_j a_j\sigma_j+\sum_i b_iX_{ij_0}\) modulo rows, with a different exceptional column per input. The old affine freezing map leaves \(\lfloor n/(p-1)\rfloor-1\) holes; column-state reduction then makes every selector affine with certificates through \(p-1\), and complete source replacement costs at most \(2p-1\). Do not use functional \(I_2\) reductions before this weak-only map; strengthen the residual base only afterward.

The column-cone normalization has conductor \(z^n\widetilde R\) and kills the old PHP \(z\)-thickness after imposing rows; its defect must be retained. The three-selector control has \(q_1+q_2+q_3=(a-b)^2\), so \(a-b\) follows at degree three. A complete block gives \(TP\in C_6\setminus C_5\), with \(\deg TP=4\). Every pair is collectively wide, but arbitrary partial designs cannot be extended to the triple. The parked derived block complex has at most two positive-resolution factors per term at \(D=4p-1\); no gluing follows.

The shared-support endpoint leaves \(N=n^\beta\), \(\beta<\varepsilon/2\), and constant vertex covers for polynomially many supports of size \(n^{2-\varepsilon}\operatorname{polylog}n\). The mixed-profile extension adds \(t\le n^\alpha\operatorname{polylog}n\) affine probes per block when \(\alpha<\beta\). Direct residue/local-state interpolation has degree \(T=O(t+1)\), creates no new blocks and replays PC through \(TD=o(N)\). It requires degree-preserving old certificates for the supplied feature representations. Functional row states are used after restriction; no subsequent weak-only freezing or multilevel coefficient inputs are covered.

Gaps identified by formalization

No formalization is assigned to this thread. The linked generic binary compiler has recorded Lean coverage; the all-prime graph exclusion and unary composition remain working informal results. The new odd-prime selector translation is an informal working proof with finite local controls only; binary formalization does not verify it or this target. Check linked corrections before mathematical reliance.

21 September 2026 — Equation clauses remain the missing bridge after restoring the earlier odd-prime compiler

Status: source and dependency audit; no new lower bound or compiler. The known full-field obstruction does not cover Boolean-domain simulations. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question and stopping test. Does the existing binary compiler extend by a change of field, yielding a polynomial-in-proof-size one-level old-affine registry of polylogarithmic PC degree? Inspect the precise source rules and earlier conversions, stopping once a transfer or a specific missing implication is identified. The goal remains the standard odd-prime equation-clause lower bound, not a substitute disequality system.

Source convention

Part–Tzameret, revision 2, Section 3, pp. 14–15 defines lines as disjunctions of affine equations, with duplicate disjuncts contracted. From \(C\vee(f=0)\) and \(D\vee(g=0)\), resolution derives \(C\vee D\vee(\alpha f+\beta g=0)\). Weakening adds an equation; simplification deletes a false nonzero constant equation. Boolean axioms are \((x_i=0)\vee(x_i=1)\). The source counts total written size, not just lines. Its stronger semantic systems are distinguished from this syntactic calculus.

Accordingly, the branch keeps its stronger proof-line objective but must prove its own registry-inventory accounting. A lower bound only in written size would not establish that objective. Standard syntactic steps, not unrestricted Boolean-semantic consequence, are the initial target.

Recovered dependencies and falsification attempt

The 14 September disequality calculus already has a complete all-prime rule simulation. Its clause is \(\bigvee_i(g_i\ne0)\), so its falsifying set is an affine flat. Its \(p\)-way split has height-independent PC cost \(4h+p-1\), with all coefficient equations \(r^p-r\) retained. Rebuilding this split would not fill the equation-clause gap.

The same earlier entry contains the exact full-field conversion obstruction: \(\bigvee_{i=1}^w(y_i=0)\) needs \((p-1)^w\) affine-disequality CNF clauses in the same unrestricted coordinates. Its falsifying set is the nonzero box. Every affine subspace contained in that box is a point, since any nonconstant coordinate takes zero somewhere. Exact CNF conversion must cover all box points by these falsifying flats. Thus a blanket polynomial full-field conversion is already refuted by an inherited result. This is a rediscovered applicability boundary, not a new theorem.

The restriction to unrestricted field coordinates is essential. Boolean domains and extra variables change the representation problem, so this obstruction alone cannot rule out domain-aware proof translations. The binary basis compression preserves a common zero set; it cannot simply be applied to a union of equation hyperplanes at odd primes. Existing graph restriction still makes the initial clauses short, but does not bound intermediate equation-clause width.

Outcome and next action

The general implication still needed is: every short standard odd-prime equation-clause PHP refutation yields an augmented graph-PC refutation satisfying the all-prime old-affine exclusion hypotheses, on the same graph and within its degree budget. This audit discharges rule identification and eliminates rediscovery of the disequality compiler; it does not discharge that implication.

The next test is Boolean-domain conversion or a bounded-degree selector encoding. Any use of \(1-L^{p-1}\) as an input must confront that it is generally nonlinear, and any added names must retain their defining equations. Search the existing nonlinear-input and domain-compression results before building a new normalization. The current affine-only approach has a documented full-field limitation, while the domain-aware route remains untested here; no probability estimate or assertion that it is short is justified.

Checks and significance. Read the cited exact internal statements and PC identities, the original equation-clause rules and size definition, and the binary registry transfer. No numerical experiment or new kernel check was performed. No new claim is registered because this is an applicability audit of existing results. The source/version and inspected anchors are recorded in the accompanying audit file.

Process assessment. Bounded claim search recovered both the older compiler and the exact obstruction, avoiding a duplicate proof; the existing tools suffice and no framework change is needed.

21 September 2026 — Standard equation clauses compile at constant degree into power-selector inputs

Status: working proof of a syntactic Res(Lin) transfer; no PHP lower bound. Its one-level inputs are degree-\(p-1\) selectors, generally not affine. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Missing implication tested. Can arbitrary-width standard equation clauses be handled without exponential residue splitting, and with a degree bound independent of DAG height? We allow polynomial selector inputs temporarily but retain their exact form and degree; a successful translation must expose, rather than hide, the remaining exclusion obligation.

Statement and source format

Fix a prime \(p\), put \(\delta=p-1\), and let \(v\) old variables be Boolean. Let a standard syntactic \(\mathrm{Res}(\mathrm{lin}_{\mathbb F_p})\) refutation have \(S\) lines, with input CNF clauses of width at most \(w\). Include Boolean axioms among the initials and set \(w_*=\max(w,2)\). The rules are the resolution, literal weakening, simplification and contraction audited in the preceding entry; arbitrary semantic inference is not assumed.

For an affine equation \(L=0\), set \(t_L=1-L^\delta\). There is a complete one-level ENS family with at most \(S\) blocks, each at accuracy one and having at most \(\binom{v+\delta}{\delta}\) inputs, each input literally some \(t_L\) from its source line. With coefficient equations \(r^p-r\), this family and the ordinary CNF polynomial axioms have an ordinary PC refutation through

\[D=\max\{4p-1,\ p+w_*\delta\}.\]

No proof-height factor occurs. The initial polynomial for \(\bigvee_i(L_i=0)\) is \(F_A=\prod_iL_i\); for Boolean CNF literals these are affine coordinate forms, up to nonzero signs. For fixed \(p\), both the registry inventory and the coefficient-variable count are polynomial in \(S,v\), even if the original intermediate clauses have enormous width. This is an existence statement about a PC refutation and registry, not a bound on monomial-expanded PC length.

Compression and complete blocks

In each line choose a basis subset of its selectors in the vector space of ordinary polynomials of degree at most \(\delta\). Every omitted selector is a constant linear combination of this subset. The bound follows from the displayed ambient dimension. No Boolean quotient is needed for this compression. Empty spans have an empty basis. For the chosen basis \(B_C\), introduce fresh coefficients and set

\[P_C=1-\sum_{j\in B_C}r_{C,j}t_j,\qquad E_{C,j}=t_jP_C.\]

The complete block contains every companion \(E_{C,j}\) and every coefficient field equation. Thus \(\deg P_C\le p\), \(\deg E_{C,j}\le2p-1\). Any original selector in line \(C\) times \(P_C\) is a constant combination of these companions at the same ceiling. No derived line \(P_C\) is added as an axiom. For an empty basis \(P_C=1\), no coefficient variables or companions are required.

Boolean vanishing certificates used below

A polynomial in old Boolean variables that vanishes on the whole Boolean cube has an ordinary Boolean-ideal certificate through its own total degree: reduce each power using \(x_i^2-x_i\) without increasing degree; the remaining multilinear polynomial is zero by evaluation on the cube. This argument works over every field. It will be used only for an explicit local selector identity, not for any PHP consequence.

Initial values and one-premise rules

For an initial clause \(A\), its original selectors satisfy

\[1-\prod_{i=1}^{a}(1-t_i)=\sum_{i=1}^{a}t_i\prod_{j<i}(1-t_j),\qquad \prod_i(1-t_i)=F_A^\delta.\]

Multiply by \(P_A\). Each summand on the right is obtained from \(t_iP_A\), a constant combination of complete companions, by multiplying by the preceding factors. The term \(F_A^\delta P_A\) follows from the initial axiom \(F_A\). All terms have degree at most \(p+a\delta\); for Boolean axioms, \(F_A=x_i(x_i-1)\) is already an old Boolean equation. Hence the initial \(P_A\) is derived within \(D\).

For weakening from \(A\) to \(T\), every basis selector of \(A\) lies in the selector span of \(T\). The identity \(P_T=P_AP_T+\sum_jr_{A,j}t_jP_T\) derives \(P_T\) from the completed line \(P_A\) and the \(T\)-companions through \(\max(D,2p)\). Simplification deletes only \(a=0\) with \(a\ne0\), whose selector is identically zero, so the same argument applies. Contraction leaves the span unchanged.

Resolution and exact degree ledger

Consider premises \(A=C\vee(f=0)\), \(B=E\vee(g=0)\), and conclusion \(T=C\vee E\vee(h=0)\), where \(h=\alpha f+\beta g\). In the basis subset of \(A\), all selectors except possibly \(t_f\) come from \(C\); let \(r_f\) be its coefficient if selected, or zero otherwise. Likewise define \(r_g\). A selector that also occurs in the context can still be designated as pivot; the remaining ones are context selectors. Using the completed premise values and the \(T\)-companions derives

\[R_A=P_T-r_ft_fP_T=0,\qquad R_B=P_T-r_gt_gP_T=0\]

through \(\max(D,2p)\). For example \(R_A=P_AP_T+\sum_{j\text{ context}}r_{A,j}t_jP_T\). Each context selector is in the selector span of \(T\).

At every old Boolean assignment, \(t_ft_g(1-t_h)=0\): if both first selectors are one then \(f=g=0\), hence \(h=0\); otherwise their product is zero. Let \(I=t_ft_g(1-t_h)\). Its degree is at most \(3\delta\), so it has the Boolean certificate just proved. Also \(t_hP_T\) is a constant combination of \(T\)-companions. Consequently

\[Q=t_ft_gP_T=t_ft_g(t_hP_T)+IP_T=0\]

has a PC derivation through \(\max(D,4p-3)\). The last local step is the ordinary polynomial identity

\[P_T=R_A+r_ft_fR_B+r_fr_gQ.\]

The middle product has degree at most \(3p\). Constructing the last term by multiplying the completed \(Q\) line by its two coefficient variables costs at most \(4p-1\) even if its derivation previously reached \(4p-3\). Thus the entire inference fits \(D\). Multiplication by a polynomial means its expansion into monomials, each implemented by variable multiplications followed by field-linear combinations. Crucially, multiply the completed premise lines rather than their whole derivations; the degree does not accumulate along the DAG.

Induct through the original topological line order, in one fixed registry. The empty final clause has \(P_\varnothing=1\), so this yields the asserted PC refutation. Coefficient field equations remain in the source although this forward simulation does not need them. The empty-initial-clause case already has axiom one and is immediate.

What this does and does not settle

This proves a working translation for the actual standard equation-clause rules, with polynomial inventory and constant degree at constant initial width. After the usual graph restriction, the missing implication becomes exclusion of complete one-level families whose inputs are these specific shifted powers of affine forms. The all-prime affine theorem does not cover them. The earlier odd-prime nonlinearity control already shows that Boolean reduction does not generally make them affine. We therefore obtain no Res(Lin) lower bound here.

For \(p=3\) the precise target inputs are \(1-L^2\), not arbitrary quadratic polynomials. The next cycle should test a common-weight exclusion for that family, preserving its structure before demanding a theorem for all bounded-degree inputs. The known full-field conversion obstruction is respected: this transfer uses nonlinear inputs and does not assert an equivalent affine-disequality CNF.

Checks, dependencies and assessment

The proof above is self-contained given the audited syntactic rules, elementary polynomial linear algebra and ordinary PC definitions. It adapts the existing complete-block replay pattern; no semantic weakening theorem or binary compression theorem is invoked at odd primes. Exact finite tests over \(\mathbb F_3,\mathbb F_5,\mathbb F_7\) cover every \((f,g,\alpha,\beta)\), and removing the second-premise selector produces counterexamples in each field. The saved results check that local relation only; they are not a full primitive-PC or Lean verification.

Focused review. Basis compression uses literal polynomial spans, not unproved semantic implications. Initials retain their product axioms. Zero coefficients, absent pivots, duplicate selectors and empty bases are allowed. Both premise selectors are essential, as the negative controls demonstrate. Original input degree \(p-1\) is charged before any reduction; no auxiliary names or omitted defining equations are introduced. The remaining lower-bound obligation is untouched by simply observing that this degree is constant.

Contribution and process. This cycle discharges the forward syntactic transfer into a precisely restricted nonlinear source; it does not discharge source exclusion. That makes the next mathematical test concrete. Reusing the earlier disequality ledger avoided redundant rule development, and no framework change is warranted.

21 September 2026 — Full-field selector zero sets need not shrink the graph weight space

Status: working obstruction to one proposed restriction map, not to the selector compiler or to Boolean-domain normalization. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Test. The affine exclusion proof selects a common weight from kernels of restrictions to high-rank zero flats. Can the same step use the full-field common-zero set of many independent power selectors instead? The required implication is a small image of the actual graph-separated weight space, not merely a small geometric zero set. Stop if even one allowed selector tuple has injective restriction on that weight space.

A maximal-rank tuple with no multilinear restriction kernel

Fix an odd prime \(p\), old coordinates \(Y_1,\ldots,Y_s\), and the legal selector inputs \(g_i=1-Y_i^{p-1}\). Their underlying affine forms have rank \(s\), and the \(g_i\) are linearly independent ordinary polynomials. Their full-field common-zero set is \((\mathbb F_p^\times)^s\). Restriction to this set is injective on the whole space of multilinear polynomials, hence on every graph weight space spanned by squarefree matching monomials.

Proof. Choose distinct nonzero \(a,b\in\mathbb F_p\). A multilinear polynomial vanishing on \(\{a,b\}^s\) is zero. Induct on \(s\): write it as \(A(Y_1,\ldots,Y_{s-1})+Y_sB(Y_1,\ldots,Y_{s-1})\). Evaluating at \(a,b\) and subtracting gives \((a-b)B=0\) on the smaller grid, so the induction hypothesis gives \(B=0\) and then \(A=0\). The base case is constant polynomials. Since the two-point grid lies in the common-zero set, injectivity follows. In particular no nonzero graph weight can lie in the ordinary ideal generated by these selectors: membership would force vanishing on that set. Linear independence of the inputs follows by comparing the distinct monomials \(Y_i^{p-1}\). This proves the assertion.

The failure already occurs for \(p=3\) and a single accuracy-one block. An arbitrary equation clause listing the coordinate equations supplies precisely this kind of selector tuple, so the compiler does not prohibit it. This is a countercontrol to a universal normalization argument for its source class, not an assertion that a hard PHP refutation necessarily contains this line.

Why this does not refute the Boolean route

On the old Boolean cube, \(g_i\) equals \(1-Y_i\). Their common-zero set there is just the all-one assignment. For positive degree, a space spanned by degree-\(k\) matching monomials restricts to constants on that point and has image dimension at most one. For \(p=3\), the identity is explicitly \(g_i-(1-Y_i)=-(Y_i^2-Y_i)\); for general \(p\), the difference is a Boolean-ideal consequence through degree \(p-1\). Thus the full-field injectivity and the Boolean-domain collapse are compatible. The normalization must preserve Boolean relations rather than demand literal ideal membership in the selector ideal alone.

This control is not a proof that every selector tuple has a large affine part after Boolean reduction. The existing two-bit control gives \(1-(x+y)^{p-1}=(1-x)(1-y)\) on Boolean inputs for odd \(p\), whose quadratic term does not vanish. Coordinate selectors are therefore a falsification control, not a general solution.

Comparison with available nonlinear machinery

The binary quadratic rank-or-leading-pairs criterion retains the actual input span modulo Boolean equations and separates its affine part. Its high case additionally requires disjoint squarefree leading pairs and its dimension budget uses the compact binary cube-volume multiplier space. Its proof cannot simply be applied to a ternary graph-PHP weight space, or to arbitrary odd-prime affine coordinate changes, which need not preserve the Boolean cube. The later binary cubic endpoint is likewise not an all-prime theorem.

Outcome and next test. The full-field restriction variant is refuted, while the actual lower-bound implication remains open. Next examine the Boolean-reduced span of ternary selector inputs: separate its affine part and determine whether the remaining quadratic span admits an affordable common-weight map on the graph base. Check both coordinate selectors and selectors of sums of two bits. A successful theorem must work for arbitrary compiled tuples and must budget image loss against the actual graph weight dimension; a binary cube estimate is not a substitute.

Significance and checks. This elementary interpolation argument is a route-specific negative result, with no novelty claim. It uses no computation or new imported theorem, and no lower bound is asserted. The self-contained proof checks both the full-field kernel and the Boolean-domain countercontrol.

Process assessment. Testing the proposed map on a maximal-rank coordinate tuple ruled it out before developing a general Hilbert-function estimate; the existing search and excerpt tools were sufficient.

21 September 2026 — Existing all-prime quadratic maps do not yet meet the graph weight budget

Status: parameter audit of inherited bounds. The raw estimates do not prove a common kernel; no impossibility of a better relative map is claimed. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

General claim under test. Combine a Boolean-domain quadratic reduction with the graph-separated multiplier space to exclude the ternary selector source. The goal needs contraction of the actual graph weight space sufficient for polynomially many blocks, plus affordable original-degree certificates. Merely recognizing selectors as quadratics does not give this.

Recovered positive ingredient. The leading-pair reduction lemma works over every prime, provided the original Boolean-coordinate input span supplies disjoint squarefree leading pairs. Its kernel has ordinary membership certificates modulo old Booleanity. The all-prime application already combines it with a compact bit-PHP row-cube separator; it is not restricted to characteristic two. However, that application does not use our graph unary base. This distinction sharpens the previous cycle’s comparison with the binary quadratic and cubic endpoints.

Budget test. Write \(s=15(N+1)\). The current graph construction guarantees a degree-\(k\) separated space of dimension at least \(\tfrac12e^{-5k^2/N}\binom sk\), in its stated range. The inherited raw leading-pair image estimate is \((s+1)e^{-Rk^2/s^2}\binom sk\), with \(R\le s/2\). Their ratio (image upper bound divided by available dimension lower bound) is

\[2(s+1)\exp\left(\left(\frac5N-\frac R{s^2}\right)k^2\right)>1,\]

because \(R/s^2\le1/(2s)<5/N\). Thus these two displayed bounds cannot certify even one image smaller than the guaranteed weight space; multiplying by the number of blocks only worsens the comparison. This holds even if disjoint leading pairs are supplied for free. It does not compare the actual image and actual weight dimensions, which may be substantially better. In particular, it is not a proof that no useful kernel exists.

Why the compact cube proof does not remove this loss automatically. The compact multiplier construction has nearly the full degree-\(k\) monomial dimension because it may choose disjoint label cubes for each target monomial from the full label set. Sparse graph rows are confined to their prescribed neighbors; that cube-choice proof does not supply the same freedom. Conversely, a direct unary-to-bit substitution turns cells into label indicators of logarithmic degree and must be charged in every original input and initial axiom. Neither conversion has been proved here to preserve the needed budget.

Route assessment and alternative. The local all-prime map is a useful existing tool, but the present composition lacks both a structure theorem for arbitrary selector spans and a compatible graph-relative dimension estimate. The numerical mismatch means that parameter tuning of these raw bounds is not the next task. The next test should count reduction images relative to the graph matching space, with old row/hole equations explicitly included, and distinguish genuinely new leading constraints from old zero monomials. Such a theorem remains unproved.

Checks and process. Read the exact graph bound, local reduction lemma, all-prime compact application and cube-volume proof, and compare their hypotheses and displayed inequalities. No computational or formal verification is claimed. This audit introduces no new structural claim; bounded dependency retrieval found a stronger all-prime ingredient and prevented an unnecessary field-generalization exercise.

21 September 2026 — A relative count removes the shared collision loss for genuinely new quadratic heads

Status: working relative counting and reduction lemma; graph separation density and selector-head structure remain unproved. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Obligation and test. The raw quadratic image bound failed against the graph weight guarantee. Test whether counting both spaces relative to the same row/hole collision graph removes that mismatch. Stop at a proved relative count with explicit polynomial-membership scope, not an automatic lower bound.

Prior-work correction. Further targeted search found that the preceding cycle’s parameter mismatch was already recorded more generally as the graph absolute-head budget obstruction. The preceding entry is an application/rediscovery of that result, not new mathematics. Its next-action warning is retained. The strong-graph relative window and complete-board old-target preservation were also inspected: neither supplies the graph density and degree range demanded below for free.

Relative independent-set count

Let \(G\) be a simple graph on \(s\) vertices of maximum degree \(\Delta\), and let \(H\) consist of \(R\) vertex-disjoint pairs that are not edges of \(G\). Write \(Z_t(J)\) for the number of independent \(t\)-sets of graph \(J\). Put \(D=\Delta+1\), and suppose

\[2\le k\le\frac{s}{8(D+1)}.\]

Then

\[\frac{Z_k(G+H)}{Z_k(G)}\le\left(1-\frac{k(k-1)}{2s^2}\right)^R\le\exp\left(-\frac{Rk(k-1)}{2s^2}\right).\tag{RH-relative}\]

All denominators are positive: the greedy extension count below constructs independent sets up to the stated size. This is an estimate relative to the original graph’s independent sets, not to \(\binom sk\).

Proof by successive pair exclusions

For any graph \(J\) of maximum degree at most \(D\), count extensions of independent \((t-1)\)-sets. Each has at least \(s-(t-1)(D+1)\) available vertices. Hence

\[tZ_t(J)\ge[s-(t-1)(D+1)]Z_{t-1}(J).\]

For a uniform independent \(t\)-set and any vertex \(v\), deletion of \(v\) shows that its inclusion probability is at most \(Z_{t-1}(J)/Z_t(J)\), and consequently at most \(t/[s-(t-1)(D+1)]\). For \(t=0\) the inclusion probability is zero.

Let \(u,v\) be a nonedge of \(J\), and set \(U=N_J[u]\cup N_J[v]\), so \(|U|\le2(D+1)\). A union bound using the preceding marginals shows that at least the fraction

\[1-\frac{2(D+1)(k-2)}{s-(k-3)(D+1)}\ge\frac12\]

of all independent \((k-2)\)-sets avoid \(U\); for \(k=2\) that fraction is one. The inequality follows from \(k(D+1)\le s/8\): the subtracted term is at most \(2/7\). Each avoiding set extends uniquely by \(u,v\). On the other hand, double deletion bounds

\[k(k-1)Z_k(J)\le s^2 Z_{k-2}(J).\]

Indeed every independent \(k\)-set has \(k(k-1)\) ordered deletions, while any remaining \((k-2)\)-set has at most \(s^2\) ordered extensions. Thus the uniform independent \(k\)-set contains both endpoints with probability at least \(k(k-1)/(2s^2)\).

Add the pairs of \(H\) one at a time. Each is still a nonedge when added, and every intermediate graph has maximum degree at most \(\Delta+1=D\), since \(H\) is a matching. Adding that edge removes at least the stated fraction of independent \(k\)-sets. Multiplying these bounds proves (RH-relative), and \(1-x\le e^{-x}\) gives its exponential form. No independence of the pair events was assumed.

Degree-filtered image consequence in the original Boolean coordinates

Suppose old polynomial axioms include \(x_i^2-x_i\) and \(x_ux_v\) for every edge \(uv\) of \(G\). Suppose a tuple of actual quadratic inputs supplies constant combinations with monic, squarefree, pairwise disjoint leading monomials corresponding to \(H\), under one degree-compatible monomial order; Boolean reductions of those combinations are allowed with their degree-two certificates. Deterministic division by these polynomials and the displayed old monomials gives a linear map through degree \(k\). Its normal monomials are independent sets of \(G+H\). As in the inherited reduction argument, no Gröbner-basis assertion is required.

The extension count above, now for \(G+H\), gives \(Z_{t-1}/Z_t\le1/7\) for \(1\le t\le k\) under our hypotheses. Therefore its image dimension is at most

\[\sum_{t=0}^kZ_t(G+H)\le2Z_k(G+H)\le2\exp\left(-\frac{Rk(k-1)}{2s^2}\right)Z_k(G).\tag{RH-image}\]

A kernel polynomial has a degree-\(k\) certificate in the actual input ideal plus the explicitly used old Boolean and collision axioms. Original input cofactors have degree at most \(k-2\). All rewrite steps lower the monomial order and do not increase total degree; replacing the chosen combinations by original inputs preserves this ceiling. This is a Boolean-domain relative image estimate over any field.

Exact connection and remaining gap

For graph PHP, take vertices to be nondistinguished cells and let \(G\) join cells in a common row or hole. Its edges are genuine old degree-two consequences in the functional graph base. Independent sets correspond to matching monomials. A candidate head already in \(G\) is not a new constraint and must not be counted in \(R\). If a separated homogeneous weight space \(W\) has \(\dim W\ge cZ_k(G)\), then \(M\) such reduction maps with common lower head count \(R\) have a nonzero common kernel provided

\[2M\exp\left(-\frac{Rk(k-1)}{2s^2}\right)<c.\]

This last implication is linear algebra: the combined image dimension is smaller than \(\dim W\). It assumes the stated density and head data. Neither has been established for every compiled selector family. The fixed distinguished alternatives used by the existing graph detector may impose extra conflicts that are not old zero monomials. They cannot be inserted into \(G\) as free reduction axioms. Nor can the displayed conditional kernel alone certify all weighted ENS images without the original-degree replay ledger.

Next obligation. Determine whether a graph-separated matching space has adequate density relative to this actual old collision graph in the range \(k\gg\sqrt N\), or exhibit a failure. Read the existing robust compatible-pair separation before trying to replace its distinguished alternatives. If a suitable density is obtained, arbitrary selector spans still need a structural alternative supplying genuine new heads or an affordable low-rank map.

Checks and process

Exact compiled counting controls use an empty graph on 64 vertices at \(k=4\) and a cycle on 96 vertices at \(k=3\), adding the antipodal matching. All subset counts and rational product-bound comparisons are in the saved check report. The largest enumeration has \(\binom{64}{4}=635376\) candidates; these fixed tests are not a large search. The negative control treats an existing edge as a new head: the ratio remains one, violating the proposed strict reduction, as it should. These finite counts do not verify graph-PHP separation or all asymptotic hypotheses.

Contribution. The relative image estimate removes the shared old-collision loss from one part of the proposed comparison. The full selector-source exclusion remains open, with two explicit obligations: a dense separated matching space and a structural alternative for the actual input spans. This is a reusable working counting lemma, not a lower bound or novelty certification.

Process assessment. Targeted search exposed the preceding cycle’s rediscovery; counting the genuine old relations, rather than tuning that old obstruction, produced a new relative estimate with a falsifying existing-edge control.

21 September 2026 — Extra detector cells cannot be hidden from the selector reduction

Status: working Boolean-domain obstruction to a support-only kernel shortcut; no refutation of graph-PHP hardness or of a base-aware normalization. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

General mechanism tested. Enlarge the row’s allowed choices to provide alternative labels outside the weight support, then count reduction images only on that support. The required implication is that a large separated weight space on the chosen cells still loses dimension under reductions by actual selector inputs, which may read all cells. We test that compatibility before proving a stronger detector-density theorem.

The complete-board sparse-support detector can choose alternatives outside the support of the polynomial being detected. The fixed-graph detector instead uses fixed distinguished neighbors and explicit compatibility. Giving rows a larger reserve may help label selection, but reserve variables remain old variables in actual proof clauses. They cannot be omitted from reduction-image accounting or fixed to constants without a justified source transfer.

A ternary family with disjoint support heads and surjective Boolean projection

Over \(\mathbb F_3\), take disjoint triples of Boolean variables \((x_i,y_i,z_i)\), \(1\le i\le r\), and selector inputs

\[q_i=1-(x_i+y_i+z_i)^2.\]

Designate \(x_i,y_i\) as the weight-support variables and \(z_i\) as reserve variables. For every Boolean assignment to all support variables, choose

\[z_i=(1-x_i)(1-y_i).\]

Then \(x_i+y_i+z_i\) is one at \((x_i,y_i)=(0,0),(0,1),(1,0)\), and two at \((1,1)\). In every case it is nonzero in \(\mathbb F_3\), so every \(q_i\) vanishes. Thus the Boolean common-zero set of the whole tuple projects onto the full support cube.

Consequence and proof. No nonzero multilinear polynomial solely in the \(x_i,y_i\) can belong to the ideal generated by these selectors and all old Boolean equations. Such membership would force it to vanish on every lifted Boolean assignment, hence on every support assignment. The usual induction on Boolean coordinates then says that the multilinear polynomial is zero. This rules out a support-only ideal kernel at every degree, without relying on a dimension approximation.

Nevertheless the Boolean representative of each selector is

\[\overline q_i=1-x_i-y_i-z_i+x_iy_i+x_iz_i+y_iz_i.\]

The difference from \(q_i\) is a combination of the three Boolean equations through degree two. Under a degree-lexicographic order placing all support variables before all reserve variables, its leading monomial is \(x_iy_i\). These \(r\) heads are squarefree, pairwise disjoint, and entirely in the designated support. Therefore those properties alone do not imply contraction when the ambient dimension is counted using only support coordinates. The actual reduction may replace a support monomial by terms containing reserve coordinates.

This does not contradict the relative counting lemma. That lemma counts normal monomials in the whole ambient variable set of its reduction. With all \(3r\) variables included its stated dimension bound remains the applicable one; replacing that ambient set by \(2r\) variables would be an additional false assertion. The example is already a legal selector tuple for an equation clause, without extra names or nonstandard inputs.

Scope, route assessment and next direction

The obstruction uses only the Boolean-domain ideal. It does not say that a polynomial cannot enter the ideal after additional graph-PHP equations are used, nor that graph-PHP has a short refutation. It also does not disprove a detector construction with extra cells. It shows that such a detector alone cannot provide a universal support-only selector kernel through the available domain reduction.

The current dimension-count line has produced a useful conditional relative estimate, but neither of its decisive hypotheses is automatic: the separated space and the reduction must use compatible ambient coordinates, and arbitrary selectors still need structural treatment. Two proposed shortcuts have failed on explicit allowed tuples: full-field restriction and hidden reserve coordinates. It is therefore premature to refine a support-density theorem while leaving this compatibility problem untouched.

Next test. Re-express the actual selectors as genuine rank-one affine-block values and inspect the existing pure-reader and retained-product tools. This is an alternative to the failing support-only comparison, not an assumed new solution: any naming must retain complete companions and coefficient domains, and the source equivalence must have a constant original-degree certificate. If the existing tools demand a global sublinear number of distinct forms or another unproved condition, record that exact condition and test it against arbitrary compiled clauses before developing the approach.

Checks and significance. The four possible support-bit pairs give the displayed explicit lifting; multilinear interpolation proves the all-\(r\) conclusion. No numerical or Lean verification was performed. This is a route-specific source-compatibility obstruction, with no novelty claim or general Res(Lin) lower-bound consequence.

Process assessment. Checking a single legal selector family prevented a potentially lengthy detector-density construction whose reserve-variable costs would not satisfy the actual compiler interface.

21 September 2026 — Equation selectors have a constant-degree bridge to genuine rank-one products

Status: working source bridge with no degree increase at the recorded compiler ceiling; existing reader exclusions still have unmet inventory/rank hypotheses. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Test and endpoint. Replace the nonlinear selector representation by complete rank-one affine bottoms, without discarding definitions or changing the proof target, and check whether existing retained-product tools close the gap. Stop at an exact source bridge and a justified applicability verdict, rather than treating a renamed selector as an affine input.

Rank-one product equality and source transfer

Fix an odd prime \(p\), old Boolean variables, an affine form \(L\), and \(t=1-L^{p-1}\). Introduce one fresh accuracy-one complete affine block with coefficient \(r\), product \(P=1-rL\), companion \(E=LP\), and coefficient equation \(r^p-r\). Then \(P-t\) has an ordinary PC (indeed NS) derivation through degree \(p+1\), using the companion and old Booleanity.

Proof. The exact polynomial identity is

\[P-t=L^{p-2}E-r(L-L^p).\tag{RS-equality}\]

The affine polynomial \(L^p-L\) has a Boolean-ideal certificate through degree \(p\): Frobenius expresses it as a constant linear combination of \(x_i^p-x_i\), and \(x^p-x=(x^2-x)\sum_{j=0}^{p-2}x^j\). Both terms of (RS-equality) fit degree \(p+1\). No unproved semantic equivalence, coefficient restriction or extra defining axiom is used. The coefficient field equation remains in the complete source, even though this identity does not use it.

Now take any complete accuracy-one parent block on selectors \(t_1,\ldots,t_b\), with coefficients \(s_i\), product \(A=1-\sum_i s_it_i\), companions \(t_iA\), and fields \(s_i^p-s_i\). Replace it by a parent reading the genuine products \(P_i\) of fresh rank-one affine bottoms, with product \(Q=1-\sum_i s_iP_i\) and companions \(P_iQ\); keep every bottom companion and field. Sharing a bottom for repeated identical \(L_i\) is optional, and one may instead keep private copies.

The difference \(A-Q=\sum_i s_i(P_i-t_i)\) has a certificate through \(p+2\). For each original parent companion, use

\[t_iA=P_iQ+(t_i-P_i)Q+t_i(A-Q).\tag{RS-parent}\]

The new parent product has ordinary degree at most three, and its companions have degree at most five. The second term in (RS-parent) has a certificate through \(p+4\); the third through \(2p+1\). Since \(p\ge3\), the common ceiling is \(2p+1\). Consequently every degree-\(D\) PC refutation of the original selector source replays over this complete two-level source through \(\max(D,2p+1)\). Derive each replaced axiom once, then replay the original proof in its unchanged old and parent-coefficient variables; arbitrary coefficient use is allowed and no proof-height factor is introduced.

For the standard clause compiler, \(D\ge4p-1\), so there is no increase at all. The number of bottom blocks is at most the original selector occurrence inventory, itself at most \(S\binom{v+p-1}{p-1}\), and there are at most \(S\) parents. All parent inputs are individual positive whole products, not old-affine polynomials. Original expanded degrees are bottom product two/companion three and parent product three/companion five, plus field degree \(p\); constant/zero cases can be cleaned without larger cost.

Applicability audit of the existing reader tools

This is a genuine special case of the pure-reader source format: above the affine bottoms, inputs read only whole products. But that theorem charges the total number \(a\) of distinct proper nonzero bottom affine spans, with required old ceiling \(B=TD+(p-1)a\), where \(T=\max(1,p-2)\) at unit accuracy, and requires \(N\ge2B-1\). The compiler supplies a polynomial occurrence bound, not a sublinear bound on \(a\). The theorem therefore does not exclude every compiled source.

This is a real unsupported hypothesis, not an automatically small parameter: clauses may contain many distinct coordinate equations, already providing linearly many distinct one-dimensional spans in the number of variables. For graph PHP the variable count is linear in \(N\), and there is no proved coefficient small enough for the displayed additive budget, let alone a uniform sublinear bound. Adding or removing redundant bottom occurrences does not merge distinct affine spans.

The complement-exposed parent theorem also does not apply just because these are products. Our parent inputs are \(P_i\), whereas that theorem requires the actual parent span to expose \(P_i-1\), together with its other stated forms. With distinct proper private bottoms, the positive products need not span these complements. Evaluating all bottom coefficients at zero makes every \(P_i\) equal one; a purported identity expressing a constant through their span must also respect the independent nonconstant coefficient monomials. Comparing those monomials forces each coefficient to zero, so no nonzero constant lies in that span. Thus subtraction of one is not free. This is the already recorded positive-product boundary in the compiler-coverage audit, not a new obstruction theorem.

Finally, the affine-core semantic map measures the actual nonlinear quotient after bottom values are substituted. Substitution here gives exactly our original selectors. Its extra rank hypothesis must still be proved, rather than inferred from each bottom having rank one.

Outcome, review and next obligation

The source bridge is a useful explicit connection to existing retained-product machinery, but it does not solve the lower bound: the known pure-reader estimate depends on too many distinct bottom types, and the complement-exposed hypothesis is not guaranteed. This recovers the same hard positive-product family in a complete source, with no hidden field, definition or degree cost.

Focused review. Checked (RS-equality) as a literal polynomial identity, included every coefficient field, charged multiplication by the actual cubic parent product, and replayed original selector companions instead of pretending the substitution is a homomorphism on old variables. The argument covers private copies and shared identical forms. No numerical experiment, independent proof review or Lean formalization is claimed.

Next test. Inspect the existing span-stratum and common-functional tools specifically for parents listing rank-one positive products. Determine whether their consistency conditions can be established without an additive charge for every distinct affine form. A candidate must handle polynomially many arbitrary forms and retain the same low-degree source; a result requiring a bounded number of forms would not discharge this obligation.

Process assessment. The exact companion identity turns naming into a checkable constant-degree bridge; immediate hypothesis comparison prevents mistaking the existing reader theorem for an unrestricted solution.

21 September 2026 — Rank-one bottoms do not discharge the retained-parent consistency conditions

Status: inherited-tool audit; the exact positive-product parent extension remains unproved. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

General implication tested. The rank-one source bridge is useful only if complete positive-product parents can be added without an unaffordable global type count. Test the existing span-stratum construction and coherent flat-conditionals theorem against arbitrary polynomial-inventory compiled families. Stop at their actual hypotheses; do not treat a sufficient special case as the full equation-clause target.

Span enumeration still sees collective complexity

The span-stratum theorem counts every distinct proper span generated by bottom subcollections through rank \(R\), plus boundary tests. If the bottom affine forms \(L_1,\ldots,L_a\) are independent and homogeneous, then their one-dimensional spans generate exactly \(\sum_{j=0}^{\min(R,a)}\binom aj\) distinct spans through rank \(R\). Different index subsets have different spans by independence. This is the rank-one instance of the direct-sum counting mechanism behind the existing cutoff obstruction; individual rank one does not make the count polynomial for a growing cutoff.

For a parent listing all corresponding products, the theorem’s displayed sufficient inventory includes this count. In its notation \(k=\lceil\sqrt{s\ln(16(\mathcal M+1))/A_0}\rceil\) and \(R\ge(4A_0+1)k\). When \(1\le R\le a/2\), the elementary bound \(\ln\binom aR\ge R\ln(a/R)\) makes the cutoff requirement grow with the low-span count itself. It cannot be replaced by the original number of bottom blocks. Distinct reference tuples may be easy or redundant in particular sources, but exploiting that would be an additional argument, not an application of the displayed bound.

The affine-core map likewise measures the span after substituting all bottom indicators, not their individual ranks. For example the homogeneous forms \(x_i+y_i\) on disjoint bit pairs are independent rank-one forms, while over \(\mathbb F_3\) their selectors are the independent nonlinear functions \((1-x_i)(1-y_i)\). Their distinct quadratic monomials prevent a nonzero combination from being affine on the full Boolean cube. Thus an arbitrary parent may retain a growing nonlinear quotient even though every bottom has rank one. This is a scope control, not a claim that this particular family is hard by every other method.

Coherent flat conditionals stop before fresh parent moments

The old-target preservation corollary supplies one retained annihilator and compatible components \(\mu_A(q)=\lambda(\chi_Aq)\) for a finite family of live affine flats within its degree cutoff. Its proof explicitly limits intersections by their selector-degree costs. It does not assign the fresh parent coefficient moments. In our source, a parent’s all-zero input event is simultaneous nonvanishing of its bottom affine forms, rather than a single affine flat. Expanding that event uses an arrangement of intersections; its degree/count costs cannot be discarded.

The earlier singleton-consistency analysis demonstrates the relevant logical boundary: compatible low prescriptions extend only if their prescribed value vanishes on every linear dependence in the actual domain. Its displayed collision residual and parameter window belong to that older binary setting and are not imported as a ternary proof. They provide a template for extracting the correct residual condition here, not an assertion that it vanishes.

Verdict and next action

The current line has discharged syntactic source construction, not low-degree source exclusion. Existing unconditional-looking shortcuts all rely on extra conditions: polynomial low-span enumeration, affordable global type count, compatible ambient image dimensions, or a fresh coefficient-moment extension. None has been verified for the full compiled family. The next task must address the last condition directly for our exact accuracy-one parent and rank-one bottoms, with both original degree and old PHP relations retained. It should end with an actual consistency proof or a specific residual witness, not another equivalent formulation labeled as a solution.

Checks and process. Read the exact source, inventory, cutoff and coherence statements, plus the earlier consistency residual. No new computation or formalization was performed. The audit prevents repeated attempts to use already recorded sufficient conditions without their hypotheses; no framework change is warranted.

21 September 2026 — All quadratic parents admit a joint NS lift at their first active degree

Status: working degree-five joint NS extension, with a sharp retained-cutoff control at p=3. It is not an augmented-PC annihilator or the compiler-degree endpoint. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Concrete test. At the first degree where genuine accuracy-one quadratic-parent companions are active, determine whether arbitrary parent count already causes incompatible moment prescriptions. Stop with an actual joint extension or a residual counterexample. This tests the initial consistency mechanism, not the full lower-bound target.

Statement

Fix an odd prime \(p\). Let \(\Gamma\subseteq\mathbb F_p[y]\) be a retained system whose variables have field equations, or certificates for them through degree \(p\). Let \(\lambda:\mathbb F_p[y]_{\le B}\to\mathbb F_p\) be normalized and annihilate the original degree-\(B\) NS consequence space \(\mathcal I_B(\Gamma)\), where \(B=\max(5,2p)\).

Add any finite collection of complete accuracy-one parents, with private coefficient variables, no dependencies among parents, and nonzero inputs \(g_{a,i}(y)\) of actual ordinary degree exactly two. Each parent has

\[Q_a=1-\sum_j s_{a,j}g_{a,j},\qquad g_{a,i}Q_a,\qquad s_{a,j}^p-s_{a,j}.\]

There is a normalized ordinary degree-five NS functional for the entire augmented system agreeing with \(\lambda\) on all retained polynomials through degree five. There is no arity or parent-count bound. The exact-degree hypothesis makes every companion’s original degree five; lower-degree inputs would activate additional multiplier constraints and are outside this statement.

The only active parent equations

Put \(V=\mathbb F_p[y]_{\le4}/\mathcal I_4(\Gamma)\). For a fixed parent, seek functionals \(\mu_j\in V^*\) such that

\[\sum_j\mu_j(g_i g_j)=\lambda(g_i)\quad\text{for every }i.\tag{FA-system}\]

These are the companion equations at degree five: their legal cofactor is only a scalar. We now prove consistency, rather than postulating it.

For a vector \((c_i)_i\in\mathbb F_p^b\), put \(G=\sum_i c_i g_i\). A linear dependence among the left-hand coefficient rows of (FA-system) means precisely

\[Gg_j\in\mathcal I_4(\Gamma)\quad\text{for every }j.\]

Hence \(G^2=\sum_jc_jGg_j\in\mathcal I_4(\Gamma)\). Multiplying its certificate by \(G^{p-2}\) puts \(G^p\) in \(\mathcal I_{2p}(\Gamma)\). Since \(\deg G\le2\), Frobenius and telescoping monomials give \(G^p-G\in\mathcal I_{2p}(\Gamma)\) from the retained field equations. Thus \(G\in\mathcal I_B(\Gamma)\), so \(\sum_i c_i\lambda(g_i)=\lambda(G)=0\). Every coefficient-row dependence therefore also annihilates the prescribed right side. Finite-dimensional linear algebra supplies the \(\mu_j\). They may be chosen over \(\mathbb F_p\); no positivity or prescribed mixed moments are needed.

A single functional on all fresh variables

Reduce fresh coefficient monomials by \(s^p=s\), which never increases degree and never removes the support of a positive exponent. On monomials of total degree at most five, define \(\Lambda\) as follows: with no fresh factor, use \(\lambda\); with canonical fresh monomial exactly \(s_{a,j}\), use \(\mu_{a,j}\) on the retained factor; with every other nonempty canonical fresh monomial, use zero. Extend linearly. The retained argument of \(\mu_{a,j}\) has degree at most four, so it is in its domain. The construction uses one solved system (FA-system) per parent.

Fresh field equations and their legal multiples vanish by canonical reduction. A retained generator multiple has either the empty component, annihilated by \(\lambda\), a single-fresh component whose retained degree is at most four, annihilated by \(\mu_{a,j}\), or a zero component. Every parent companion is annihilated by (FA-system). There are no nonconstant companion multipliers at degree five, hence no omitted cross-parent equations. Finally \(\Lambda(1)=\lambda(1)=1\), and all retained moments through five are preserved. This proves the joint NS extension.

Why the retained cutoff cannot simply be five

Over \(\mathbb F_3\), take four Boolean retained variables \(x,y,u,v\), put \(G=xy+uv\), and let \(\Gamma\) consist of Booleanity and the original degree-four axiom \(G^2\). Then \(G\notin\mathcal I_5(\Gamma)\). Indeed, a degree-five certificate would give \(G=AG^2\) on the Boolean cube for an affine polynomial \(A\). Setting \(x=y=1\) and varying \((u,v)\) through \((0,0),(1,0),(0,1)\) forces the \(u,v\) coefficients of \(A\) to zero. Setting \(u=v=1\) and varying \((x,y)\) through the same three pairs forces the other two coefficients to zero and the constant to one. At all four bits equal to one, however, \(G=2\), \(G^2=1\), so \(A=1\) is impossible.

The exact finite dual witness supplies point weights giving \(\lambda(1)=\lambda(G)=1\), while annihilating \(G^2\) and its four variable multiples. Since Boolean equations vanish pointwise, this is an old degree-five NS functional. Adding a single quadratic-input parent with companion \(G-rG^2\) derives \(G\) through five, by adding \(rG^2\). That old functional cannot extend. Conversely \(G\in\mathcal I_6(\Gamma)\) by the Frobenius argument above. Thus the extra retained degree in the ternary theorem is substantive. This is a counterexample to preserving every old degree-five functional, not to existence of some augmented functional over this satisfiable example.

Connection to the actual source and remaining limits

Every proper rank-one bottom product \(P=1-rL\) in the retained representation has actual degree two. After constant/zero cleanup, parents listing these products fit the theorem. An appropriate retained affine-layer annihilator through \(2p\) can therefore be extended jointly to degree-five NS, without paying for every bottom type. This is stronger at that cutoff than separately choosing one-parent slices.

The proof does not say that \(\Lambda\) annihilates the augmented PC closure. An NS annihilator need not survive arbitrary low-degree PC reuse. It also does not reach the compiler ceiling \(\max(4p-1,p+(p-1)w_*)\), which is larger than five. At degree six, old-variable and fresh-coefficient multiples of companions become active; moments involving two parents can no longer all be assigned zero independently. The next required test is whether the dependence-annihilation argument controls those coupled equations and PC reuse, or produces a specific residual obstruction.

Checks and contribution. The general proof treats every legal degree-five original axiom multiple and all fresh field reductions. The finite control solves a 7-by-16 system over \(\mathbb F_3\) and checks all 243 affine cofactors, with full point weights retained. It does not verify the asymptotic PHP endpoint. This is a new working initial-window extension, not a claim that the full reader problem is solved.

Process assessment. Extracting the actual first-active-degree dependence kernel yielded a consistency proof and a falsifying cutoff control, rather than another abstract restatement of linear extension.

21 September 2026 — Prime-field moment coordinates and the genuinely coupled degree-six system

Status: working all-prime rectangular pairing lemma and exact coupled NS equations. Joint degree-six feasibility and augmented PC exclusion remain open. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Test. Determine what changes at degree six for the actual quadratic-input parents, and whether the characteristic-two moment-coordinate construction can be used legitimately over odd primes. The required endpoint is a consistent coupled extension with an affordable retained degree, not merely coordinates for an unsolved system.

Why the previous zero mixed components no longer work

For a proper rank-one bottom, \(P=1-rL\) and \(E=LP\) satisfy \(P^2-P=-rE\), through degree four. This cheap idempotence is available in the actual retained source. Nevertheless, consider two upper singleton parents on this same \(P\), with fresh coefficients \(s,t\). Their companions are \(P-sP^2\) and \(P-tP^2\), each of original degree five. If the retained moment of \(P\) is nonzero, the first-window equations and idempotence force \(\Lambda(sP)=\Lambda(tP)=\Lambda(P)\). At degree six the legal multiple \(t(P-sP^2)\) then forces \(\Lambda(stP^2)=\Lambda(P)\ne0\). Thus assigning all two-parent components zero cannot extend the previous construction unchanged.

This is a control on the prescription, not an obstruction to the source: setting \(s=t=1\) maps both companions to the old consequence \(P-P^2\). Identical singleton parents are easy and should be cleaned, not treated as a hard instance. The example only demonstrates that the mixed equations are real.

A retained pairing over every prime

Let \(\Gamma\) be a retained system over \(\mathbb F_p\), with field equations for all variables or certificates for them through degree \(p\). Write \(C_B=\mathcal C_B(\Gamma)\), assume \(1\notin C_B\), and let \(t\ge1\), \(pt\le B\). For \(u\le B\) put \(Q_u=\mathcal P_{\le u}/(C_B\cap\mathcal P_{\le u})\), a vector quotient, not an asserted algebra.

Working lemma. Over some finite extension \(K/\mathbb F_p\), one normalized \(\lambda\in\operatorname{Ann}(C_B)\) makes the pairing

\[Q_t\times Q_{(p-1)t}\longrightarrow K,\qquad ([f],[g])\longmapsto\lambda(fg)\]

nondegenerate in the first argument. Consequently every \(K\)-linear functional on \(Q_t\otimes K\) has a density represented by a polynomial of degree at most \((p-1)t\). The density need not be unique. For finitely many requested cutoffs, one \(\lambda\) works simultaneously; a field with more than the sum of their dimensions \(\dim Q_t\) suffices. When \(p=2\), the rectangular pairing becomes the square nonsingular pairing of the earlier binary lemma.

Proof using Frobenius and formal differentiation

The pairing is well defined: a completed PC consequence of degree at most \(t\) can be multiplied by a polynomial of degree at most \((p-1)t\) through the same ceiling \(B\), since their product degree is at most \(pt\). The same argument applies if the second factor is the consequence. This uses actual PC reuse, not closure of a truncated NS space.

Choose an \(\mathbb F_p\)-basis \(e_0=1,e_1,\ldots,e_{d-1}\) of \(Q_t\), with representatives through degree \(t\). Their values may be prescribed freely, subject to \(\lambda(1)=1\), and extended to an annihilator of \(C_B\). Choose such an affine family \(\lambda_z\) with \(\lambda_z(e_0)=1\) and \(\lambda_z(e_i)=z_i\) for \(i>0\). Work temporarily over the rational-function field \(\mathbb F_p(z_1,\ldots,z_{d-1})\).

If the pairing matrix did not have full row rank over this field, there would be a nonzero left-kernel vector, represented by \(a=\sum_i c_i(z)e_i\). Its pairing with every polynomial through \((p-1)t\) would vanish, including with \(a^{p-1}\). Each \(e_i\) is defined over \(\mathbb F_p\), so field reduction gives \(e_i^p-e_i\in C_B\) through degree \(pt\). Therefore

\[0=\lambda_z(a^p)=c_0(z)^p+\sum_{i=1}^{d-1}c_i(z)^p z_i.\]

Differentiate this rational-function identity with respect to \(z_j\). In characteristic \(p\), the derivative of every rational function’s \(p\)-th power is zero. The result is \(c_j(z)^p=0\), hence \(c_j=0\), for every \(j>0\). The original identity then gives \(c_0=0\), contradicting the kernel vector. If \(d=1\), the identity alone gives the contradiction. Thus some maximal minor is a nonzero polynomial of degree at most \(d\), because every matrix entry is affine in the functional parameters.

For several cutoffs, use the full affine space of normalized annihilators. For each cutoff at least one maximal minor is a nonzero polynomial on that space, as shown by the preceding affine subfamily argument. Their product is nonzero and has degree at most the sum of the \(d\)'s. A finite extension field larger than that degree has a specialization where it is nonzero. At that specialization the dual map from \(Q_{(p-1)t}\) onto \(Q_t^*\) is surjective, proving the density assertion. The extension changes functional values only; all source variables still have their original \(\mathbb F_p\) domains.

The degree-six parent equations and their coordinates

Let \(g_{a,i}\) be the actual degree-two inputs of each accuracy-one parent. An ordinary degree-six NS functional has an old component \(\lambda_0\) through degree six, single-fresh components \(\mu_{a,j}\) through five, and symmetric double-fresh components \(\nu_{a,j;b,l}\) through four. The latter includes repeated positions. At odd primes, \(s^2\) is not reduced to \(s\). The full active parent constraints are

\[\lambda_0(u g_{a,i})=\sum_j\mu_{a,j}(u g_{a,i}g_{a,j})\qquad(\deg u\le1),\]
\[\mu_{b,l}(g_{a,i})=\sum_j\nu_{b,l;a,j}(g_{a,i}g_{a,j})\qquad\text{for all }a,i,b,l.\]

Each component must also annihilate the legal retained axiom multiples at its cutoff. These equations exhaust the parent multipliers, since the original companion degree is five. Remaining fresh moments can be specified with consistent reduction by \(s^p=s\); no Boolean reduction is imposed on odd-prime coefficients.

For the stronger ansatz that each component annihilates the corresponding intersection with retained \(C_B\), the new pairing lemma supplies exact coordinates when \(B\ge6p\). Choose the common generic \(\lambda\) and express \(\lambda_0,\mu,\nu\) by density polynomials of degrees at most \(6(p-1),5(p-1),4(p-1)\), respectively. All pairings fit \(B\). The old component is an unknown normalized density, not automatically the constant one. This gives a finite coupled linear system in valid coordinates. It does not prove that the system has a solution.

Outcome and focused review

The initial extension attempt did not settle degree six. It identified a compulsory mixed component even for easy duplicate parents, and removed a characteristic-two restriction from the moment-coordinate tool with explicit degree cost. The general required implication remains joint feasibility plus control of augmented PC reuse at the actual compiler ceiling. Neither idempotence nor the existence of a pairing supplies that implication.

The pairing proof was checked for well-definedness on vector quotients, the normalized constant direction, rational-function coefficients, field reduction through \(pt\), and finite specialization. In particular, the Frobenius step uses \(\lambda_z(e_i^p)=\lambda_z(e_i)\), not the false identity \(\lambda_z(a^p)=\lambda_z(a)^p\) for extension-valued functionals. No numerical or Lean verification was performed.

Next test. Analyze the actual coupled coefficient-row dependencies using these coordinates, retaining the original PC/NS distinction. A solution must handle arbitrary parent arity and sharing; independently solving the displayed first equations is insufficient. Check any proposed cancellation against the duplicate-parent control and the existing mixed-interface counterexamples before extending the degree window.

Process assessment. The direct equation audit prevented reuse of the zero-mixed prescription; the Frobenius differentiation proof supplies the needed prime-field coordinates without silently importing the binary determinant argument.
