21 September 2026 — Generic NS lifting already fails inside the rank-one positive-reader format

Status: working adaptation of the recorded pebbling control, with a degree-nine NS certificate. It is not a PHP counterexample; retained PC degree is only three. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Line review and test. The degree-five lift and degree-six equations concern ordinary NS moments. Before refining this into a generic lift at the actual compiler window, test the claim that high retained NS degree alone controls polynomially many positive-product parents. The recorded pebbling prefix is the relevant obstruction, but it must be converted to our exact rank-one-bottom format before drawing that conclusion.

The complete source and its degree-nine refutation

Take a topologically ordered DAG on \(n\) vertices with indegree at most two and sink \(n\). Put \(b_i=1-x_i\), and use the old pebbling base

\[\mathcal F_G=\{b_i\prod_{j\in\operatorname{pred}(i)}x_j:i\le n\}\cup\{x_n\}\cup\{x_i^2-x_i:i\le n\}.\]

Over any prime field, add one complete rank-one affine bottom for each coordinate \(x_i\), with fresh \(r_i\), product \(P_i=1-r_ix_i\), companion \(F_i=x_iP_i\), and field equation \(r_i^p-r_i\). Denote this retained system by \(\Gamma_0\).

For every prefix \([k]\), add a complete accuracy-one parent on the individual positive products \(P_1,\ldots,P_k\), with fresh coefficients \(s_{k,j}\), product \(Q_k=1-\sum_{j\le k}s_{k,j}P_j\), companions \(P_iQ_k\) and fields \(s_{k,j}^p-s_{k,j}\). There are \(n\) bottoms and \(n\) parents, and \(n(n+1)/2\) parent companions. Bottom inputs have degree one; proper parent inputs have actual degree two, products three and companions five. Sharing is explicit: the same bottom products occur in several parents.

Working consequence. This complete source has an ordinary NS refutation through degree nine. Meanwhile \(\Gamma_0\) has exactly the same minimum NS refutation degree as \(\mathcal F_G\). On the imported high-pebbling family, that retained degree grows as recorded, so polynomial inventory and this exact source grammar do not yield a generic NS-only lifting theorem up to degree nine.

Proof with original-degree charges

Let \(A_k=1-\sum_{j\le k}s_{k,j}b_j\) be the products in the historical accuracy-one prefix certificate. That certificate refutes the old pebbling base augmented by companions \(b_iA_k\) through degree six. Each such original companion has degree three, hence every cofactor attached to it has degree at most three.

Write \(B_i=P_i-b_i\). The exact old/bottom certificate is

\[B_i=F_i+r_i(x_i^2-x_i),\]

through degree three. Also \(A_k-Q_k=\sum_{j\le k}s_{k,j}B_j\). Thus every original prefix companion is obtained from the new source by

\[b_iA_k=P_iQ_k-B_iQ_k+b_i\sum_{j\le k}s_{k,j}B_j.\]

After substituting the displayed certificate for each \(B_j\), the first term is a new parent axiom of degree five. The two pieces of \(B_iQ_k\) have ordinary degree at most six: \(F_i\) costs three and \(Q_k\) costs three, or \(r_i\) costs one, Booleanity two and \(Q_k\) three. The last sum costs at most five. Hence \(b_iA_k\) has an ordinary NS certificate through six. Substituting these certificates in the historical degree-six identity adds at most its degree-three companion cofactor, for a final ceiling nine. Old-generator terms retain their original ceiling. This is explicit NS certificate substitution, not PC final-line reuse.

For retained hardness, specialize all \(r_i=1\). Each bottom companion becomes \(x_i(1-x_i)\), an old Boolean axiom up to sign; its field equation becomes zero. This specialization does not raise degree. Therefore any NS refutation of \(\Gamma_0\) gives an old NS refutation of the same degree. The reverse inequality holds because the old base is contained in \(\Gamma_0\).

The recorded imported pebbling family supplies unbounded, polynomially growing old NS degree. Its source is de Rezende–Meir–Nordström–Robere, arXiv:2001.02481v1, whose abstract confirms the field-independent reversible-pebbling/NS correspondence. The exact graph-family parameterization is reused from the existing import, not newly re-proved here.

Why retained PC closure is essential to the next argument

The old pebbling base already has a PC refutation through degree three, by topological reuse of predecessor lines. So does \(\Gamma_0\). This control does not refute a lift assuming nonrefutable retained PC closure, and it does not refute a PHP-specific theorem. The new pairing explicitly requires \(1\notin C_B\), which fails here.

The control does rule out continuing the degree-five NS theorem into a universal ordinary-moment lift at the full compiler ceiling solely by raising the retained NS cutoff by a fixed polynomial in degree and logarithmic inventory. At fixed output degree nine and polynomial source inventory, the historical family still has much larger retained NS degree. Even two consecutive upper blocks per certificate term suffice, as inherited from the prefix identity; sparse local interaction by itself is not a rescue.

Decision. The next cycle must keep retained PC saturation as a real hypothesis in the coupled degree-six system, and determine which coefficient-row dependencies it kills. If only NS annihilation is used, this explicit source defeats the proposed endpoint. The lower bound still needs either that PC-based consistency construction or additional PHP structure; polishing a generic NS recurrence cannot solve it.

Checks and process. Read the exact historical prefix identity and old-PC proof, verified each replacement term’s ordinary degree, and checked the retained specialization. No numerical or Lean test was needed for these literal identities. The primary-source abstract was checked for field scope; the existing imported graph lower bound remains an imported dependency. This stress test prevented extending the wrong hypothesis to the compiler window.

21 September 2026 — Retained PC hardness alone also fails for the complete reader format

Status: working application of the existing ordering construction to rank-one readers, with constant-degree augmented PC refutations. PHP-specific structure remains essential. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Test. The pebbling reader defeated NS-only hypotheses but had easy retained PC. Test the stronger proposed endpoint on a retained-PC-hard base before further generic consistency work. The relevant older ordering construction already refutes a broad generic transfer; here we check its exact rank-one positive-product representation and constant PC ceiling.

Imported base and already recorded source

Galesi–Lauria, Section 3, Lemma 1 and Theorems 1–2, supplies graph-ordering systems of constant initial degree and old PC degree \(\Omega(n)\) on an infinite bounded-degree expander family. The variables are \(x_{ij}\), \(i<j\), and cyclic-triangle equations enforce a total order. For oriented literals \(t_{ij}+t_{ji}=1\), the no-local-minimum axiom is \(\prod_{j\in N(i)}t_{ij}\). The stated construction has maximum graph degree at most nine. The relevant sections were checked in the author PDF; the degree lower bound is imported, not re-proved here.

The existing balanced minimum construction is stronger than the earlier literature-only audit: it already gives a complete one-level affine ENS source and an ordinary NS refutation. At accuracy one its degree is \(D_{\rm ord}=\max(2H+6,\Delta+2)\), where \(H=\lceil\log_2 n\rceil\), with polynomial inventory. The generic-transfer obstruction is therefore prior work, not a new discovery in this entry.

Exact reader-format application

Every input in that source is a Boolean old literal \(g=1-t_{ij}\). For each distinct needed literal, add a complete rank-one affine bottom on \(L=1-g=t_{ij}\), with product \(P=1-rL\), companion \(F=LP\) and field equation \(r^p-r\). Replace every original accuracy-one block on its literals \(g_j\) by an accuracy-one parent on the individual positive products \(P_j\). Keep all bottom and parent companions and coefficient fields.

Working control. Over every fixed prime, the retained ordering base plus these bottoms still has PC refutation degree \(\Omega(n)\). Adding the positive-product parents gives an ordinary PC refutation through \(\max(8,\Delta+2)\), at most eleven for the imported graph family. It also gives an ordinary NS refutation through \(D_{\rm ord}+3=O(\log n)\). The inventories are polynomial. Thus the exact source grammar and high retained PC degree do not imply augmented PC hardness in general.

Proof and degree accounting

First, the existing balanced construction has a constant PC ceiling before this representation change. Its local comparison and pair identities have ordinary NS proofs through eight at accuracy one, hence PC proofs through eight. At every internal tree node, the recorded partition recursion is

\[Z_A+Z_B-1=R+(\alpha-Z_A)(\beta-1)+(1-Z_B)(\alpha-1).\]

Here \(R\) is the locally proved comparison sum, and each displayed product or sum of block products has actual degree at most two. Once child partition identities have been derived, multiply their completed lines by the displayed degree-two factors through degree four, retaining the earlier ceiling eight. PC reuse therefore eliminates the tree-height accumulation that is present in the ordinary NS proof. At the root, the recorded old-axiom/companion identity costs \(\Delta+2\). This proves the original source’s PC ceiling \(\max(8,\Delta+2)\), without claiming its ordinary NS degree is constant.

For the new representation, \(L\) is a Boolean literal, so \(L^2-L\) is an old Boolean axiom up to its literal expansion. The literal identity

\[P-g=F+r(L^2-L)\]

has an NS certificate through three. For an original block \(A=1-\sum_j s_jg_j\) and its replacement \(Q=1-\sum_j s_jP_j\), use

\[g_iA=P_iQ-(P_i-g_i)Q+g_i\sum_j s_j(P_j-g_j).\]

Exactly as in the preceding pebbling conversion, every original degree-three companion has a new-source NS certificate through six. In PC, derive these original companions once and replay the constant-degree original proof in its unchanged old and parent-coefficient variables. Reusing their final degree-three polynomials preserves \(\max(8,\Delta+2)\). In NS, substituting the certificates in the existing degree-\(D_{\rm ord}\) identity adds at most three to the ceiling. No coefficient field axiom is omitted; the displayed witnesses simply do not need them.

Finally, specialize every bottom coefficient to one. Its product becomes \(1-L=g\), its companion becomes \(L(1-L)\), an old Boolean consequence through two, and its field equation becomes zero. This maps any retained PC refutation to an old ordering PC refutation without increasing degree. Retained PC degree is therefore at least the imported old lower bound; the reverse inequality follows from containment of the old axioms.

What this rules out for our branch

This is an application/refinement of the existing ordering no-go result to the exact rank-one reader source. It rules out proving the full desired reader exclusion from retained PC nonrefutability alone, even with polynomial inventory, individual rank-one bottoms and all complete field/companion equations. The degree-five NS lift and rectangular pairing are not refuted: neither asserts that all fresh-parent constraints are feasible with augmented PC closure at the full source ceiling.

The old base here is ordering, not PHP. A valid lower-bound proof must exploit a stronger PHP-specific property. In particular the full old matching-class preservation theorem supplies structured old-target information, not merely the absence of a refutation. It must be used at its exact complete-board scope and window; the graph counterpart preserves a specified subspace rather than every old target.

Next task. Test whether the matching old-target/filtration information forces the coupled parent residuals to vanish in their required cutoffs. Identify the exact point where this property is used and why the ordering control lacks the needed hypothesis. If raw bottom coefficients survive, their elimination must retain the original degree charge. Further generic pairing or per-parent feasibility lemmas alone would leave the decisive implication untouched.

Checks and process. Read the exact existing balanced source and its local/root identities; checked the PC-reuse version of its recurrence and both source-conversion ledgers. The imported graph degree lower bound and formula encoding were checked against the author PDF; no numerical or formal verification was performed. Targeted search recovered the already completed ordering construction, avoiding a redundant new source construction.

21 September 2026 — Linear row equations remove the initial-width cost on the complete unary board

Status: working PHP-specific full-board compiler with degree 4p-1. It removes a base-scope obstacle but does not exclude the nonlinear selector source. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Required implication tested. The strongest old matching-class preservation theorem is on the complete unary board, whereas the earlier compilation used a sparse graph to make initial clauses short. Before seeking another graph-relative substitute, test whether PHP’s own linear row equations can derive the wide initial clause values cheaply. The generic CNF width bound is sufficient, not a necessity theorem for this special base.

Complete-board transfer

Fix a prime \(p\), write \(\delta=p-1\), and let \(v=N(N+1)\). Every \(S\)-line standard syntactic \(\mathrm{Res}(\mathrm{lin}_{\mathbb F_p})\) refutation of ordinary unary \(\mathrm{PHP}^{N+1}_N\), or of its functional variant, yields a complete accuracy-one selector ENS family over the full functional linear-row PHP base, with at most \(S\) blocks and at most \(\binom{v+p-1}{p-1}\) inputs per block, and an ordinary PC refutation through

\[D=4p-1.\]

Each input is still literally \(1-L^{p-1}\) for a source affine equation \(L=0\). Every coefficient equation is retained. There is no restriction of the old cell board in this transfer and no assumption on intermediate clause width. This is the earlier selector compiler with a specialized proof of its pigeon initials; all inference and inventory arguments are unchanged.

The wide pigeon initial has a constant-degree certificate

For a fixed pigeon row let \(X_1,\ldots,X_N\) be its cells and let \(R=\sum_jX_j-1\) be the old linear row axiom. Encode the positive literal \(X_j\) as \(1-X_j=0\), with selector \(t_j=1-(1-X_j)^{p-1}\). For its compressed clause block write \(P=1-\sum_i r_i t_i\), where the selected selectors span all original row selectors as ordinary polynomials. Thus each \(t_jP\) is a constant combination of the block’s complete companions.

The univariate difference \(t_j-X_j\) vanishes at zero and one. For \(p>2\) it is a multiple of \(X_j^2-X_j\) through degree \(p-1\); for \(p=2\) it is identically zero. The exact identity

\[P=\sum_{j=1}^N t_jP-\sum_{j=1}^N(t_j-X_j)P-RP\tag{FB-row}\]

therefore derives \(P\). Its companion and Boolean-correction terms cost at most \(2p-1\), and the row-axiom term at most \(p+1\le2p-1\). The number of summands depends on \(N\), but the ordinary degree does not.

The linear row axiom is essential: if it is omitted, all \(X_j=0\) makes all selectors and companions zero while \(P=1\), and satisfies Booleanity and the exclusion axioms. This is an explicit falsification control for the proposed initial bridge.

Remaining initials and full replay

Hole clauses and functional row-exclusion clauses have width two; their ordinary clause products are the old quadratic exclusion axioms. Boolean-domain clauses likewise use the old Boolean equation. The earlier initial-product argument supplies each of these values through \(p+2(p-1)=3p-2\). The wide pigeon initials instead use (FB-row). Thus all initials and the earlier syntactic inference replay fit \(4p-1\).

For the weak unary CNF, use the functional algebraic base as an explicit strengthening. This is legitimate for a lower-bound reduction: every initial weak-CNF clause has the displayed value proof over that stronger base, and the functional base is exactly the one whose matching lower-bound properties are available. No onto axioms or extra column sums are added. The final empty clause still has value one.

For fixed \(p\), the inventory remains polynomial in \(S,N\). For odd \(p\), the rank-one retained representation also fits this same ceiling, since \(2p+1\le4p-1\). It is optional: the direct old-selector source already suffices to formulate the remaining exclusion problem on the complete board.

Clarification of the earlier graph-ordering claim

The earlier unary proof correctly restricts before applying a generic width-based CNF bridge. Its warning that compiling the full row first incurs degree \(N\) is a limitation of that generic bridge, not an unconditional necessity. Statements in this branch treating that order as mandatory were too strong; (FB-row) supplies an alternative. The previous graph proof and its result are not invalidated.

As a binary sanity check, the verified affine-DAG controller may use these actual initial-value proofs in place of its generic width estimate. Pigeon values then cost three, binary hole/domain values cost four, and the verified local inference ceiling is five. The existing complete-board affine exclusion consequently recovers the already recorded unrestricted unary parity theorem without a separate proof-graph restriction stage. No new binary lower bound or new Lean verification is claimed.

PHP-specific next obligation

The odd-prime source still has nonlinear inputs. But we can now use the complete-board old-target window without pretending it holds on an arbitrary sparse graph. In characteristic three, the optional rank-one bottoms also have a degree-preserving simultaneous specialization \(r_L=L\): their products become \(1-L^2\), and both companion and coefficient-field images are old Boolean consequences through three. Hence raw bottom variables need not remain in the proposed test of the old-selector source.

The next mathematical task is to use the full old matching/filtration information on the actual coupled selector residuals, distinguishing it from mere PC hardness, which the ordering control disproves as a sufficient hypothesis. The wide initial clause and complete-board scope are no longer the obstacle. Nonlinear source exclusion, augmented PC closure and the fixed-prime quantifiers remain open.

Review and process. Checked (FB-row), univariate Boolean divisibility, original companion degrees, the weak/functional strengthening, all quadratic initials and the unchanged compiler inventory. No numerical or formal verification was performed. The decisive improvement came from using the actual PHP row axiom rather than imposing the generic initial-width interface.

21 September 2026 — Functional PHP has row-or-column affine Boolean values at degree two

Status: working classification with exact degree-two controls. It identifies zero ternary selectors, not all mixed selector dependencies. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Targeted PHP test. Determine which individual ternary selector inputs already vanish in the old low-degree quotient. The existing column-only theorem concerns weak linear-row PHP, without same-row exclusion axioms. Our new full-board compiler uses the functional base, so that theorem cannot be applied unchanged. The following classification accounts for the additional row equations explicitly.

Statement

Let \(N\ge4\), \(m=N+1\), and let \(\mathcal F^{\rm fun}_{m,N}\) consist of Booleanity, same-row and same-column exclusions, and \(\rho_i-1\). Over a field of odd characteristic, an affine polynomial \(b\) satisfies

\[b^2-b\in\mathcal I_2(\mathcal F^{\rm fun}_{m,N})\]

if and only if, modulo the linear span of the row equations, it is a partial-row indicator, a partial-column indicator, or the complement of one:

\[\sum_{j\in J}X_{ij},\qquad \sum_{i\in A}X_{ij},\qquad 1-\sum_{j\in J}X_{ij},\qquad 1-\sum_{i\in A}X_{ij}.\]

Empty subsets include constants. A row indicator’s complement is also a row indicator modulo its row equation. Membership and sufficiency here are at the original degree-two NS ceiling, not merely semantic Booleanity on a satisfying assignment.

Projected degree-two relations

Eliminate the last cell in every row by \(X_{iN}=1-S_i\), where \(S_i=\sum_{j<N}Y_{ij}\). This is an affine substitution. Every degree-two old certificate projects to a constant combination of:

  • \(Y_{ij}^2-Y_{ij}\);
  • same-row products \(Y_{ij}Y_{i\ell}\), \(j\ne\ell\), and same-column products \(Y_{ij}Y_{kj}\), \(i\ne k\);
  • \(F_{ik}=(1-S_i)(1-S_k)\), \(i\ne k\), from last-column exclusions.

All projected Boolean and same-row relations involving a last cell are already in the first two spans: expand \(S_i^2-S_i\) and \(Y_{ij}(1-S_i)\). Row multiples project to zero. Conversely these displayed relations have degree-two old certificates after the substitution is lifted. In particular, their span contains no nonzero affine polynomial. For each pair of rows, a cross monomial \(Y_{ij}Y_{k\ell}\) with \(j\ne\ell\) forces the coefficient of \(F_{ik}\) to zero in an affine target; then diagonal and collision monomials force the remaining coefficients to zero. Thus the affine part of the original \(\mathcal I_2\) is exactly the row-equation span.

The quadratic coefficient restriction

Write the projected affine form as \(b=c+\sum_{i,j<N}a_{ij}Y_{ij}\). For distinct rows \(i,k\), comparing cross monomials in \(b^2-b\) gives

\[a_{ij}a_{k\ell}=d_{ik}\quad\text{for every }j\ne\ell,\tag{FB-cross}\]

where division by two is valid. Indeed only \(F_{ik}\) has a nonzero coefficient on these cross-row, cross-column monomials, and that coefficient is constant across the pair of rows.

There are at least three projected columns. If \(d_{ik}\ne0\), all entries in the two row vectors are nonzero; comparing two entries against a third column makes each row vector constant. Any other nonzero row vector compared with one of these constant vectors must also be constant. In this case \(b=c+\sum_i a_iS_i\), which is a form supported on the last column modulo row equations.

If all \(d_{ik}=0\), then any two nonzero row vectors are each supported on the same single column: otherwise a nonzero entry in one and a nonzero entry in a different column in the other violate (FB-cross). With at least two nonzero rows this yields a column-supported form. With at most one nonzero row, it yields a row-supported form. The zero case is constant. This exhausts the quadratic restriction without imposing the weak-system’s same-row coefficient equations.

Reduce the remaining affine coefficients

For a row-supported form, write its values on the row’s one-hot states as \(\beta_j\). Using the row, Boolean and same-row axioms, its Booleanity polynomial reduces through degree two to \(\sum_j(\beta_j^2-\beta_j)X_{ij}\). This affine polynomial lies in the row span only if every coefficient is zero: its constant term is zero and other rows have no variables. Therefore every \(\beta_j\) is zero or one, giving a partial-row indicator.

For a column-supported form \(c+\sum_i a_iX_{ij}\), Boolean and column exclusions reduce its Booleanity polynomial to

\[c(c-1)+\sum_i a_i(a_i+2c-1)X_{ij}.\]

A column-supported affine polynomial lies in the row span only if it is zero, by inspecting other columns. Thus \(c\in\{0,1\}\), and the nonzero \(a_i\)'s are one when \(c=0\), or minus one when \(c=1\). These are the stated column indicators and complements.

Conversely an indicator \(S\) on a row or column satisfies \(S^2-S=\sum (X^2-X)+2\sum_{e<f}X_eX_f\), using the corresponding exclusions through two. Complements have the same Booleanity polynomial. A row-span change \(b-S\) changes Booleanity by \((b-S)(b+S-1)\), again through two. This completes necessity and sufficiency.

Consequence for actual ternary selectors

Over \(\mathbb F_3\), put \(b=(L+1)/2\). Then \(b^2-b=(L^2-1)/4\). Consequently \(1-L^2\in I_2\) precisely when \(L\) is, modulo rows, \(2S-1\) for an indicator or complement of the stated row/column kind. The old matching filtration theorem makes this the same zero test in \(C_B\) throughout its stable range \(N\ge2B-1\), \(2\le B\le m\). This gives an exact PHP-specific cleanup test for an individual selector that vanishes at a larger stable cutoff.

It does not classify arbitrary linear combinations of selectors, nor the products and cross-parent dependencies in the degree-six equations. The coupled source exclusion remains open. The weak-system column-only theorem is not contradicted: adding same-row exclusions genuinely enlarges the degree-two ideal.

Exact tests and limits

The independent checker builds the full ordinary degree-two NS span on the five-row, four-hole functional board, using all row multiples, Booleanity and both exclusions. It compares membership with the finite set of all row/column indicators modulo row gauges. Over \(\mathbb F_3\) it checks all affine forms in a canonical two-row slice and all four column slices; over \(\mathbb F_5\) it checks a canonical one-row slice. Both fields add 500 deterministic full-board samples and explicit positive/negative controls.

All 6,732 tests agree. Each NS matrix has 231 monomial columns and rank 165. The same-row sum \(X_{11}+X_{12}\) is accepted, unlike the earlier weak-system control; \(X_{11}+X_{22}\) is rejected. Results and the compiled checker are preserved. These bounded slices do not constitute exhaustive board-wide enumeration or replace the proof.

Contribution and process. The cycle identifies precisely which single ternary selector inputs disappear by old functional PHP equations, using a property absent from a generic retained-hardness assumption. It does not discharge mixed-parent consistency. Checking the existing theorem’s weak-base scope prevented incorrectly discarding the new row case.

Timing limitation. Initial reading, derivation and checker drafting preceded the corrected clock because a completed session label was reused; refused commands performed no computation. An invalid phase marker was then corrected before the successful checks.

21 September 2026 — The affine selector interface has an exact row-or-column boundary in characteristic three

Status: working sharp affinization criterion and restricted-query lower-bound corollary; unrestricted mixed queries remain the goal. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

General hypothesis tested. Could every ternary equation selector become affine after using functional PHP equations, allowing direct application of the existing affine endpoint? The previous Booleanity proof gives a sharp answer, not merely another positive example.

Exactly which selectors have an affine representative

On the full functional \((N+1)\)-by-\(N\) board, \(N\ge4\), over \(\mathbb F_3\), an affine \(L\) has \(1-L^2-A\in I_2\) for some affine \(A\) if and only if \(L\), modulo the linear row equations, is supported in a single row or a single column (constants included).

Necessity. Repeat only the quadratic-coefficient part of the functional classification. After eliminating each last-row cell, write \(L=c+\sum a_{ij}Y_{ij}\). Subtracting an arbitrary affine \(A\) changes none of the quadratic coefficients. Membership therefore requires \(a_{ij}a_{k\ell}\) to be constant over \(j\ne\ell\), for each distinct row pair. With at least three projected columns, a nonzero constant forces both row vectors to be constant and then forces every other nonzero row vector to be constant, giving support in the eliminated column modulo rows. If every such constant is zero, two nonzero rows must be supported in the same single column; if only one row is nonzero its support is unrestricted. These are precisely column- and row-local forms. No Boolean-value restriction on the remaining coefficients is imposed because \(A\) may be any affine form.

Sufficiency and witnesses. For a row-local form \(L=c+\sum_j a_jX_{ij}\), let

\[A=\sum_j\bigl[1-(c+a_j)^2\bigr]X_{ij}.\]

Expanding the square and reducing by the row equation, Booleanity and same-row exclusions gives \(1-L^2-A\in I_2\). For a column-local form \(L=c+\sum_i a_iX_{ij}\), use

\[A=1-c^2+\sum_i\bigl[c^2-(c+a_i)^2\bigr]X_{ij}.\]

Booleanity and same-column exclusions give the degree-two certificate. A row-gauge change \(L-L_0\) changes the square by \((L-L_0)(L+L_0)\), also through two. This proves the equivalence with original-degree witnesses.

The old filtration theorem makes the criterion unchanged if membership is instead requested in an old \(C_B\) within its stable range: the difference still has degree at most two. Thus a genuinely mixed form does not acquire an affine representative just by allowing a larger stable old proof degree. For example \(L=X_{11}+X_{22}\) is not row- or column-local modulo rows, so its surviving cross-matching term cannot be removed this way.

What the affine endpoint consequently covers

Working restricted-query corollary. For every fixed \(K>0\), sufficiently large ordinary unary PHP, and its functional variant, has no \(\mathbb F_3\) standard syntactic Res(Lin) refutation of at most \(N^K\) lines in which every equation form is row- or column-local modulo row equations. Clauses may mix these forms, have arbitrary width, and share subproofs without a depth restriction. This is a restricted class of the original target, not a replacement for it.

Source reduction. Use the complete-board compiler at degree eleven. Replace every selector input \(q_i=1-L_i^2\) by its certified affine representative \(A_i\). For a block write \(P_q=1-\sum_i r_iq_i\), \(P_A=1-\sum_i r_iA_i\). Then \(P_q-P_A\) has an old NS certificate through three. The identity

\[q_iP_q=A_iP_A+(q_i-A_i)P_A+q_i(P_q-P_A)\]

derives every original companion from the new complete affine block through degree five: the three terms cost at most three, four and five respectively. Constant inputs remain unchanged; zero inputs can be omitted. All coefficient field equations remain the same. Derive these source axioms once and replay the original PC refutation, preserving its degree-eleven ceiling. The number of blocks and inputs remains polynomial.

The existing complete-board affine exclusion at \(p=3\) contradicts this refutation. That working theorem’s review limitations are inherited. This proves the corollary without a graph-before-compilation requirement.

Checks, general gap and next action

The independent checker tests \(L^2-L\in I_2+\mathcal P_{\le1}\), equivalent to selector affinization, against all row/column-local affine classes modulo rows. Its ordinary NS-plus-affine matrix has rank 181 in 231 columns on the five-row, four-hole ternary board. All 5,605 bounded two-row/column-slice and deterministic sample tests agree, with 3,124 positive decisions. The report and checker are retained. The reused checker’s renamed main function initially warned about its implicit return; adding an explicit return removed that warning, without changing the numerical algorithm or repeating the accepted tests.

The unrestricted goal still allows forms spanning multiple rows and columns. The result shows that wholesale conversion to affine inputs is false even modulo all stable old PHP consequences. Collective combinations of selectors can nevertheless have a much larger affine part than the individual inputs suggest. The next test is a degree-certified reduction of the whole input span modulo old relations, retaining the remaining nonlinear rank and its cost. Do not infer a bound on that rank from this individual-input classification.

Process and timing. Reusing the earlier coefficient proof isolated the exact boundary and avoided a new case enumeration. The initial proof sketch preceded this cycle’s clock; subsequent checking and recording are instrumented.

21 September 2026 — Certified PHP quotient rank gives a sublinear nonlinear-profile endpoint

Status: working complete-source exclusion under a sublinear quotient-rank hypothesis. A quadratic-rank two-column control shows this condition is sufficient, not necessary. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Obligation tested. Individual selector classification does not control combinations. Test whether the whole input span can be reduced modulo actual old degree-two PHP certificates, then feed its remaining nonlinear rank into the existing complete profile map. The general goal still requires handling the large-rank remainder; no rank bound on arbitrary proof lines is assumed.

Statement on the complete functional board

Work over \(\mathbb F_3\), on full functional \(\mathrm{PHP}^{N+1}_N\). In \(Q_2=\mathcal P_{\le2}/I_2\), let \(V_a\) be the span of the actual selectors \(q_{a,i}=1-L_{a,i}^2\) in block \(a\), and let \(A_a=V_a\cap\overline{\mathcal P_{\le1}}\). Define the certified nonlinear quotient rank

\[r_a=\dim(V_a/A_a).\]

The quotient is by the degree-two NS consequence space, not the unrestricted ideal of the unsatisfiable base. Fix polynomial block inventory, a polylogarithmic proof-degree bound, and \(0\le\sigma<1\). If every \(r_a\le N^\sigma\operatorname{polylog}N\), with fixed logarithmic exponent, then for sufficiently large \(N\) the complete accuracy-one selector source has no such ordinary PC refutation. All original companions and coefficient fields are included. This is a working application of the existing fixed-graph profile theorem after the following explicit complete-board source conversion.

Degree-certified basis conversion

Choose affine representatives \(F_1,\ldots,F_b\) for a basis of \(A_a\), then choose \(r_a\) original selector positions whose classes extend it to a basis of \(V_a\). Call the resulting list \(f_j\); every \(f_j\) is affine or an original selector. There are constant matrices giving

\[q_i=\sum_j\alpha_{ij}f_j+u_i,\qquad f_j=\sum_i\beta_{ji}q_i+v_j,\qquad u_i,v_j\in I_2.\tag{QR-basis}\]

These memberships supply original degree-two certificates by definition of the finite quotient. The second relation follows because each basis class lies in the original input span. Scalar blocks can first be cleaned; nonconstant original selectors have actual degree two. Ordinary polynomial basis compression bounds the input inventory polynomially even if an original line listed many redundant equations.

For a new complete block on \(f_j\) with coefficients \(t_j\), let \(P_f=1-\sum_jt_jf_j\). Map original coefficients by \(r_i=\sum_j\beta_{ji}t_j\). The original product becomes

\[\phi(P_q)=P_f+E,\qquad E=\sum_j t_jv_j\in I_3.\]

Here the degree-three membership means a certificate from the old axioms in the ring including the fresh \(t_j\); it is not an old-only polynomial identity. For each original companion,

\[q_i\phi(P_q)=\sum_j\alpha_{ij}f_jP_f+u_iP_f+q_iE.\]

Every term has a new-source certificate through five: \(\deg P_f\le3\), the old residuals cost two, and \(q_i\) has degree two. This is exactly the original companion ceiling. The coefficient-field image is a constant combination of the new \(t_j^3-t_j\), through three. All retained variables are fixed, and the variable substitution is linear. Thus source refutations transfer without degree increase, for both NS certificates and PC replay. The construction is simultaneous across the family; no per-block losses are added. It is the degree-certified input-basis principle, with both directions of (QR-basis) made explicit rather than treating old relations as free equalities.

Apply the existing complete profile map

Replace only the selected nonlinear basis positions by the rank-one affine-bottom representation. The resulting parents list old affine forms and individual whole products. For a mixed parent, the product comparison still has a certificate through five. Original affine-input companions are derived through six, and original quadratic-input companions through seven. Hence the PC ceiling becomes at most \(\max(D,7)\), a harmless constant enlargement; for the actual degree-eleven compiler it does not grow.

Choose a robust graph from the recorded profile endpoint, restrict this already converted complete-board proof, and apply its global row projection. All bottoms remain rank at most one. Every parent has at most \(r_a\) nonlinear basis positions plus affine inputs, so its nonlinear function-space quotient on the full remaining Boolean cube has dimension at most \(r_a\). Restrictions or row projection cannot increase that position count. This is not an assertion that old PHP quotient identities hold for free on a different graph: they were already used, with certificates, before restricting the proof.

The existing theorem therefore applies with \(\rho=0\) and the stated \(\sigma<1\). Its complete coefficient map has

\[T=O\!\left(N^{1/2}\operatorname{polylog}N+N^\sigma\operatorname{polylog}N\right),\qquad TD+k=o(N),\]

within the fixed graph’s linear separation range. It preserves all source fields, original companions, sharing and raw coefficient uses. This proves the source exclusion. No new field extension or unproved PC-to-NS equality is used.

Applying the complete-board compiler gives a necessary condition on a hypothetical polynomial-line ternary refutation: for every fixed \(\sigma<1\), sufficiently large instances must have some clause whose selector span has nonlinear quotient rank greater than \(N^\sigma\). This does not prove that no such high-rank clause exists or that its presence makes a proof long.

Large rank alone is not hardness: an exact two-column control

Let \(m=N+1\), and for every \(i\ne k\) take

\[g_{ik}=1-(1+X_{i1}+X_{k2})^2.\]

Modulo Booleanity through two, \(g_{ik}\equiv X_{i1}X_{k2}\). These \(m(m-1)\) classes are linearly independent even modulo \(I_2+\mathcal P_{\le1}\). To see this, eliminate a column other than the first two. For each row pair, the projected cross-coefficient matrix of a putative relation is supported only at the first/second-column pair and its reverse. The old degree-two relations require this off-diagonal matrix to be constant; a third projected column forces that constant to zero. Thus both coefficients are zero for every row pair. The tuple’s nonlinear rank is \(m(m-1)=\Theta(N^2)\).

Yet a complete block on this tuple has a cheap old-only map: set its coefficient \(r_{ik}=X_{i1}X_{k2}\), of degree two. In the Boolean and row/column collision quotient, at most one of these matching indicators is nonzero. With \(C_j=\sum_iX_{ij}\), the product image is \(1-C_1C_2\), and every companion image is zero. Boolean/collision monomial reduction gives certificates through six for the actual companion images; coefficient fields also cost six. Thus any degree-\(D\) PC source proof under this map replays over the old base through \(2D\). This is an instance of the already available low-column-profile mechanism, not a new hard family.

The exact control on a five-row, four-hole board confirms nonlinear rank twenty for twenty selectors, and checks the map on all 31 valid states of the two columns. The wrong all-zero coefficient map fails on twenty of those states. Complete check results and code are preserved. These checks do not certify the asymptotic profile theorem; its exact source and degree hypotheses were read separately.

Route assessment and next task

The low nonlinear-rank case is now linked to the actual PHP quotient by an original-degree source reduction. The general missing implication is a structural alternative for the remaining tuples: high quotient rank may still have cheap column or other profiles, as the control shows, but arbitrary high-rank mixed forms are not covered. A rank count by itself is neither an obstruction nor a hardness measure.

The next test should distinguish high-rank tuples with cheap state profiles from those requiring a genuinely new image or consistency argument. Require a uniform criterion that preserves the actual input span and proof-degree budget, and test it on the two-column control. Do not replace the unrestricted goal with the proved low-rank sufficient condition.

Process assessment. The existing complete profile theorem supplied the asymptotic endpoint; the new work was the certified quotient conversion and its high-rank easy control. Starting the clock from the previous finisher avoided an instrumentation gap.

21 September 2026 — Boolean-reduced polar rank does not support the binary head-extraction step at odd primes

Status: working extraction obstruction with exact finite controls; it does not refute selector-source exclusion or a domain-aware alternative. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

High-case test. The binary collective-rank lemma changes coordinates within the Boolean cube and extracts disjoint quadratic leading heads. Test whether its rank hypothesis alone can justify the corresponding move over odd primes, before importing the later binary dichotomy. The distinction between an ordinary quadratic representative and its Boolean reduction matters here.

A family of arbitrarily high reduced polar rank with few disjoint heads

Fix an odd prime \(p\), an integer \(a\ge p\), and disjoint groups of \(m\) Boolean variables, with total dimension \(v=am\). Let \(L\) be the sum of all variables and \(L_i\) the sum in group \(i\). Define \(f_i\) to be the multilinear Boolean representative of \(LL_i\), and let \(W=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb F_p}\{f_1,\ldots,f_a\}\).

Every nonzero combination has polar rank at least \(m-2\), calculated from its canonical multilinear representative. Yet under every invertible affine change that maps the Boolean cube onto itself, and every degree-compatible monomial order, at most \(p-1\) members of \(W\) can have pairwise disjoint quadratic leading monomials. Taking \(m\ge4a^2+2\) therefore meets the numerical binary rank threshold while preventing the asserted \(a\) heads. The binary lemma itself is unchanged; its characteristic-two domain hypothesis is essential.

Rank proof

For a nonzero coefficient vector \(c\), let \(b\in\mathbb F_p^v\) be constant \(c_i\) on group \(i\), and let \(\mathbf1\) be the all-one vector. The ordinary product \(L\sum_i c_iL_i\) has polar matrix \(\mathbf1b^T+b\mathbf1^T\), of rank at most two. Replacing every \(x_j^2\) by \(x_j\) removes the diagonal contribution and gives the canonical polar matrix

\[B_c=\mathbf1b^T+b\mathbf1^T-2\operatorname{diag}(b).\]

The diagonal matrix has rank at least \(m\), since one group has nonzero coefficient and two is invertible. Rank subadditivity yields \(\operatorname{rank}B_c\ge m-2\). In particular the \(f_i\) are independent once \(m>2\). This is a property of Boolean-reduced representatives, not of the ordinary rank-two product.

The leading-head obstruction

Consider the graded square-zero algebra \(S=\mathbb F_p[x_1,\ldots,x_v]/(x_j^2)_j\), used only for the highest-degree parts of Boolean polynomials. The degree-two part of every member of \(W\) is the image of \(LM\) for some linear \(M\). In this algebra \(L^p=\sum_jx_j^p=0\). Consequently the product of the degree-two parts of any \(p\) members of \(W\) is zero.

If those \(p\) members had disjoint squarefree quadratic leading monomials, their product would have a nonzero squarefree leading monomial of degree \(2p\). A degree-compatible monomial order makes its coefficient the product of the nonzero leading coefficients; lower terms cannot cancel it. That term survives the square-zero quotient, a contradiction.

The argument survives all permissible affine cube automorphisms. Indeed, an affine function mapping every Boolean point into \(\{0,1\}\) has at most one nonzero variable coefficient in odd characteristic: its Booleanity polynomial has cross coefficient \(2a_i a_j\), which must vanish. An invertible affine cube automorphism is therefore a coordinate permutation with optional complements. Its highest-degree action is a signed permutation, preserving the square-zero algebra and the common-factor argument. Arbitrary invertible \(\mathbb F_p\)-linear changes need not preserve the Boolean cube and are not licensed to introduce new Boolean equations.

The control is easy by a different profile, and relates to the actual selectors

On Boolean points, \(f_i=LL_i\). Conditioning on the one field-valued affine probe \(L=\alpha\) makes every input equal to the affine function \(\alpha L_i\). Thus high reduced polar rank here coexists with a cheap common profile. Over \(\mathbb F_3\), an explicit complete-block reduction to affine inputs uses original coefficient images \(r_i=L t_i\), where \(t_i\) are the fresh coefficients of a target block on the \(L_i\). With \(H=\sum_i t_iL_i\), the source product is congruent to \(1-L^2H\), and its companions are congruent to \(LL_i(1-H)\), using \(L^3-L\) and the Boolean reductions. Their certificates cost at most six. The coefficient-field image is

\[(Lt_i)^3-Lt_i=L^3(t_i^3-t_i)+t_i(L^3-L),\]

also through six. The variable map has degree two, so the complete source maps to the affine source within a factor-two PC degree budget. This is a positive control for cheap profile handling, not a hard-source example.

At \(p=3\), these functions occur as actual selector combinations:

\[(1-(L-L_i)^2)-(1-(L+L_i)^2)=4LL_i=LL_i.\]

Thus the span of the \(2a\) legal equation selectors contains the displayed high-rank subspace after Boolean reduction. The obstruction concerns extraction from that subspace using only its reduced polar-rank data. It does not say that the larger selector span has no other useful directions, or that PHP-specific old relations cannot provide another representation.

Exact controls and route verdict

For \(p=3,a=3,m=38\), the checker evaluates all 26 nonzero combinations of the three polar matrices of order 114. The minimum reduced rank is 39, above the required 36; each ordinary product’s polar rank is at most two. Separate six-variable square-zero multiplication confirms the threefold product is zero in characteristic three and nonzero in characteristic five. All ranks and field controls are preserved. The all-parameter and coordinate-change statements are proved above, not inferred from these finite checks.

The proposed direct reuse of binary high-polar extraction is therefore invalid in this scope. A corrected alternative must recognize ordinary common-factor/probe representations before treating reduced polar rank as a high case, and must retain the actual Boolean-domain equations under any other coordinate change. The proved low PHP-quotient-rank endpoint remains valid; this control neither closes nor refutes the remaining high-rank selector exclusion.

Next task. Return to the actual ordinary affine-form/selector span and its certified old relations, rather than the polar matrices of one canonical Boolean representative. Test a source-compatible profile/high-case alternative against both this one-probe family and the two-column high-quotient-rank control. An extraction theorem that changes the domain or ignores those easy profiles cannot supply the required lower bound.

Process assessment. Reading the precise binary coordinate step and checking its field dependence produced a decisive countercontrol before an unsupported odd-prime theorem was used.

21 September 2026 — Counting all distinct quadratic heads supplies a genuine dense-rank case

Status: working low-or-dense graph-rank exclusion in characteristic three. It leaves an intermediate projected-rank window and does not prove unrestricted PHP hardness. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

High-case test. The previous obstruction concerned extracting many disjoint heads by a binary coordinate change. Test a different implication: a sufficiently large quadratic quotient rank already gives many distinct heads in the original coordinates, and their full edge graph may shrink the reduction image. No coordinate change or false Boolean equation is introduced.

An elementary filtered image bound

Let a graph on \(s\ge2\) vertices have \(q\le s(s-1)/4\) distinct edges, and let \(J_{\le k}\) count its independent sets of size at most \(k\), where \(1\le k\le s/4\). Put \(a=\sqrt{s(s-1)-2q}\). Then

\[J_{\le k}\le4\frac{a^k}{k!}\le4\binom sk\exp\left(\frac{k^2}{s}-\frac{qk}{s^2}\right).\tag{DH-count}\]

Proof. An ordered independent \(j\)-tuple is an ordered clique in the complement. If \(j\) is even, pair consecutive positions; there are at most \(a^j\) choices of the resulting ordered complement edges. If \(j\) is odd, the last vertex gives at most \(s a^{j-1}\) choices. Ignoring distinctness only enlarges these counts. Since \(a\ge s/2\), both give at most \(2a^j/j!\) independent \(j\)-sets. Consecutive terms backwards from \(k\) have ratio at most \(k/a\le1/2\), proving the first inequality.

For the second, use \(\log(a/s)\le-1/(2s)-q/s^2\), and

\[-\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\log(1-j/s)\le\frac{k(k-1)}{2(s-k)}\le\frac{k^2}{s}.\]

Dividing \(a^k/k!\) by \(\binom sk\) and dropping the favorable term \(-k/(2s)\) proves (DH-count). This argument is purely combinatorial and does not require an imported clique theorem.

Reduction meaning. If \(q\) degree-two polynomials have distinct squarefree quadratic leading monomials, use their pairs as graph edges. Monomial-by-monomial reduction by these polynomials and old Booleanity, in a fixed degree-compatible order, has image dimension at most \(J_{\le k}\). Disjointness is unnecessary. Lower terms can overlap arbitrarily. When each divisor is a constant combination of actual inputs plus an old degree-two certificate, a kernel polynomial has

\[f=\sum_i c_i g_i+E,\qquad \deg c_i\le k-2,\qquad E\in I_k(\Gamma).\tag{DH-membership}\]

Every reduction decreases the monomial order and preserves the degree ceiling. This is a linear normal-form map, not a Gröbner-basis claim.

Finite source criterion on a separated graph

Work over \(\mathbb F_3\). Let \(H\) and \(W_k\) be a graph and matching space supplied by the sixteen-neighbor separation lemma, with \(s=15(N+1)\) nondistinguished coordinates, at sufficiently large \(N\) and \(k=O(\sqrt{N\log N})\). Project row coordinates as in the existing graph construction. Projected old degree-two generators have old graph certificates through two, so a proof of a polynomial in these coordinates lifts at the same ceiling at least two.

Consider \(M\ge1\) complete accuracy-one old-quadratic blocks, of input degree at most two, with all coefficient fields, and a requested PC degree \(D\ge5\). For each tuple let \(r_a\) be its nonlinear rank in \(P_{\le2}/I_2(\Gamma_H)\) modulo the affine image. Set

\[\Lambda=\log(64(M+1)),\quad k=\left\lceil\sqrt{N\Lambda}\right\rceil,\]
\[H_* =\left\lceil k+\frac{s}{k}\left(\Lambda+\frac{5k^2}{N}\right)\right\rceil,\quad Q_* =\left\lceil\frac{s^2}{k}\left(\frac{k^2}{s}+\frac{5k^2}{N}+\Lambda\right)\right\rceil.\]

Assume \(2\le k\le s/4\), \(Q_*\le s(s-1)/4\), and every block has either \(r_a\le R\) or \(r_a\ge Q_*\). Put \(T=2H_*+4R+2\) and require \(TD+k\le N/10^6\). Then the source has no ordinary PC refutation through \(D\).

For polynomial \(M\), polylogarithmic \(D\), and \(R\le N^\sigma\operatorname{polylog}N\), \(\sigma<1\), these conditions hold eventually apart from the explicit low-or-dense rank hypothesis. Here \(H_*=O(\sqrt{N\log N})\), \(Q_*=O(N^{3/2}\sqrt{\log N})\), and \(TD+k=o(N)\). The ranks are measured after the specified graph restriction and row projection, not silently identified with full-board ranks.

Choose one weight for dense heads and large affine cores

For a dense block, reduce only quadratic coefficient columns modulo the quadratic parts of old \(I_2\), retaining all affine tails. Row reduction of the resulting input span supplies \(Q_*\) constant combinations with distinct quadratic leading monomials. Booleanity removes square pivots, so these are squarefree pairs. Their representatives differ from actual input combinations only by old degree-two certificates. We have not subtracted arbitrary affine equations from the source.

Apply (DH-count). Its image dimension is at most

\[4\binom sk e^{-5k^2/N-\Lambda}\le\frac{\tfrac12e^{-5k^2/N}\binom sk}{8(M+1)}.\]

For low-rank blocks, use the two-sided certified basis conversion: affine basis inputs plus at most \(R\) quadratic representatives, with every source-axiom image proved through five. Since \(D\ge5\), PC replay stays within \(D\), including mixed-degree inputs. No degree-preserving NS flattening for every mixed-degree replacement is inferred.

For each proper affine core of rank at least \(H_*\), ordinary restriction to its common-zero flat has image dimension at most \(\binom sk\exp[-k(H_*-k)/s]\), hence at most the same graph-space lower bound divided by \(32(M+1)\). Unit cores need no restriction; empty or smaller cores use the exact prefix below. The sum of all dense and affine image dimensions is strictly smaller than \(\dim W_k\). Therefore a nonzero homogeneous \(f\in W_k\) lies in every required kernel simultaneously.

Complete maps and original-degree certificates

For a dense block, (DH-membership) gives \(f=\sum_i c_i g_i+E\). Set its coefficient images to \(\beta_i=fc_i\), of degree at most \(2k-2\). Its product becomes \(1-f^2+fE\), so

\[f g_iP(\beta)=g_i(f-f^3)+f^2g_iE.\]

The first term has an old Boolean certificate through \(3k+2\), and the second an old \(\Gamma_H\) certificate through the same ceiling. This is why the old residual \(E\) in the kernel representation is legal; it is not falsely declared Boolean-zero. Weighted coefficient fields cost at most \(7k\). All fit the original-degree budget \(Te+k\) with \(T\ge2k\).

For a low-rank block, normalize its affine core using the common weight. A high proper core uses its literal affine-ideal kernel representation to make its product \(1-f^2\), with coefficients through \(2k-1\). A core of rank below \(H_*\) uses its exact Fermat zero indicator, of degree below \(2H_*\); a unit core has product zero. Denote the resulting product by \(A\). For its at most \(R\) nonlinear basis inputs \(G_j\), append the exact prefix for \(\prod_j(1-G_j^2)\), making the product \(A\prod_j(1-G_j^2)\). Nonlinear coefficient degree is at most \(2H_*+4R-2\), and affine coefficients fit the same \(T\). On old Boolean points where \(f\ne0\), every companion vanishes. Boolean division certifies each weighted image at its actual degree, within \(Te+k\); coefficient fields cost \(3T+k\).

All maps use old coordinates only and share the same weight. Old axioms remain legal weighted multiples. Substituting and weighting the full PC proof therefore derives \(f\) over the old graph base through \(TD+k\). Its supplied separation excludes that consequence. This proves the finite criterion, without a fresh-field moment ansatz or a change of Boolean coordinates.

Checks, scope and remaining window

Exact enumeration on four 16-vertex head graphs checks all 65,536 subsets per graph and the rational form of (DH-count) at cutoff four. A duplicate-head control is rejected: one actual edge leaves 2,411 independent sets through four, while incorrectly counting it as sixty distinct heads would claim an upper bound of 2,400. Counts and code are retained. These finite checks concern the combinatorial estimate, not the asymptotic graph separator.

The earlier absolute-head obstruction assumed disjoint heads, hence at most \(s/2\) quadratic pairs. The new case uses up to quadratic-many distinct pairs and does not contradict it. It also avoids the invalid odd-prime polar extraction: all heads come from coefficient row reduction in the actual coordinates, and old relations have explicit certificates.

Combining this criterion with the full-board compiler means a hypothetical polynomial-line ternary proof must, on a graph supplied for these parameters, have some block in the intermediate projected-rank range \(R<r_a<Q_*\). It does not show that every proof can avoid this window, nor that high full-board rank stays high after restriction. The unrestricted lower bound remains open.

Next task. Address the intermediate window using source-compatible profile structure or a sharper reduction image, tested against the one-probe and two-column easy controls. A new hypothesis must reduce this actual remaining window, not merely restate that its rank is large.

Process and timing. Counting all distinct heads replaced an inapplicable coordinate-change argument with a direct reduction. Initial exploratory derivation preceded this cycle’s clock; subsequent work is instrumented, with interruption and pause-request handling mixed into the active windows. The user requested a pause after this checkpoint.

21 September 2026 — Broad geometry survey: filtered images and certificate costs must be controlled separately

Status: bounded literature and route review with elementary falsifying examples; no new PHP lower bound or unblocking theorem. Research pauses after this requested cycle. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question, stopping point and outcome

The missing implication is a uniform treatment of selector tuples in the intermediate projected-rank window of the low-or-dense criterion. At the user’s request, screen hyperplane arrangements, Hilbert functions/order shattering, regularity, algebraic cryptanalysis and scheme-theoretic language broadly. Stop after identifying their hypotheses, elementary failure controls and a concrete applicable next test; do not launch a new extended line. A privately supplied external conversation motivated the survey but is not a mathematical authority.

Outcome. No screened theorem closes the window. The useful refinement is to seek a degree-compatible reduction with certificates attached to its reducers, tracking both image dimension and derivation cost. This extends the already used head-count viewpoint rather than replacing the route with a generic geometry claim. The main notebook already discusses degree falls and semi-regular Hilbert-series heuristics; those are rediscovered connections, not new mechanisms.

1. Affine rank and density do not supply the needed restriction bound

Use rank of the linear part of the map \(x\mapsto(g_i(x))_i\), not dimension of a span containing constants. Its full-field image is an affine \(r\)-flat \(A\). A choice of \(r\) coordinate functions gives an injective projection of \(A\), so \(A\cap(\mathbb F_p^\times)^s\) has at most \((p-1)^r\) points. Each fiber is a translate of the common kernel. This validates the parallel-flat description on the full field, before intersecting with the Boolean cube. It does not identify the image of the Boolean cube with all of \(A\).

A direct falsification of rank-only shrinkage is \(g_i=1+x_i\), \(1\le i\le v\), over \(\mathbb F_3\). The linear rank is \(v\), but all values on the cube are nonzero, so the false set is the entire cube. Its degree-\(\le k\) restriction dimension is \(\sum_{j=0}^{\min(k,v)}\binom vj\): Boolean multilinear monomials are independent, as follows successively by evaluating each variable at zero and one. Moreover \(1-(1+x_i)^2=x_i-x_i^2\), already an old Boolean relation through two. Thus our certified nonlinear quotient correctly discards this example. It does not obstruct a theorem after appropriate cleanup, but raw affine rank cannot replace that cleanup. A vaguely specified factor “subexponential in rank” is not necessarily contradicted in the small-\(k\) regime; it must be small enough for the actual common-kernel inequality.

The density hint in the external discussion uses the support bound in the wrong direction. If a nonzero degree-\(k\) multilinear polynomial vanishes on \(S\), the bound on its support says \(1-|S|/2^v\ge2^{-k}\), not \(|S|/2^v\le2^{-k}\). The support bound follows by induction: for \(f=x_v a+b\), if \(a\ne0\), each assignment where \(a\ne0\) has at least one nonzero extension; otherwise discard \(x_v\). Sparse sets alone do not give a good filtered-image estimate: the \(v\) unit vectors have density \(v/2^v\), yet the coordinate functions restrict to \(v\) independent functions on them. No asymptotic theorem is inferred from a density heuristic.

2. Standard monomials are relevant; ordinary lexicographic order needs care

Mészáros–Rónyai, Standard Monomials and Extremal Vector Systems, §§1.2–1.3 relates shattering, standard monomials and coordinate downshifts. Its account distinguishes arbitrary term orders from lex orders and provides special criteria for order-independent bases. This supports using the framework, not assuming that a lex basis preserves total-degree filtration.

Here is an exact four-point control over any field. Let \(S=\{(yz,y,z):y,z\in\{0,1\}\}\). The vanishing ideal is generated by \(x-yz,y^2-y,z^2-z\): substitution leaves the Boolean two-variable quotient, whose evaluations on four points are independent. For lex order \(x>y>z\), these generators give standard monomials \(1,y,z,yz\), only three of degree at most one. But \(1,x,y,z\) restrict to \(1,yz,y,z\), four independent functions. Hence \(H_S(1)=4\), not three. A degree-compatible order, or a separately justified filtered basis, is necessary for the intended degree count. This control audits a potential use of order shattering; it does not refute the cited results.

The existing dense-head count already implements a partial standard-monomial argument with a degree-compatible order and explicit certificates. Its image may exceed the true Hilbert function because its reducers need not form a Gröbner basis. Improving that overestimate with cheaply derived additional heads is a concrete possibility; semantic Hilbert bounds alone would not provide those certificates.

3. Regularity has the right vocabulary but the wrong automatic conclusion

Derksen–Sidman bounds the regularity of the homogeneous ideal of an arrangement of \(d\) projective linear subspaces by \(d\). Applied naively to exponentially many components this does not meet our budget. Intersecting with the Boolean cube and using the original selector generators introduce further obligations; the arrangement ideal is not automatically the required presentation of our truncated consequence space.

Caminata–Gorla, Solving degree, last fall degree, and related invariants, Theorem 3.1, separates solving degree into last fall degree and the largest reduced-Gröbner-basis degree for a degree-compatible order. Their regularity study stresses hypotheses and homogenizing the supplied equations. This is useful orientation for the missing certificate cost; no theorem from these papers is imported as a PC or NS bound here. Solving-degree algorithms, bounded PC closure and NS certificates need their own exact translation.

In particular, a low-degree basis of the final ideal does not itself bound the degree needed to obtain that basis from the given axioms. For an unsatisfiable PHP system the unrestricted ideal is the unit ideal, yet its low-degree consequence spaces are precisely what the lower bound studies. Homogenizing an ideal and homogenizing a chosen generator list must not be interchanged without accounting for saturation and degree falls.

4. Semi-regularity, arrangements and schemes: promising language, missing hypotheses

Bardet–Faugère–Salvy–Spaenlehauer analyze Boolean quadratic solving under explicit algebraic assumptions. Arbitrary proof-derived selectors over \(\mathbb F_3\), together with structured PHP relations, do not come with those genericity assumptions. Search found the main notebook’s no-fall criterion and semi-regular-series discussion; its closure condition is an obligation, not a free theorem about augmented sources. The new literature connection does not discharge it.

Arrangement point counts or intersection data alone do not control the total-degree filtration of restrictions to the Boolean cube. No applicable arrangement-complement theorem was established in this short screen. The ordinary false-set ideal contains Booleanity, and its coordinate ring is a quotient of \(\prod_{a\in\{0,1\}^v}\mathbb F_p\): evaluation gives this isomorphism, with point indicators \(\prod_{a_i=1}x_i\prod_{a_i=0}(1-x_i)\). Every ideal in that product is a product of zero or whole-field ideals, so these quotients are reduced. Thus nilpotent structure is not the source of the semantic-versus-certificate gap here; the degree filtration and original presentation are. Homogenization can retain useful information at infinity, so zero-dimensionality is not a reason to dismiss all regularity or scheme methods. No positive-dimensional relaxation or base-change argument with the required quantitative transfer was found.

Route decision and concrete next test

Keep the unrestricted odd-prime PHP goal and the intermediate-rank obligation. Refine the next test to modest-degree Macaulay consequences of the actual selectors together with permitted old relations, retaining explicit NS witnesses for every new reducer. Compare their standard-monomial image bound with the same separated space \(W_k\), and independently track the cost of expressing a kernel weight in the original generators. Require the sum of image dimensions across all blocks to be strictly smaller than \(\dim W_k\), and require the resulting weighted companion and field certificates to remain inside the graph’s PC ceiling. Merely computing a smaller semantic Hilbert function, or a low-degree final basis, is not success.

Before a substantial computation, test the proposed criterion on the already recorded common-factor, two-column and reserve-coordinate controls. Stop or redirect if image savings require unaffordable certificate degrees, or if an allowed control defeats a uniform claim. This is a more specific experiment within the current route, not an assertion that higher-degree completion will solve it. The disequality compiler remains an alternative requiring an efficient domain-aware equation conversion.

Verification and provenance. The examples above were checked by their explicit symbolic arguments; no numerical sweep, dependency installation or formalization was performed. Primary-source scope was checked through the linked public pages and passages on 21 September 2026. An attempted older author-hosted lex-game PDF failed; the accessible Mészáros–Rónyai paper supplied the limited standard-monomial facts used here. No third-party full text is copied into the repository. Initial task framing preceded the clock; this cycle stops at the survey and checkpoint.

21 September 2026 — Full Boolean ideal completion cannot repair the absolute image bound

Status: working exact image obstruction with finite modular controls. It rules out a Boolean-only dimension shortcut, not PHP-relative reduction or the desired lower bound. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Missing implication and test. The intermediate-rank selector case needs a simultaneous kernel whose certificates fit the graph degree budget. The geometry survey suggested additional certified reducers. Before a general completion computation, test whether unrestricted Boolean ideal completion can uniformly supply enough image contraction. Reuse the actual selector family from the reserve-coordinate control, now counting all coordinates. Stop at its exact filtered image and compare it with the existing graph guarantee.

Exact filtered quotient for the ternary triple family

Over \(\mathbb F_3\), take \(r\ge1\) disjoint triples \((x_i,y_i,z_i)\), \(s=3r\), and actual selector inputs \(q_i=1-(x_i+y_i+z_i)^2\). Let \(S\) be their common-zero set on the Boolean cube and let \(J\) be generated by all \(q_i\) and Boolean equations. Then the associated graded Hilbert series of the total-degree filtration of \(\mathbb F_3[X]/J\) is

\[ (1+3t+2t^2)^r=(1+2t)^r(1+t)^r. \tag{TI-series} \]

In particular, the dimension \(H_S(k)\) of restrictions of degree-\(\le k\) polynomials is the sum of the coefficients through \(k\). The standard monomials are exactly the squarefree monomials containing no pair \(x_iy_i\), for a degree-compatible order with \(x_i>y_i>z_i\) in each triple. The original degree-two selector/Boolean generators already give this exact image; higher-degree completion cannot improve its dimension.

Proof in one triple. The false set consists of the six assignments of weights one and two; \(000\) and \(111\) are excluded. Modulo Booleanity through two,

\[ \bar q=1-x-y-z+xy+xz+yz,\qquad z\bar q\equiv xyz \quad\text{modulo Booleanity through three}. \]

Thus every squarefree monomial reduces, without increasing degree, to the span of \(1,x,y,z,xz,yz\): use \(\bar q\) for \(xy\), and \(z\bar q\) for \(xyz\). Each replacement has an NS witness from the original selector and Boolean equations at the degree of the replaced monomial. These six functions are independent on the six points. Indeed, vanishing of \(a_0+a_xx+a_yy+a_zz+a_{xz}xz+a_{yz}yz\) at the three singleton assignments gives \(a_x=a_y=a_z=-a_0\). Evaluation at \(110\) gives \(a_0=0\); evaluation at \(101\) and \(011\) then gives \(a_{xz}=a_{yz}=0\). So the quotient has no additional relations among these monomials, and its degree counts are \(1,3,2\).

For disjoint triples the evaluation matrix on product points is the tensor product of these invertible six-by-six matrices. Products of the six local basis elements are therefore independent. Local degree-preserving reductions span every filtered piece by products of total degree at most the cutoff. This proves (TI-series), the exact restriction dimension and the equality of \(J\) with the vanishing ideal of \(S\). The same argument shows that reducing only by Booleanity and the \(\bar q_i\) in the indicated degree-compatible order has these standard monomials: they span the quotient, are independent, and the leading forbidden pairs are \(x_iy_i\). All reduction witnesses retain their original degree ceiling.

The tuple has nonlinear rank exactly \(r\) modulo Booleanity and affine polynomials, since each \(\bar q_i\) has its own unique \(x_iy_i\) coefficient. This is a statement about the Boolean quotient, not a claim that its rank remains \(r\) modulo graph-PHP relations.

An actual-image obstruction, not just a loose upper estimate

For \(1\le k\le s/4\), count ordered \(k\)-subsets avoiding the \(r\) disjoint forbidden pairs. After \(j\) distinct allowed vertices have been chosen, at most \(j\) additional vertices are forbidden partners, so at least \(s-2j\) choices remain. Hence

\[ H_S(k)\ \ge\ \frac{\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}(s-2j)}{k!} \ \ge\ \binom sk\exp(-k^2/s). \tag{TI-lower} \]

For the second inequality, divide by \(\binom sk\) and apply \(\log(1-u)\ge-u/(1-u)\) to \(u=j/(s-j)\). The logarithm of the ratio is at least \(-\sum_{j<k}j/(s-2j)\ge-k(k-1)/(2(s-2k+2))\ge-k^2/s\). This is an exact inequality, with no probabilistic independence assumption.

Set \(s=15(N+1)\) and \(r=5(N+1)\). The available graph guarantee is \(L=\tfrac12e^{-5k^2/N}\binom sk\). Therefore

\[ \frac{H_S(k)}{L}\ge 2\exp\!\left((5/N-1/s)k^2\right)>1. \]

Even the true full-cube restriction dimension cannot satisfy the sufficient absolute comparison \(H_S(k)<L\), already for one such block. Any linear reduction whose kernel consists of polynomials in \(J\) has image dimension at least \(H_S(k)\): its kernel is contained in the kernel of evaluation on \(S\), and rank-nullity applies. Thus arbitrary Boolean ideal completion, even with certificates free, cannot repair that particular comparison.

This strengthens the earlier disjoint-head estimate obstruction on a legal selector family: that record compared an upper estimate with a guaranteed lower estimate; here the actual Boolean image is already too large. It refines the prior reserve-coordinate example rather than introducing a new family. Its point density is \((3/4)^r\), exponentially small, while its low-degree image remains large. The obstacle is dimension relative to the guaranteed separator, not sparse point counting.

Limits, exact controls and route decision

The conclusion does not assert that the restriction of a particular \(W_k\) is injective, that the true graph-separated space has dimension only \(L\), or that the image remains large after adding bounded-degree old PHP relations. The full PHP ideal is the unit ideal and must not be used as an uncosted semantic quotient. Nor does the Boolean rank calculation identify the projected graph rank needed in the low-or-dense theorem.

An exact modular checker enumerated at most 64 Boolean assignments and 64 monomials. Over \(\mathbb F_3\), filtered ranks were \(1,4,6,6\) for one triple and \(1,7,20,32,36,36,36\) for two, agreeing with (TI-series). A field-change control over \(\mathbb F_5\), using the actual selectors \(1-L^4\), has ranks \(1,4,7,7\); the ternary formula correctly fails there. Complete results and the adjacent C++ checker are retained. These are bounded tests of the symbolic proof, not graph-PHP or asymptotic checks.

Next action. Continue the intermediate-rank obligation using the image of the actual separated space modulo a degree-bounded span generated by selectors and old graph relations, with explicit certificates. Do not perform a generic Boolean-only completion sweep: this exact control shows it cannot yield the required universal absolute bound. A successful relative estimate must retain the same \(W_k\), graph and degree ceiling and handle cheap-profile controls; otherwise reconsider the source-specific route.

Contribution and process assessment. This cycle discharges a falsification test, not the main missing implication: it makes the dependence on PHP-relative structure unavoidable for the proposed completion approach, and reusing an existing control avoided an uninformative large computation. No framework change was needed.
