21 September 2026 — Row-local selectors enter the all-prime matching-reader endpoint

Status: working all-prime source exclusion and restricted-query Res(Lin) consequence for row support \(n^\alpha\operatorname{polylog}n\), \(\alpha<1/5\). Unrestricted spread-out forms remain open. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question and change of approach. The intermediate-rank case needs PHP-specific structure after the exact Boolean-image control. Before computing new relative kernels, inspect the recent main-notebook empty-aware switching theorem. It applies to actual incompatible matching terms and does not charge their number. Test whether bounded-row selectors have that representation with original-generator certificates, allowing different row supports for every input. Stop at a complete source conversion and its parameter range, or a failed degree ledger.

Certified expansion of one row-local selector

Use full functional unary PHP on \((n+1)\)-by-\(n\) over a prime field \(\mathbb F_p\). Suppose an affine form \(L\), modulo constant combinations of the old row equations, has a representative \(L'\) supported on a set \(A\) of \(t\) pigeon rows. Put \(\delta=p-1\), \(q=1-L^\delta\), and

\[ G=\sum_{\substack{\phi:A\hookrightarrow[n]\\L'(e_\phi)=0}} \prod_{i\in A}X_{i,\phi(i)}. \tag{RL-expansion} \]

Then \(q-G\in I_{\max(\delta,t)}(\Gamma_{\rm fun})\). All coefficients in \(G\) are one, and its matching terms are pairwise incompatible. The empty-row case is a constant zero or one. This is ordinary NS membership in the original ring, not just equality on models of an unsatisfiable base.

Proof. First \(L-L'\) has an old row certificate through one. Factoring \(L^\delta-(L')^\delta\) gives a certificate through \(\delta\). For any polynomial \(P\) supported on \(A\), reduce powers by Booleanity and monomials using two cells in one row by same-row exclusions, preserving the original degree. Each remaining monomial chooses at most one cell per row. Multiply it by the row sums of its missing rows to obtain a homogeneous row-state expansion. Telescoping these multiplications uses row-generator witnesses through \(t\). Thus

\[ P-\sum_{\phi:A\to[n]}P(e_\phi)\prod_{i\in A}X_{i,\phi(i)} \in I_{\max(\deg P,t)}. \]

The coefficients are fixed by evaluation at one-hot row assignments, where the row-state monomials are the point indicators. Apply this to \(P=1-(L')^\delta\); Fermat makes every coefficient zero or one. Every noninjective assignment term contains an old same-column collision, with a certificate through \(t\), and can be removed. Distinct injective assignments on the same \(A\) differ on some row, so their terms are incompatible by a same-row exclusion. This proves the assertion. It extends the monomial-reduction method of the two-row interpolation lemma; that lemma's recorded binary Lean coverage does not verify the present all-prime, arbitrary-row statement.

Complete source conversion, with the cost charged before switching

Let a complete accuracy-one block have old selectors \(q_i\) as inputs, with every support size at most \(R\). Replace them by the corresponding \(G_i\), keeping the same coefficient variables and all equations \(r_i^p-r_i\). Write \(C=\max(p-1,R,1)\), \(P_q=1-\sum_i r_iq_i\), \(P_G=1-\sum_i r_iG_i\), and

\[ E=P_q-P_G=\sum_i r_i(G_i-q_i)\in I_{C+1}. \]

This certificate is over the old generators but in the ring including the unchanged fresh coefficients. For every original companion,

\[ q_iP_q=G_iP_G+(q_i-G_i)P_G+q_iE. \tag{RL-companion} \]

The first term is a target companion of degree at most \(2R+1\). The second has an old certificate through \(C+R+1\), and the third through \(C+(p-1)+1\). All are at most \(2C+1\). Fields and old axioms are unchanged. Hence an ordinary source PC refutation through \(D\) gives a target PC refutation through

\[ D_*=\max(D,2C+1). \]

The replacement is simultaneous over the family; costs do not accumulate over inputs, blocks or proof height. A proof line is reused once derived. No degree-preserving NS-flattening assertion is needed. A block may have inputs on entirely different row sets; no common small row support for the whole tuple is assumed.

Source exclusion for every fixed exponent below one fifth

Working theorem. Fix a prime \(p\), \(0\le\alpha<1/5\), a polynomial block-count bound \(M\le n^c\), and fixed polylogarithmic factors. Suppose each input of each complete accuracy-one selector block is \(1-L^{p-1}\), where \(L\) is supported modulo rows on at most \(R\le n^\alpha\operatorname{polylog}n\) rows. Then for sufficiently large \(n\), no ordinary PC refutation of polylogarithmic degree exists over full functional unary PHP with these blocks and all coefficient fields.

Proof and parameters. Choose a fixed \(\beta\) with

\[ \alpha<\beta<(1-\alpha)/4,\qquad N=\lceil n^\beta\rceil. \]

This interval is nonempty precisely for \(\alpha<1/5\). Apply (RL-expansion) and (RL-companion), obtaining \(D_*=n^{\alpha+o(1)}\) (an upper bound also when \(\alpha=0\)). For every block, form the ordered disjunction of all matching terms appearing in its inputs. Term width is at most \(\max(1,R)\). The empty-aware switching estimate on a uniform partial matching leaving \((N+1)\)-by-\(N\) gives, for any fixed positive integer \(s\), a depth-\(s\) failure probability at most

\[ 2\left((N+2)\sqrt{\frac{8\max(1,R)N(N+1)}{n-N+1}}\right)^s =n^{-s(1-\alpha-4\beta)/2+o(1)}. \]

Its small-parameter and \(s\le N\) hypotheses hold for large \(n\). Choose \(s>2c/(1-\alpha-4\beta)\). The union bound over \(M\) blocks is \(o(1)\), so some restriction gives every block a tree of height below \(s\). The estimate is independent of the number of terms: the possibly large row-state expansions create no new blocks and no hidden factor \(n^R\) in this probability.

Use the complete matching-reader coefficient map from that same record, with \(T\le s\). Terms within each input remain incompatible under restriction. Its degree-\(T\) coefficients have all original companion images certified within \(T e_i\) and all coefficient-field images within \(Tp\). Therefore the target proof becomes an old functional-PHP PC refutation through

\[ B=sD_*=n^{\alpha+o(1)}=o(N). \]

The all-field old matching barrier excludes this once \(N\ge2B-1\). There are no remaining affine-source blocks in this application, so the mixed affine endpoint's additional degree conditions are unnecessary. This proves the theorem. The inherited switching and matching statements remain working research dependencies; the computation below does not independently verify them.

Consequence for ordinary unary PHP proof lines

The full-board compiler turns a hypothetical \(n^c\)-line standard syntactic Res(Lin) refutation over fixed \(\mathbb F_p\) into at most \(n^c\) complete selector blocks at degree \(4p-1\). Its span compression can select actual selector inputs, so row locality is retained. Thus, for every fixed \(\alpha<1/5\), the subclass in which every equation form is supported modulo rows on at most \(n^\alpha\operatorname{polylog}n\) pigeon rows has superpolynomial proof-line complexity for ordinary unary PHP. Strengthening the algebraic base to functional PHP is legitimate for excluding a refutation of the weaker ordinary CNF; functionality is not silently assumed in that CNF.

Equivalently, a hypothetical polynomial-line unrestricted refutation must contain a form exceeding that row-support budget, for each fixed \(\alpha<1/5\) and sufficiently large \(n\). This is a necessary locality obstruction, not an unrestricted line lower bound. It does not cover a single column-local form with coefficients varying across all rows; the earlier ternary row-or-column-query result therefore supplies a different scope. It is also stronger than requiring every whole clause to live on a common small row set: different inputs may collectively read every row.

The previous triple-family Boolean-image barrier is compatible with this theorem. Each triple selector reads at most three rows when interpreted in the full incidence board, so such complete-board selector families are covered regardless of block rank or how many triples a block contains. Their cheap removal uses a matching restriction and certified PHP structure, not a falsely improved full-cube Hilbert bound. This is a concrete family-level way around that obstruction.

Checks, remaining gap and process

Exact ordinary-polynomial NS identities were generated and checked over \(\mathbb F_3\) for one, two and three rows on four columns, and over \(\mathbb F_5\) for three rows. Certificate ceilings were respectively \(2,2,3,4\), exactly \(\max(p-1,t)\); removing a target matching term makes the certificate identity fail. A weak-row control over \(\mathbb F_3\) has four ones in its first row and one in a separate fifth column in its second row. It satisfies Booleanity, row sums and column collisions but violates same-row exclusions. For \(L=X_{00}+X_{01}\), its selector value is zero while the row-state expansion evaluates to two. Thus functionality is an essential hypothesis of this expansion. The checker explicitly verifies the retained axioms on this assignment. Full certificates, encoding and controls, with the adjacent C++ source, are retained. No new finite run purports to prove switching or asymptotic exclusion.

Remaining implication. The unrestricted goal still requires handling forms spread across many rows. Row support is different from nonlinear quotient rank; this theorem neither bounds that support in every short proof nor proves a complete rank/profile dichotomy. Next inspect whether a source-compatible small set of shared global probes can reduce those forms to row-local ones without multiplying profile cost by the number of inputs or blocks. Test the interface against dense low-rank forms and the one-probe control before attempting a broad branching construction.

Process assessment. Reading the newly merged matching-reader result before implementing relative matrices exposed an already proved stronger interface; exact original-generator expansion, rather than another generic kernel computation, supplied a new scoped endpoint. The proof-line consequence is a working application, with no independent novelty claim or new Lean coverage.

21 September 2026 — Clausewise logarithmic global probes can be combined with row-local queries

Status: working complete source transfer and scoped all-prime exclusion. The probe-plus-local decomposition is an explicit hypothesis, not a theorem about arbitrary clauses. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Missing implication and stopping point. The row-local theorem leaves spread-out forms. Test whether a shared global probe part can be handled without multiplying a cost for every input, fixing an unsupported affine fiber, or dropping fresh coefficient fields. The all-prime one-probe map and binary multicoordinate transfer suggest exact residue interpolation. Stop at a complete multi-probe ledger and its affordable scope; do not assume every clause has the required decomposition.

Setup and statement

Fix a prime \(p\), \(0\le\alpha<1/5\), and constants \(c,c_0\). Let \(M\le n^c\) complete accuracy-one old-selector blocks be given over full functional unary PHP. In each block \(a\), assume there are old affine probes \(U_{a,1},\ldots,U_{a,t_a}\), with \(t_a\le c_0\log_p n\), such that every input form admits

\[ L_{a,i}\equiv \ell_{a,i}+\sum_{j=1}^{t_a}b_{a,i,j}U_{a,j} \pmod{\operatorname{span}\{\rho_h-1\}}, \qquad |\operatorname{rows}(\ell_{a,i})| \le n^\alpha\operatorname{polylog}n . \tag{PL-setup} \]

The probes may be dense, dependent and different in every block. The local row sets may differ in every input. Then the source has no polylog-degree ordinary PC refutation for sufficiently large \(n\), with all coefficient fields retained. Consequently ordinary unary-PHP Res(Lin) over this fixed field has no polynomial-line refutation if every clause's forms admit (PL-setup) with these fixed bounds. This remains a restricted-source theorem.

The complete interpolation map

Work first with one block and \(t\) probes, putting \(\delta=p-1\), \(d=\delta t\). Remove scalar selector inputs first: zero inputs can have their coefficients set to zero, while an input one removes its whole block by the unit map. Every remaining actual input \(q_i=1-L_i^\delta\) has degree \(\delta\), so its original companion degree is \(e=2\delta+1\).

For each \(u\in\mathbb F_p^t\), define a row-local branch selector and an old residue indicator

\[ q_{u,i}=1-\left(\ell_i+\sum_jb_{i,j}u_j\right)^\delta,\qquad \chi_u=\prod_{j=1}^t\left(1-(U_j-u_j)^\delta\right). \]

Introduce one complete fresh block on \((q_{u,i})_i\), with coefficients \(R_{u,i}\), product \(P_u=1-\sum_iR_{u,i}q_{u,i}\), companions \(q_{u,i}P_u\) and all fields \(R_{u,i}^p-R_{u,i}\). Define

\[ \Phi(r_i)=\beta_i=\sum_u\chi_uR_{u,i},\quad V=\sum_u\chi_uP_u,\quad T=d+1. \tag{PL-map} \]

The sum of the univariate residue indicators is identically one: it has degree at most \(p-1\) and agrees with one at every field value. Hence \(\sum_u\chi_u=1\) as an ordinary polynomial identity. On each old Boolean assignment exactly one \(\chi_u\) is one, even when the probes are dependent. No equation fixing a probe value is added.

Original-degree certificates and all fresh fields

Let \(E_{u,i}=\chi_u(q_i-q_{u,i})\). First replace \(L_i\) by the right side of (PL-setup); factoring the difference of \(\delta\)-th powers costs \(\delta\) in old row certificates. After that replacement \(E_{u,i}\) vanishes at every old Boolean assignment, and Boolean division gives the remaining witness. Thus \(E_{u,i}\in I_{d+\delta}(\Gamma_{\rm fun})\). Keeping the new \(R\)'s formal gives the exact comparison

\[ \Delta=\Phi(P_q)-V=-\sum_{u,i}R_{u,i}E_{u,i} \in I_{d+\delta+1}(\Gamma_{\rm fun}). \]

Every original companion has the decomposition

\[ q_i\Phi(P_q) =q_i\Delta+\sum_u\chi_u(q_{u,i}P_u)+\sum_uE_{u,i}P_u . \tag{PL-companion} \]

All three certificate costs are at most \(d+2\delta+1\le T(2\delta+1)=Te\). The middle term uses actual branch companions. The other two use old certificates with their original degree charged; they are not free equalities in a semantic PHP quotient.

Frobenius supplies

\[ \beta_i^p-\beta_i =\sum_u\chi_u^p(R_{u,i}^p-R_{u,i}) +\sum_u(\chi_u^p-\chi_u)R_{u,i}. \tag{PL-fields} \]

The first sum uses genuine branch-field axioms through \(pd+p=pT\). The second has old Boolean witnesses through \(pd+1\le pT\). The coefficients are field-valued, not assumed Boolean. As a simple falsifying control for the wrong domain, over \(\mathbb F_3\) choose any old assignment and set its selected branch coefficient to two: then \(\beta_i=2\), its field equation holds, and \(\beta_i^2-\beta_i=2\ne0\).

The coefficient substitution has degree at most \(T\), fixes old variables and is simultaneous for every block with disjoint branch coefficients. All original axioms have images certified within \(T\) times their original degree. Consequently ordinary PC replay takes degree \(D\) to at most \(TD\); substituted NS cofactors also obey the same bound. There is no factor for the number of inputs, overlaps of probes, blocks or proof height. This is the multicoordinate prime-field extension of the cited interpolation mechanism, now with row-local selector branches rather than assumed affine branches.

Inventory and composition with the row-local endpoint

There are at most \(\sum_a p^{t_a}\le n^{c+c_0}\) branch blocks, and \(\max_aT_a=O(\log n)\). For polylogarithmic \(D\), the branch source still has polylogarithmic PC degree. Every branch input is a selector on at most \(R=n^\alpha\operatorname{polylog}n\) rows. Apply the row-local source exclusion. More explicitly, its matching expansion first costs

\[ D_*=\max\!\left(TD,\,2\max(p-1,R,1)+1\right); \]

it does not multiply the row-expansion cost by \(T\). Choose \(\alpha<\beta<(1-\alpha)/4\), leave \(N=\lceil n^\beta\rceil\) holes and choose constant switching height \(s>2(c+c_0)/(1-\alpha-4\beta)\). The resulting old degree \(sD_*=n^{\alpha+o(1)}\) is \(o(N)\), giving the contradiction. The full-board compiler supplies the stated proof-line consequence. Selecting actual input positions during span compression preserves a supplied common probe decomposition.

The exponential branch count has been paid, not hidden. For a polynomially growing \(t\), this application no longer has polynomial branch inventory; a new switching and degree argument would be required. Nor is “low rank” of an unrelated quadratic representation a certificate of (PL-setup). The full old coefficient matrix of the actual forms and the allowed row-equivalence rewrites matter.

Contribution, limits and next test

This adds dense global directions to the row-local endpoint without requiring a common local row support for the clause or common probes across the whole proof. It handles, for example, forms \(L_i=U+\ell_i\) with one arbitrary dense \(U\) and independently chosen allowed local \(\ell_i\). Families whose entire affine form span has logarithmic dimension are included with \(\ell_i=0\). Two-row mixed selectors from the earlier two-column control are included with no probes. These are consistency checks of the stated scope, not a classification of arbitrary high-rank sources.

The main missing implication is now explicit: a hypothetical short unrestricted proof might contain blocks having no affordable probe-plus-row-local decomposition and not covered by the dense graph-rank alternative. Nothing here rules that out. Before further profile refinements, test a proposed structural dichotomy on families of independent dense forms and inspect how a matching restriction changes the relevant affine and selector ranks. A mere restatement in terms of a decomposition parameter would not advance the unrestricted goal.

Review and process. Checked the literal partition identity, old row/Boolean witnesses, actual companion degrees, prime-field domains and the polynomial branch count. No numerical sweep or new formalization was needed for this extension of the recorded interpolation argument. Reusing that complete map avoided an invalid argument that would fix an affine fiber and then apply an unconditioned PHP theorem.

21 September 2026 — Dense selectors defeat the fixed-size dichotomy but become dense enough on smaller boards

Status: working probabilistic control with explicit bounds and local exact checks. It falsifies a fixed-size structural shortcut and identifies a smaller-board escape; no arbitrary-family theorem. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

General claim under review. The intended route needs every source block to become removable within one common degree budget. The current sufficient classes are small nonlinear rank, many quadratic heads, and logarithmic probes plus row-local remainders. Test the missing implication that choosing a sixteen-neighbor graph at the same board size always puts a block in one of these classes. Then test whether a further matching restriction changes the answer. Do not identify a counterexample to that fixed-size implication with a lower bound against all normalizers.

A simultaneous two-size control over \(\mathbb F_3\)

Let \(m=N+1\), \(h=\lceil100N\ln N\rceil\), and \(L=\lceil\sqrt N\rceil\). For sufficiently large \(N\), there are \(h\) linearly independent homogeneous forms \(F_1,\ldots,F_h\) in the full incidence variables of the \(m\)-by-\(N\) board with the following properties for the single legal selector tuple \(q_i=1-F_i^2\).

  1. For every graph with sixteen distinct neighbors per row and every distinguished-neighbor choice, restrict to the graph and eliminate the distinguished variable by the row equation. The nonlinear selector rank modulo old \(I_2\) and affine polynomials lies strictly above \(N\) and at most \(h\).
  2. On each such projected graph, no sublist of \(N\) of the actual forms admits a shared-affine-probe-plus-row-local representation with at most \(T_0=\lfloor N/10\rfloor\) probes and at most \(R_0=\lfloor N/(100\ln N)\rfloor\) rows in each local remainder. The probes, sublist and local supports may be chosen after the graph. In particular this excludes the proved logarithmic-probe/subfifth-row criterion for the whole list and for selected-input bases of its nonlinear quotient.
  3. After every partial matching restriction leaving \((L+1)\)-by-\(L\), and on every sixteen-neighbor graph and distinguished projection of that residual board, the nonlinear selector rank is strictly above \(L^2\).

Sampling the full-board coefficient vectors independently and uniformly over \(\mathbb F_3\) has all these properties with probability tending to one. This is a quantitative construction argument, not an assigned probability that the research goal will succeed.

Dimension and uniform distribution after projection

Consider any sixteen-neighbor graph on an \((a+1)\)-by-\(a\) board. There are \(v=15(a+1)\) nondistinguished coordinates \(Y\). The old projected quadratic generators number at most

\[ 16(a+1)+\binom{16}{2}(a+1) +\sum_j\binom{d_j}{2} \le (136+8a)(a+1), \]

because \(d_j\le a+1\) and \(\sum_jd_j=16(a+1)\). Projected row equations vanish. All other individual projected generators still have degree two: their quadratic parts are squares, products in one row, or products across two distinct rows. Thus the quadratic parts of old \(I_2\) have dimension at most this generator count. Modding out affine polynomials leaves a quotient \(Q\) of the homogeneous quadratic coefficient space with

\[ \dim Q\ge \binom{v+1}{2}-(136+8a)(a+1) =\frac{(a+1)(209(a+1)-241)}2. \tag{DS-room} \]

This is only the degree-two NS quotient; no PC saturation or unrestricted ideal is inserted. Degree-one multiples of original row equations project to zero, so they add no missing quadratic directions.

For a sampled full-board form, the projected coefficient of \(Y_{ij}\) is \(c_{ij}-c_{i,p_i}\). Conditional on the distinguished coefficients, the nondistinguished coefficients remain independent uniform field elements. Hence the whole \(v\)-vector is uniform. A preceding matching restriction only adds a constant and discards other coefficients, so the same assertion holds for every fixed matching restriction and graph. Constants do not affect the nonlinear class of \(1-F_i^2\). Different sampled forms stay independent.

A rank-tail estimate for quadratic selectors

For uniform \(b\in\mathbb F_3^v\), the quadratic class is that of \(-(b\cdot Y)^2\). Squares span all homogeneous quadratics: use the coordinate squares and \((Y_j+Y_k)^2-Y_j^2-Y_k^2=2Y_jY_k\). Thus every nonzero linear functional on \(Q\) gives a nonzero quadratic polynomial in \(b\). Such a polynomial is nonzero on at least one third of \(\mathbb F_3^v\). For completeness, the usual degree-two root bound follows by induction: choose a variable of degree \(d\ge1\), bound vanishing of its leading coefficient by \((2-d)/3\), and otherwise bound its univariate zeros by \(d/3\).

Until the current span fills \(Q\), a functional annihilating that span therefore proves a conditional probability at least \(1/3\) of a rank increment. For any integer \(u<\dim Q\), \(u\le h\),

\[ \Pr(\operatorname{rank}\{q_1,\ldots,q_h\}\le u) \le \binom hu(2/3)^{h-u}. \tag{DS-tail} \]

Indeed, if the final rank is at most \(u\), all successful increments lie in some set of \(u\) positions. For a fixed such set, every other position must fail; on that event the preceding rank never exceeds \(u\), so the conditional failure bound remains valid. Union over the position sets. This also works when \(h>\dim Q\); only \(u<\dim Q\) is required.

There are at most \(N^{16(N+1)}\) ordered graph choices at the original size, which also cover all distinguished choices. Apply (DS-tail) with \(u=N\). The logarithm of the union bound is at most

\[ 16(N+1)\ln N+N\ln(eh/N)-(h-N)\ln(3/2) =\bigl(16-100\ln(3/2)+o(1)\bigr)N\ln N\to-\infty. \]

Here (DS-room) exceeds \(N\). This proves property 1 with failure probability tending to zero.

Counting all probe-plus-local representations, including selected sublists

For a fixed original-size graph and a fixed sublist of \(N\) forms, its linear coefficient matrix is uniform in \(\mathbb F_3^{N\times v}\). Any proposed representation gives a matrix decomposition \(A=CU+B\), where \(U\) has \(T_0\) rows, \(C\) has \(T_0\) columns and each row of \(B\) is supported on at most \(R_0\) of the \(N+1\) pigeon blocks, each of size fifteen. Padding allows fewer probes. All affine constants can be absorbed in local constants and do not change this necessary coefficient condition.

The number of such matrices is at most

\[ 3^{T_0v+NT_0} \left((R_0+1)(N+1)^{R_0}3^{15R_0}\right)^N. \tag{DS-local-count} \]

This enumerates the probes and their coefficients as well as every local support; it does not fix a preferred basis. Dividing by \(3^{Nv}\), the base-three logarithm tends to a negative constant times \(Nv\). In particular it is at most \(-Nv/2\) for sufficiently large \(N\): \(T_0/v\to1/150\), \(T_0/N\to1/10\), \(R_0\log_3(N+1)/v\to1/(1500\ln3)\), and the remaining normalized terms vanish. These estimates leave ample room below one.

Union over at most \(\binom hN\le h^N\) sublists and \(N^{16(N+1)}\) graphs still tends to zero, since their logarithms are \(O(N\ln N)\) whereas \(Nv=\Theta(N^2)\). This proves property 2. Because the nonlinear rank is above \(N\), any basis selected from actual selector positions contains an \(N\)-sublist and cannot evade this direct form-decomposition control. More general degree-certified changes to the input representation are not excluded.

The smaller-board rank is already in the dense regime

The number of matching restrictions leaving \(L+1\) rows and \(L\) holes is at most \((N+1)^{2N}\). For each, there are at most \(L^{16(L+1)}\) residual graph choices. Apply (DS-tail) with \(u=L^2\), which is below (DS-room). The log union bound is at most

\[ 2N\ln(N+1)+16(L+1)\ln L+ L^2\ln(eh/L^2)-(h-L^2)\ln(3/2). \]

Since \(L^2=N+O(\sqrt N)\), this is \((2-100\ln(3/2)+o(1))N\ln N\), tending to minus infinity. This proves property 3. Finally, the probability that the original \(h\) coefficient vectors are dependent is at most \(3^{h-N(N+1)}\), by a union bound over nonzero combinations. It also tends to zero. All properties therefore hold simultaneously for some family.

At the original size the upper rank \(h=O(N\ln N)\) is below the dense threshold \(Q_*=\Theta(N^{3/2})\) for one block, while the lower rank \(N\) is above every fixed sublinear-power low threshold. Together with property 2, this refutes the proposed exhaustiveness of those direct sufficient cases on an unchanged board, even after selecting the sparse graph adaptively.

At the smaller size, however, \(L^2\) eventually exceeds its threshold \(Q_*=\Theta(L^{3/2})\). Choose the residual graph supplied by the separated-space lemma and apply the existing dense-source map. For any polylogarithmic requested proof degree in the original \(N\), the resulting degree \(O(\sqrt L\,\operatorname{polylog}L)\) is \(o(L)\), within the old barrier. Thus these very same selector blocks are removable by the existing dense endpoint after a further matching restriction. The control does not refute a structural alternative that is allowed to choose a smaller board. It supplies positive evidence for that precise escape on this random family, not a universal restriction theorem.

Checks and assessment of the line

An exact local check tested all 728 nonzero quadratic functionals in three variables over \(\mathbb F_3\), each on all 27 coefficient vectors. Minimum nonzero support was \(12/27\), above the conservative \(1/3\) used in the proof. Square coefficient vectors span dimension six; over \(\mathbb F_2\) the negative control has dimension only three, confirming that this argument must not be ported across characteristic two. A two-row, three-neighbor coefficient projection has exactly three preimages for each of its 243 projected coefficient/constant values. Full counts and scope and the adjacent checker are retained. No finite enumeration of the asymptotic graph family was attempted.

The earlier dense affine survival control already warned that literal killing does not follow from rank. The present test concerns ternary nonlinear quotient ranks on all sixteen-neighbor projections and their change under a smaller matching residual, rather than reusing that binary claim beyond its scope.

Route assessment. Further refinements of a fixed-size local/probe dichotomy will not settle the unrestricted goal: the explicit count above defeats its current direct form, including selected-input bases. The successful smaller-board test supports investigating a degree-certified alternative across residual sizes. The necessary next implication is deterministic control for arbitrary input families, including structured low-support families; random-coefficient success alone cannot establish it. Test a quantitative rank-survival/profile alternative under a common matching restriction, retaining old quotient certificates and the original degree budget, or find a family that defeats that stronger claim.

Process assessment. One family tested both the proposed obstruction and its smaller-board escape, preventing an overstatement that generic dense forms defeat the whole route. The initial counting idea arose during the preceding checkpoint; the detailed proof and exact checks belong to this cycle. No framework change or independent novelty claim was needed.

21 September 2026 — Restricting before expansion removes the one-fifth row-support limit

Status: working all-prime refinement: any fixed sublinear power of per-input row support is allowed, also with clausewise logarithmic global probes. Arbitrary dense remainders remain open. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Missing implication and test. The route needs a common restriction that puts arbitrary allowed blocks in a proved removable class. The dense-family review showed that changing board size matters. Test the cheap-profile side uniformly for every coefficient family whose individual row remainders have sublinear-power support. The previous one-fifth bound charged an expansion on the original board; first restrict the actual constant-degree source instead. Stop at a simultaneous support bound with all inventory, probe and degree costs preserved.

Statement and exact scope

Fix a prime \(p\), \(0\le\alpha<1\), and fixed polynomial/polylogarithmic bounds. Consider \(M\le n^c\) complete accuracy-one selector blocks over full functional \((n+1)\)-by-\(n\) PHP, with all coefficient fields and a requested polylogarithmic PC degree. In each block suppose

\[ L_{a,i}\equiv \ell_{a,i}+\sum_{j=1}^{t_a}b_{a,i,j}U_{a,j} \pmod{\operatorname{span}\{\rho_h-1\}}, \quad t_a\le c_0\log_p n,\quad |\operatorname{rows}(\ell_{a,i})|\le n^\alpha\operatorname{polylog}n . \tag{RT-source} \]

The global affine probes may differ between blocks, and row supports may differ between inputs. For sufficiently large \(n\), there is no such PC refutation. With \(t_a=0\), this includes every fixed exponent \(\alpha<1\) of per-input row support. Via the full-board compiler it gives the corresponding superpolynomial proof-line lower bound for ordinary unary-PHP Res(Lin) whose clauses admit (RT-source). It does not assert that arbitrary clauses admit it.

Compress the actual selector inventory before counting supports

Write \(\delta=p-1\) and \(v=n(n+1)\). In each tuple, remove scalar zero inputs by setting their coefficients to zero; an actual input one allows the unit map removing its block. From the remaining selectors choose a basis of their ordinary polynomial span, consisting of actual input positions. There are at most \(\binom{v+\delta}{\delta}\) positions. Set every nonselected original coefficient to zero and identify selected coefficients with the new block coefficients. The product becomes exactly the basis product. Every original input is a constant combination of selected inputs, so its companion is the same combination of their companions. All nonscalar selectors have actual degree \(\delta\), so this costs no additional degree; coefficient fields become either zero or the corresponding genuine new field equation.

This complete source compression preserves every raw coefficient use under substitution and preserves the supplied row/probe representations because the basis positions are actual selectors. Thus the total number \(K\) of row-support sets needing a union bound satisfies

\[ K\le M\binom{n(n+1)+p-1}{p-1}\le n^C \]

for some fixed \(C>c+2(p-1)\) and sufficiently large \(n\). No assumption that the original tuples were short is needed. No basis change in the affine forms themselves is declared a free clause transformation.

One restriction thins all local remainders

Choose \(0<\beta<1-\alpha\), set \(n_1=\lceil n^\beta\rceil\), and take a uniform partial matching leaving \(n_1+1\) rows and \(n_1\) holes. Its live row set \(S\) is a uniform \((n_1+1)\)-subset of the \(n+1\) rows. For any fixed support set \(A\) of size at most \(R=n^\alpha\operatorname{polylog}n\), and any fixed positive integer \(u\),

\[ \Pr(|A\cap S|\ge u) \le \binom Ru\,\frac{(n_1+1)_u}{(n+1)_u} \le \binom Ru\left(\frac{n_1+1}{n+1}\right)^u \le n^{-u(1-\alpha-\beta)+o(1)} . \tag{RT-tail} \]

The first inequality unions over the \(u\)-subsets of \(A\). The second follows term by term from \((n_1+1-j)/(n+1-j)\le(n_1+1)/(n+1)\). This is sampling without replacement; no independence or negative-association assumption about different supports is used. Pick a fixed integer \(u>(C+1)/(1-\alpha-\beta)\). Union over all \(K\) supports gives failure probability \(o(1)\). Some single matching restriction therefore leaves fewer than \(u\) live rows in every local remainder simultaneously.

Assigned rows make only constant contributions to each affine form. Original row equations restrict to zero or to the residual row equations, so (RT-source) remains a certified identity modulo rows. Global probes remain old affine forms and retain their number, not their original support. The restricted old base is precisely functional \((n_1+1)\)-by-\(n_1\) PHP. Restricting the existing PC proof does not increase degree.

All parameters still fit the established endpoint

Since \(n_1\) is a fixed power of \(n\), polynomial inventory stays polynomial in \(n_1\), the requested degree stays polylogarithmic, and \(t_a=O(\log n)=O(\log n_1)\). Every local remainder now reads only the constant number \(u-1\) of rows. Apply the complete logarithmic-probe theorem on this residual board with its local-support exponent zero. Its underlying row-state expansion costs only \(O_p(u)\), independently of the original \(R\); that is the order-of-operations gain.

Explicitly, residue interpolation costs \(T=O(\log n_1)\), followed by degree

\[ D_*=\max\!\left(TD,\,2\max(p-1,u-1,1)+1\right) =\operatorname{polylog}n_1. \]

A further matching restriction at the established endpoint leaves a fixed positive power of \(n_1\) holes and yields constant-height matching-reader maps, whose old degree is still polylogarithmic. The all-field linear old barrier then applies. No degree factor \(R\), product of support sizes, or sum over blocks has been introduced. For zero probes, simply use the constant-row case of the earlier direct theorem.

The constant-degree full-board compiler gives the proof-line consequence. The earlier one-fifth theorem and its logarithmic-probe extension remain valid, but their original row-support exponent restriction is no longer the best available bound. This is a refinement by reordering certified operations, not a correction of their proofs.

Checks and remaining implication

The exact support checker enumerated all 4,368 five-row live sets among sixteen rows, for twenty support-size/threshold pairs. It verified the hypergeometric counts, the witness-subset union bound and its power upper bound using integer arithmetic. The full-support control retains all five live rows on every sample, demonstrating why this thinning argument does not automatically extend to unrestricted row supports. All counts and bounds and the adjacent C++ checker are retained. This finite check does not substitute for the asymptotic union bound or the inherited source theorems.

The existing sublinear-feature theorem charges a common feature span for a whole parent. Here the local supports are bounded per input and can collectively cover the board, so that earlier hypothesis is not being inferred from their individual sizes. The new common restriction is the needed additional mechanism.

Contribution and next task. One deterministic sufficient side of the across-size alternative is now uniform over all coefficients: fixed sublinear-power row remainders become constant-row remainders simultaneously. The remaining family may require \(n^{1-o(1)}\) rows in some remainders even after all logarithmic-probe choices, and need not behave like the random dense control. The next task is to test whether structured spread-out coefficient families acquire dense nonlinear rank at a common smaller scale or have another proved cheap profile. Do not extrapolate (RT-tail) past its positive exponent or confuse this necessary locality obstruction with the unrestricted lower bound.

Process assessment. Checking a cheap preliminary restriction before the expensive conversion removed an artificial exponent limit; the broad structural implication remains open. The initial source reading also included exploratory interpretation before the mathematics marker. No framework change or new formalization was required.

21 September 2026 — Weak-base compilation makes column-occupancy freezing available

Status: working weak-base compiler audit and scoped all-prime source exclusion. Ordinary PHP is the target; the functional variant cannot use this freezing step unchanged. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question and stopping point. Test a structured spread-out family, the ternary forms \(\sigma_j+\sigma_k\) with \(\sigma_j=\sum_iX_{ij}\), whose selectors represent pairs of empty holes modulo column axioms. Their row support is large and they need not fit a small common probe span. Before building a new graph or symmetric-count argument, inspect existing occupancy tools. The recorded freezing map handles all column statistics, but only on the weak base. The required missing interface is therefore whether the ordinary-CNF compiler really needs functionality. Stop after auditing that interface and a complete application, keeping the two bases distinct.

The ordinary unary compiler needs only the weak linear-row base

Let \(\Gamma_{\rm w}\) contain \(X_{ij}^2-X_{ij}\), same-column collisions \(X_{ij}X_{i'j}\) for \(i\ne i'\), and the linear row equations \(\rho_i-1=\sum_jX_{ij}-1\). It does not contain same-row exclusions or onto axioms. Every \(S\)-line standard syntactic Res(Lin) refutation of ordinary unary PHP over fixed \(\mathbb F_p\) gives at most \(S\) complete accuracy-one selector blocks over \(\Gamma_{\rm w}\), with inputs \(q=1-L^{p-1}\), all coefficient fields, polynomial input inventory after actual-position compression, and a PC refutation through \(4p-1\).

Audit and proof. The previous complete-board proof deliberately strengthened to functional PHP, but the ordinary-CNF derivations use fewer generators. For a positive pigeon row put \(t_j=1-(1-X_{ij})^{p-1}\) and use its block product \(P\). The identity

\[ P=\sum_jt_jP-\sum_j(t_j-X_{ij})P-(\rho_i-1)P \]

uses only complete companions, Booleanity and the linear row equation, through \(2p-1\). The difference \(t_j-X_{ij}\) is Boolean-zero through \(p-1\). Hole initials have width two and use the same-column collision axiom; Boolean initials use Booleanity, both through \(3p-2\). There are no functional same-row initial clauses in ordinary PHP.

The full local-rule proof was reread: weakening, contraction and simplification use source spans and complete companions; resolution uses the Boolean identity \(t_ft_g(1-t_{\lambda f+\mu g})=0\), followed by the two completed premise lines. These steps require no PHP exclusions beyond the initials. Their ceiling remains \(4p-1\), with no proof-height factor. Thus the entire forward compilation already works over \(\Gamma_{\rm w}\).

This is a weaker algebraic strengthening of the ordinary CNF, not a removal of a CNF axiom. The functional-CNF variant still requires its same-row initials and retains the earlier functional-base compiler. Likewise, previous reductions certified only modulo functional \(I_2\) may not be applied before a weak-only map. One may explicitly strengthen a residual weak base later when a lower-bound endpoint permits it.

The existing affine freezing map is now applicable

The recorded occupancy map takes \(K=p-1\), makes a matching prefix of size \(n\bmod K\), and uses \(K\) copies of an \((N+1)\)-by-\(N\) residual board, where

\[ N=\left\lfloor n/(p-1)\right\rfloor-1. \]

Copied cells map to residual \(Y_{ij}\), a distinguished original row uses \(u_j=1-\sum_iY_{ij}\) in each column copy, and the other cells are constants. Every original column sum becomes a literal zero or one. The degree-one/two witnesses for the weak base were checked: copied rows and the distinguished row map to residual row combinations; \(u_j^2-u_j\) and \(Y_{ij}u_j\) have Boolean/column-collision certificates through two. Thus the affine map preserves ordinary PC degree and the original NS generator costs.

The existing statistic-package theorem already removes arbitrary finite packages depending only on column sums. In particular the empty-pair selector family is an application of that old theorem, not a new discovery. For the intended proof-line application, however, the initial cell-literal selectors must also be handled; they are not column statistics. The next argument includes them.

Functionality cannot be silently restored before freezing. The map repeats an empty-column indicator in the distinguished row and does not certify all same-row collisions. The recorded functional-freezing barrier is quantitative: its frozen pattern has imbalance \(p\), so an original-degree functional upgrade would leave at most \(2p-2\) holes, not an unbounded hard residual board.

One exceptional column per input, with arbitrary global occupancy shifts

Working source theorem. Fix \(p\) and polynomial block inventory. Suppose every old selector input is \(q_i=1-L_i^{p-1}\), where, modulo the weak row-equation span,

\[ L_i=c_i+\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{ij}\sigma_j +\sum_{u=1}^{n+1}b_{iu}X_{u,j_i}. \tag{WC-input} \]

The exceptional column \(j_i\), all coefficients and the global occupancy part may differ in every input, with no common low-dimensional probe bound. Complete accuracy-one families of these inputs over the weak base have no polylog-degree ordinary PC refutation for sufficiently large \(n\). Therefore ordinary unary-PHP Res(Lin) proofs whose equation forms all satisfy (WC-input) have superpolynomially many lines. Ordinary literals are included, so this statement includes the actual initial clauses without an exemption.

Proof. Apply the weak occupancy map globally, leaving source coefficient variables free. Its literal freezing makes the first two terms in (WC-input) constant. The cells in one original column map either to constants or to affine functions of a single residual column: in a copied column they are zero, cells \(Y_{uj}\), or \(1-\sum_uY_{uj}\). Thus each transformed \(L_i\), modulo a residual row combination, is affine in one residual column. Row-equivalence errors have certificates through one, and taking the \((p-1)\)-st power raises that cost only to \(p-1\).

For a polynomial \(q'\) of degree at most \(\delta=p-1\) in one column \(Y_1,\ldots,Y_{N+1}\), the degree-complete column reduction gives

\[ F=q'(0)+\sum_u\bigl(q'(e_u)-q'(0)\bigr)Y_u,\qquad q'-F\in I_\delta({\rm BOOL}+{\rm column\ collisions}). \tag{WC-affine} \]

Indeed, erase monomials using two distinct cells of the column and reduce powers; the remaining affine coefficients are fixed by evaluation at the empty and singly occupied states. This preserves degree in the original polynomial ring, needs no row functionality, and works over every prime. Including the preceding row-equivalence witness, each actual transformed selector has an affine representative \(F_i\) with error certified through \(\delta\).

For the complete block keep its coefficient variables, set \(P_F=1-\sum_i r_iF_i\), and write \(E=P_q-P_F=\sum_i r_i(F_i-q_i)\). Then \(E\) has an old certificate through \(\delta+1\), and

\[ q_iP_q=F_iP_F+(q_i-F_i)P_F+q_iE. \tag{WC-complete} \]

These terms are certified through at most \(\max(3,\delta+2,2\delta+1)=2p-1\). Every fresh field equation is unchanged, and the comparison is simultaneous over all blocks. Thus a source degree \(D\) PC refutation yields one over the residual weak base plus complete old-affine blocks through \(\max(D,2p-1)\). Since \(N=\Theta_p(n)\), the all-prime affine endpoint excludes it. That endpoint may strengthen the residual base to functional PHP after the valid weak-base transformations. The weak-base compiler proves the stated ordinary-CNF consequence.

What changed in the route

The key new interface is the weak-base compiler, not a new freezing construction. It allows weak-only projections to act before functionality is imposed. The application handles spread-out occupancy directions that can collectively have linear dimension, plus a different whole-column perturbation in every query; it is not a disguised logarithmic-probe hypothesis. On the other hand a generic coefficient matrix is not row-constant outside one column, so (WC-input) is not claimed for arbitrary Res(Lin) queries.

The initial alternative was to seek new symmetric-count certificates for empty-pair graphs. The existing statistic map already handles that whole family for the ordinary weak-base route, so no additional graph certificate is needed here. This does not import that map into the functional variant or erase the separate elementary-symmetric obstruction to functional freezing.

Checks and evidence. This cycle reread the complete local compiler rules, the wide-row initial bridge, the freezing map's original-generator identities, column-state division and the affine endpoint. The archived 4,874 base-image checks and column-state certificate controls were reused as existing evidence, not rerun or described as new tests. The new argument is the explicit source composition above; no numerical experiment or Lean verification was performed.

Next obligation. Test what the weak affine freezing maps do to genuinely arbitrary coefficient families before choosing a functional graph. Seek a common projection leaving inputs in an already proved affine/local/profile class or creating a useful dense selector span. Do not replace this obligation with a symmetry assumption or apply functional quotient identities before a weak-only map.

Process assessment. Auditing the weakest base actually used by the compiler recovered a substantial existing normalization tool and avoided developing a redundant restricted-family argument. The unrestricted source exclusion and the functional variant's separate constraints remain open.

21 September 2026 — Scalar rectangle mixing does not supply one layout for all source blocks

Status: working obstruction to a proposed simultaneous rectangle mixer, with an explicit cheap-support alternative for the same family. Arbitrary weak-source normalization remains open. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

General implication under test. The weak compiler makes affine occupancy projections legal before functionality. A possible high case would use many nonzero coefficient rectangles to randomize residual coefficients. The goal, however, needs one map for the whole source family, not a favorable map for each input space. Test the implication “large minimum rectangle packing in every block supplies one common disjoint-column switch layout.” The counterfamily below refutes it even at linear nonlinear selector rank. It is nevertheless easy after a support-aware matching restriction, so this is a failure of that proposed classifier, not a hard source counterexample.

What the affine copy map measures

Write an old form as \(F=c+\sum_{ij}A_{ij}X_{ij}\). For a fixed \(K\)-copy weak freezing map, label the copied original rows \(r_{a,i}\), copied columns \(s_{a,j}\), and distinguished row \(*\). Its residual coefficient matrix is

\[ B_{ij}=\sum_{a=1}^{K} \left(A_{r_{a,i},s_{a,j}}-A_{*,s_{a,j}}\right). \tag{FR-coeff} \]

The remaining terms contribute a constant. In particular row-plus-column matrices \(A_{ij}=u_i+v_j\) become row-constant coefficient matrices, hence constants modulo residual row equations. This recovers the already used occupancy/row directions; it is not a statement that every other form has high residual rank.

A column-pair swap within one copy reverses the contribution of the corresponding anchored rectangle

\[ \Delta(A;r,*,j,k)=A_{rj}-A_{*j}-A_{rk}+A_{*k} \]

to a residual row's column difference. If the switched pairs are disjoint, their signs can be chosen independently. For a scalar sum \(Z=c+\sum_{\ell=1}^q\varepsilon_\ell d_\ell\) over an odd prime field, with independent uniform signs, let \(b\) be the number of nonzero \(d_\ell\). Fourier inversion gives

\[ \left|\Pr(Z=a)-\frac1p\right| \le \frac{p-1}{p}\left(\cos\frac{\pi}{p}\right)^b . \tag{FR-scalar} \]

Indeed every nontrivial character has expectation \(\prod_\ell\cos(2\pi t d_\ell/p)\), whose absolute value is at most the displayed power; average the \(p-1\) nontrivial characters. Thus scalar bias can be small. At a fixed prime the relevant target is closeness to \(1/p\), not a real-valued point-probability bound tending to zero.

Linear-rank source spaces with no common column-disjoint layout

For sufficiently large \(n\), set \(h=\lfloor n/10\rfloor\). There is a linear code \(V\subseteq\mathbb F_3^n\) of dimension \(h\) in which every nonzero vector has at least \(n/3\) nonzero coordinates. To see this, choose an \(n\times h\) generator matrix \(C\) uniformly. For fixed nonzero \(a\), the weight \(W\) of \(Ca\) has independent coordinates with nonzero probability \(2/3\), so

\[ \Pr(W<n/3)\le 2^{n/3}\mathbb E\,2^{-W} =2^{n/3}(2/3)^n. \]

Union over fewer than \(3^h\) nonzero \(a\) gives a bound tending to zero, since \(\ln3/10+\ln2/3+\ln(2/3)<0\). Positive minimum weight also proves full generator rank.

Use the first \(n\) rows of an \((n+1)\)-by-\(n\) board. For \(a=0,\ldots,n-1\), let \(\pi_a(i)=i+a\pmod n\), and let \(U_a\) consist of matrices supported on the permutation cells \((i,\pi_a(i))\), with weights from \(V\). The extra row is zero. The \(n\) supports partition the \(n\times n\) square.

Every nonzero matrix in every \(U_a\) has at least \(\lfloor n/12\rfloor\) vertex-disjoint nonzero rectangles. Of its at least \(n/3\) nonzero weights, at least \(n/6\) have the same value in \(\{1,2\}\). Pair those entries. The rectangle on each pair of their rows and permutation columns has difference \(2v\ne0\), since its cross entries vanish. These rectangles are mutually row- and column-disjoint.

Now let \(\mathcal R\) be any collection of coefficient-rectangle tests whose column pairs are disjoint. It may use a shared anchor row, as the actual freezing switches do. Then \(|\mathcal R|\le\lfloor n/2\rfloor\). Each rectangle touches only four cells, hence its evaluation functional can be nonzero on at most four of the spaces \(U_a\). If \(E_a:U_a\to\mathbb F_3^{\mathcal R}\) is the resulting test map,

\[ \sum_{a=0}^{n-1}\operatorname{rank}E_a \le \sum_a\#\{\text{tests nonzero on }U_a\} \le4|\mathcal R|\le2n. \tag{FR-capacity} \]

For \(h>2\), not all \(E_a\) can be injective. Some nonzero form combination in some block is invisible to every test in the common layout, despite its linear-size individual rectangle packing. Even selecting arbitrary three-dimensional subspaces in all blocks cannot make these maps simultaneously injective. This refutes the proposed common-code deduction. It does not exclude overlapping-column switches, other random maps, or a different complete source normalization.

The selector rank is genuinely linear before restriction

In block \(a\), take the \(h\) actual forms supplied by the columns of \(C\), supported on its permutation cells, and use selectors \(q_\ell=1-F_\ell^2\). Their nonlinear rank modulo functional old \(I_2\) and arbitrary affine polynomials is exactly \(h\), for large \(n\).

For a putative nonzero coefficient vector \(d\), the quadratic matrix of \(\sum_\ell d_\ell F_\ell^2\) in the permutation-cell variables is \(C\operatorname{diag}(d)C^{\mathsf T}\). If all its off-diagonal entries vanished, this would be a diagonal matrix. Every column belongs to the code \(V\), which contains no nonzero coordinate vector, so the diagonal matrix must be zero. A left inverse for \(C\) then forces \(\operatorname{diag}(d)=0\), a contradiction. Thus some cross term on two permutation cells survives Boolean reduction.

The degree-two signed detector in the proof of sparse-cell matching separation, with maximum row support one and \(n\ge5\), detects a highest-degree term of this polynomial. Its two selected rows also make it annihilate every affine polynomial, including ones outside the permutation support. It annihilates old \(I_2\). Therefore no such nonzero combination can become affine modulo \(I_2\). Independence modulo the stronger functional base also implies independence modulo the weak base. This is not merely an affine-form rank example.

The same whole family has a cheap common matching restriction

Take a uniform matching restriction leaving \(m=\lfloor n^{1/4}\rfloor\) holes and \(m+1\) rows. The live row and column sets are independent uniform subsets: for every such pair the number of matching bijections on their complements is the same. For a fixed permutation support \(F_a\), the probability that at least \(k\) of its cells survive is at most

\[ \binom nk\frac{(m+1)_k}{(n+1)_k} \frac{(m)_k}{(n)_k} \le n^{-k/2+o(1)} \quad\text{for fixed }k. \tag{FR-survival} \]

The cells of \(F_a\) have distinct rows and columns, so the witness-subset union bound gives the first expression exactly at the required marginals. At \(k=4\), union over all \(n\) blocks tends to zero. Some single restriction leaves at most three old cells in every block's common support; all its other form contributions are constants.

For each block evaluate its inputs on the at most eight Boolean assignments to those cells. At a state with a nonzero input choose its first such input and set its coefficient to the inverse value; at a common zero set all coefficients to zero. Boolean state interpolation gives old-only coefficient polynomials of degree at most three. Every actual companion image is Boolean-zero, certified through at most \(b+4\) when \(b\le3\) cells survive, and every coefficient-field image through \(3b\). For \(T=\max(1,b)\), these fit \(5T\) and \(3T\), the respective original degrees scaled by \(T\). Constant-state blocks use constant coefficients.

Apply these complete maps simultaneously. A polylog-degree source PC proof would become an old weak PHP proof through at most three times that degree, while the residual board has \(m=n^{1/4+o(1)}\) holes. The old linear lower bound excludes it. Functionality is not needed for these coefficient maps, because the residual dependence is on at most three individual Boolean cells. Thus the counterfamily is a proved easy family under a different common map. The failed rectangle classifier must recognize this support geometry rather than call it a hard residual.

Literature screen, exact checks and route assessment

The array inverse Littlewood–Offord theorem of Do, Nguyen, Phan, Tran and Vu concerns real-valued arrays; the Berger–Berkowitz–Devlin–Vu permutation paper likewise states real-valued hypotheses. Neither was imported as a fixed-prime rank theorem. The elementary modular formula (FR-scalar) is proved above. A separate encoding sanity check found Atserias–Bonet–Esteban's exponential weak-PHP bounds for Res(2); the ability of ternary equations to express two-literal conjunctions supplies no automatic easy PHP proof or transfer of those lower bounds to unrestricted Res(Lin).

The exact checker used \(n=30,h=3\), taking the 27 vectors of \(\mathbb F_3^3\) as code rows and padding zero rows. Its nonzero codewords have minimum weight eighteen, supplying four equal-value disjoint rectangles; the off-diagonal square map has rank three. A coordinate-basis control has off-diagonal rank zero. All 202,275 coefficient rectangles met the four-block incidence bound. Ten signed-sum counts over \(\mathbb F_3\) met the exact bound \(|3c_a-2^b|\le2\); counting zero differences as active fails, and signs do not randomize in characteristic two. Complete checked counts and the adjacent C++ source are retained. These finite tests do not claim a universal rank condenser.

Assessment of this line. Scalar anticoncentration is available but the common-layout step is false under the proposed minimum-packing hypothesis. Building a more elaborate one-block mixer would leave that obstruction untouched. A promising necessary alternative is to detect low-dimensional state profiles after sparse support restrictions; the same family demonstrates this concretely. The next bounded task is a uniform complete map for polynomially many blocks with subquadratic shared cell supports, followed by an explicit check of which base assumptions it uses. The unrestricted dense-support case and compatibility with weak-only maps remain open.

Process assessment. A family-level capacity count stopped a per-block construction before it became an unsupported global claim. Source reading and exploratory interpretation overlapped before the final mathematics marker; no old verification suite or formalization was rerun.

Subquadratic shared cell supports admit one common constant-degree profile restriction

21 September 2026 · Working proof with exact finite combinatorial controls · Route: odd-prime-clause-transfer. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question and stopping point. The unrestricted goal requires removal of arbitrary complete selector blocks, not just high scalar mixing rank. The preceding common-layout obstruction is easy because its supports become small. Test the general sufficient condition that polynomially many blocks have subquadratic shared cell supports. Stop after a uniform restriction bound, full original-generator degree ledger and falsifying controls; the user requested a pause after this cycle.

Working theorem: shared sparse supports

Fix a prime \(p\), constants \(c,A\ge0\) and \(0<\varepsilon\le2\). On the balanced Boolean unary board with \(n+1\) rows and \(n\) holes, take at most \(n^c\) complete, one-level, old-input ENS blocks. Block \(a\) has arbitrary finitely many polynomial inputs \(g_{ai}(X)\), a positive integer accuracy \(h_a\), fresh coefficient variables, all their field equations, and all companions \[ g_{ai}P_a,\qquad P_a=\prod_{\ell=1}^{h_a}\left(1-\sum_i r_{ai\ell}g_{ai}\right). \] Assume that one cell set \(F_a\), of size at most \(n^{2-\varepsilon}(\log n)^A\), contains the variables of every input in that block. There are no inputs depending on other blocks' coefficients. Then the augmented weak or functional linear-row PHP system has no polylogarithmic-degree PC refutation for large \(n\). This also excludes NS refutations in that degree range. This is a common-support hypothesis within each block, not a bound on each input separately.

Consequently, for \(0<\varepsilon\le1\), ordinary unary PHP requires superpolynomially many proof lines in the subclass of standard syntactic \(\mathrm{Res}(\mathrm{Lin}_{\mathbb F_p})\) whose every clause has a union of cell supports of size at most \(n^{2-\varepsilon}(\log n)^A\). The same scoped conclusion holds for the functional variant. Apply the weak or functional compiler: selectors preserve the clause's common support, input-basis compression introduces no new variables, and degree is \(4p-1\). For any proposed polynomial line bound, the source block inventory is polynomial. The restriction \(\varepsilon\le1\) allows the ordinary pigeon initial clauses, whose support has \(n\) cells.

One restriction controls every support

Choose \(0<\beta<\min(1,\varepsilon/2)\), and set \(N=\lceil n^\beta\rceil\). Choose a uniform partial matching fixing \(n-N\) pigeons to distinct holes; retain \(N+1\) rows and \(N\) columns. The survivor sets are independent uniform subsets: every pair admits exactly \((n-N)!\) matched complements. Set matched cells to one, conflicting cells to zero, and keep only the survivor board. The weak and functional bases restrict to their corresponding residual bases. No substitution increases degree.

A fixed \(k\)-edge matching in \(F_a\) survives with probability \[ \frac{(N+1)_k}{(n+1)_k}\frac{(N)_k}{(n)_k}. \] There are at most \(|F_a|^k/k!\) such matchings. Thus for each fixed \(k\), \[ \Pr[\nu(F_a')\ge k]\le \frac{|F_a|^k}{k!}\frac{(N+1)_k}{(n+1)_k}\frac{(N)_k}{(n)_k} \le n^{-k(\varepsilon-2\beta)+o(1)}. \] Choose \(k\) with \(k(\varepsilon-2\beta)>c+1\). A union bound gives one restriction with \(\nu(F_a')<k\) simultaneously for all blocks. The endpoints of any maximal matching cover every edge; hence each residual support has a row/column vertex cover of size \(s_a\le2(k-1)\). This does not assert a constant number of residual cells: a row star can retain \(N\) edges and still have a one-vertex cover.

Complete local states and bounded certificates

Now strengthen the residual weak base to the functional base if necessary; a weak refutation would remain a refutation after strengthening. Write \(I_d\) for combinations of these original residual generators with every product of degree at most \(d\). For one support cover, query its \(r\) rows and \(t\) columns, \(s=r+t\). A queried row has states \(X_{ij}\), summed over all residual columns. A queried column has states \(X_{ij}\), summed over residual rows, together with the empty state \(u_j=1-\sum_iX_{ij}\). Row states sum to one modulo the row equation; column states sum to one literally. Empty states must be retained: no onto axiom is used.

Take products \(\pi_\tau\) of one indicator per queried vertex. They have degree at most \(s\). Discard locally inconsistent choices. Two occupied states disagreeing on a row or column contain a collision factor. An occupied cell and the empty state of its column vanish through degree two because \[ X_{ij}u_j=-(X_{ij}^2-X_{ij})-\sum_{b\ne i}X_{ij}X_{bj}. \] Multiplying by the other \(s-2\) factors gives a certificate through \(s\). These cases cover every inconsistent partial assignment. Expanding the full product of the state sums and telescoping the queried row equations consequently gives \[ 1-\sum_{\tau\ {\rm consistent}}\pi_\tau\in I_s. \] Consistency here is local matching consistency, not a complete PHP model.

Every supported cell touches a queried vertex, so a consistent state determines its Boolean value. For such a cell \(e\), the identity \(\pi_\tau(X_e-\tau(e))\in I_{s+1}\) follows from its chosen occupied indicator and Booleanity/collision, or from the displayed empty-state identity. Telescope a monomial one variable at a time, including repetitions, and extend linearly. For every polynomial \(g\) on this support, \[ \pi_\tau(g-g(\tau))\in I_{s+\deg g}. \] These are original-generator certificates; pointwise vanishing alone is not being used for the PHP relations. The construction combines the row-state first-success argument with empty-aware column states.

All companions, accuracies and coefficient fields

On each consistent state choose the first nonzero input, assign its coefficient the inverse of its value in \(\mathbb F_p\), and assign the other coefficients zero. If all inputs vanish, assign them all zero. Interpolate these values: \(\beta_i=\sum_\tau b_{i\tau}\pi_\tau\). Replace \(r_{i1}\) by \(\beta_i\) and every \(r_{i\ell}\), \(\ell>1\), by zero. The image of the actual product is therefore \(P'=1-\sum_i\beta_i g_i\), independent of the accuracy. Let \(V=\sum_{\tau:\,g_j(\tau)=0\ \forall j}\pi_\tau\), and let \(\delta_i,\delta\) denote the original pre-restriction input degrees and their maximum. The preceding identities give \[ P'-V\in I_{s+\delta},\qquad g_iV\in I_{s+\delta_i}, \qquad g_iP'\in I_{s+\delta+\delta_i}. \] For example, the first identity is the partition identity minus \(\sum_{\tau,i}b_{i\tau}\pi_\tau(g_i-g_i(\tau))\); the second uses that every summand state has \(g_i(\tau)=0\). Restriction may lower degrees, so using the original degrees is safe.

The coefficients \(\beta_i\) need only be field-valued, not Boolean. Their coefficients lie in \(\mathbb F_p\), so \(\beta_i^p-\beta_i\) vanishes on every Boolean assignment, even assignments violating PHP. Its multilinear reduction is identically zero; ordinary variable-by-variable Boolean reduction is an original Boolean-axiom certificate through degree at most \(ps\). The zero higher-accuracy coefficients have trivial fields.

Take \(T=\max(1,\max_a s_a)\), a constant. For a nonzero input, its original companion has degree \(e_i=h_a(\delta+1)+\delta_i\). Hence \[ s_a+\delta+\delta_i\le T e_i,\qquad ps_a\le Tp. \] Empty supports admit constant coefficient choices and exact zero companions; identically zero input companions are trivial. Every old variable has substitution degree one. Thus every substituted original generator has an old certificate within \(T\) times its original degree. Applying the substitution to all blocks at once replays any PC proof of degree \(D\) as an old functional PC proof of degree at most \(TD\): substitute addition lines linearly, simulate multiplication by a degree-\(\le T\) polynomial term by term, and initialize generators from the certificates. NS replay has the same bound. There is no multiplicative factor for the number of blocks or states.

Since \(\log n=\Theta(\log N)\), a polylogarithmic \(D\) gives \(TD=o(N)\). The all-field old matching barrier excludes this residual refutation (choose an old cutoff \(B\ge TD\) with \(N\ge2B-1\)). This proves the source statement.

Checks, scope and assessment

The new exact integer check enumerates all \(7{,}056\) pairs of four surviving rows and three surviving columns on a nine-by-eight board for each of six fixtures. It checks the matching union bound for \(k=1,2,3\), and that greedy maximal-matching endpoints cover every live edge with at most twice the maximum matching number. The fixtures are row/column stars, a matching, a four-by-four block, their two-star union, and the complete board. The row star retains three cells with matching number one; the complete board has matching number three in every restriction, a negative control against unsupported universal thinning. All checks passed. These finite graph checks test the combinatorial implementation, not the general algebraic proof. C++20 checker; complete counts. Build with g++ -std=c++20 -O2 check.cpp -o checker and run checker --out checks.json, both through the protected launcher.

Focused review. The row-star control prevents confusing cell count with vertex-cover size; column emptiness avoids an illicit onto axiom; the full source ledger retains arbitrary accuracy and every field equation. The result extends the preceding permutation-support example and complements the per-input row-remainder theorem, whose support unions may be dense. It needs functionality for the row states after restriction. It does not justify placing this profile before a subsequent weak-only occupancy-freezing map, and does not handle raw earlier coefficient inputs.

Contribution and remaining implication. This supplies a uniform complete-source endpoint for subquadratic shared supports, for every fixed prime. It does not show that arbitrary dense-support blocks become sparse or enter the dense-head endpoint on one compatible restriction. That necessary implication remains open; the positive result narrows the unresolved geometry without proving the unrestricted lower bound. Novelty has not been checked independently. Spin is paused after this checkpoint; a future resumed cycle should test that common sparse-profile/dense-image implication against the existing two-size dense control and common-layout obstruction before refining another scalar criterion.

Process assessment. The matching-number tail and mixed vertex profiles abstract the previous easy counterfamily without another growing case list; the existing bounded retrieval and exact checker suffice, so no framework change is warranted.

Polynomially many global probes can share a sparse-support profile without new blocks

21 September 2026 · Working all-prime source extension and finite local controls · Route: odd-prime-clause-transfer. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, high reasoning).

Missing implication and stopping point. Arbitrary dense selector blocks are not known to enter a cheap profile or dense-image case after one common restriction. Test whether dense dependence on a limited number of global quantities can coexist with the shared-support endpoint without the exponential auxiliary inventory of the earlier probe-branch construction. Stop at the exact degree ledger and a control against interpreting the resulting decomposition as a general classification. Spin resumed at the user's request; initial commentary and resume part one preceded this cycle's clock.

Working extension and its quantitative scope

Fix \(p,c,A\), \(0<\varepsilon\le2\) and \(0\le\alpha<\min(1,\varepsilon/2)\). Suppose at most \(n^c\) complete one-level old-input ENS blocks over weak or functional balanced unary PHP have the following supplied representations. In each block \(a\), choose \(t_a\le n^\alpha(\log n)^A\) old affine probes \(U_{aj}\) and a common cell set \(|F_a|\le n^{2-\varepsilon}(\log n)^A\). For every nonzero input \(g_{ai}\) of original degree \(\delta_{ai}\), require a polynomial \(G_{ai}(z,x)\) of degree at most \(\delta_{ai}\) with \[ g_{ai}-G_{ai}(U_a,X_{F_a})\in I_{\delta_{ai}}. \] The certificate is in the original old base, before any weak-only transformation. Then these complete blocks, at arbitrary positive accuracies and with every coefficient field equation retained, admit no polylog-degree PC or NS refutation for sufficiently large \(n\). The degree-preserving representation is a hypothesis, not an inference from semantic equality.

In particular, it applies to selectors \(g_i=1-L_i^{p-1}\) whenever each block's affine forms are, modulo row equations, linear combinations of its \(t_a\) probes plus forms on \(F_a\). Factoring differences of powers gives the required degree-\((p-1)\) certificates. Constant selectors are handled directly. Through the existing ordinary and functional compilers this yields corresponding restricted superpolynomial proof-line lower bounds; as before, \(\varepsilon\le1\) admits the wide ordinary pigeon initials.

Mixed finite-field and matching states

Choose \(\alpha<\beta<\min(1,\varepsilon/2)\) and leave \(N=\lceil n^\beta\rceil\) holes. The preceding matching-tail proof, applied only to the original \(n^c\) sets \(F_a\), supplies one restriction with constant-size row/column covers for every residual support. Neither probe values nor their number enter that union bound. The original certificates restrict without increasing degree. Strengthen a weak residual base to the functional base now if required.

For a block, let \(s\) be its cover size and \(\pi_\tau\) the consistent partial matching-state products from that proof. They have degree at most \(s\), partition one modulo \(I_s\), and satisfy \(\pi_\tau(H-H(\tau))\in I_{s+\deg H}\) for supported-cell polynomials. For \(u\in\mathbb F_p^t\), set \[ \chi_u=\prod_{j=1}^t\bigl(1-(U_j-u_j)^{p-1}\bigr),\qquad \theta_{u,\tau}=\chi_u\pi_\tau,\qquad d=(p-1)t+s. \] The field indicators sum to one literally, so \(1-\sum_{u,\tau}\theta_{u,\tau}\in I_d\). The probes may be dependent or conflict with a local state; no affine fiber equation is imposed and no satisfiability assertion is made about every combined state.

For the supplied representation, first replace supported cells by their values in \(\tau\), using the local state certificates term by term. Then replace probes by \(u\): \(\chi_u(H(U)-H(u))\) vanishes on the entire Boolean cube, and Boolean division certifies it through degree \((p-1)t+\deg H\). Multiplication by \(\pi_\tau\) adds at most \(s\). Including the supplied old certificate gives \[ \theta_{u,\tau}\bigl(g_i-G_i(u,\tau)\bigr)\in I_{d+\delta_i}. \] This argument keeps the original generators and handles inconsistent probe/local value combinations automatically.

Complete elimination and degree costs

On each combined state, select the first nonzero value \(G_i(u,\tau)\), set its coefficient to its inverse and all others to zero; if every value is zero, use all zeros. Let \(\beta_i\) be the interpolation of these coefficients against \(\theta_{u,\tau}\). Set the first accuracy row to \(\beta_i\), and every later accuracy row to zero. Thus \(\deg\beta_i\le d\), and the actual product image is \(P'=1-\sum_i\beta_i g_i\). Let \(V\) sum the combined-state indicators on which every input value is zero, and \(\delta=\max_i\delta_i\). Exactly as in the shared-support proof, \[ P'-V\in I_{d+\delta},\quad g_iV\in I_{d+\delta_i},\quad g_iP'\in I_{d+\delta+\delta_i}. \] Also \(\beta_i^p-\beta_i\) has a Boolean certificate through \(pd\), since its coefficients lie in \(\mathbb F_p\). It need not be a Boolean-valued polynomial.

With \(T=\max(1,\max_a d_a)\), every original companion of degree \(e_i=h_a(\delta+1)+\delta_i\) has image certificate through \(Te_i\), every coefficient field fits \(Tp\), and every substitution has degree at most \(T\). Constant/zero-input blocks use their direct scalar maps. One simultaneous substitution replays PC or NS degree \(D\) through \(TD\). Here \(T=O(n^\alpha\operatorname{polylog}n)\), so \(TD=o(n^\beta)=o(N)\) for polylogarithmic \(D\), contradicting the old matching-filtration barrier.

There can be exponentially many combined states and many monomials in \(\beta_i\). This is allowed in a degree lower-bound argument: no proof-size bound for the substituted proof is asserted. Crucially, there are no new ENS blocks, hence no exponential inventory in the restriction estimate. The earlier logarithmic-probe theorem used auxiliary branch blocks because its local supports could differ across inputs without a common cover. The present result is a stronger probe allowance under a different, shared-support hypothesis, not an unrestricted replacement for that theorem.

What the algebraic decomposition does and does not establish

For affine input forms, the literal coefficient matrix restricted to cells outside \(F_a\) has rank at most \(t_a\) exactly when such a probe-plus-supported-remainder decomposition exists: choose a basis for those restricted rows, lift its forms as probes, and subtract their linear combinations. One may first apply supplied row-equation rewrites, whose certificates are degree one. Thus the new result handles dense support that becomes low rank after deleting a shared set of cells, without counting all probe fibers as separate blocks.

This rank condition cannot be a complete hardness invariant. Take all cell forms \(L_{ij}=X_{ij}\) in one tuple. Their coefficient matrix is the identity on \(n(n+1)\) cells; after deleting \(o(n^2)\) columns its rank is still \(\Theta(n^2)\). Yet every selector \(1-X_{ij}^{p-1}\) equals the affine input \(1-X_{ij}\) modulo Booleanity with certificate degree \(p-1\). The affine source endpoint already handles the complete tuple. Hence failure of the decomposition does not imply dense nonlinear selector rank. The two-size dense family and common-layout obstruction remain essential tests for any proposed family-wide alternative; no new implication for their arbitrary dense analogues is proved here.

Verification, significance and next direction

A new exact check uses a satisfiable two-row, three-hole board over \(p=3,5\), with the first row and first column queried and a dense affine probe. Its inputs are one selector and one affine polynomial. For each field it checks both coefficient fields on all 64 Boolean assignments and both companions on all six full matching assignments. Omitting the column-empty state causes two matching-model failures in each field. The coefficient maps take non-Boolean values in 20 and 32 of the tested coefficient evaluations, rejecting an incorrect Boolean coefficient-domain assumption. These are finite semantic controls, not a verification of the general original-generator certificate proof. C++20 checker; complete output. Build with g++ -std=c++20 -O2 check.cpp -o checker; run checker --out checks.json, using the protected launcher.

Focused review and contribution. Checked the exact partition, degree-preserving representation requirement, original companion costs, all field equations, accuracy cleanup and absence of a new-block inventory. This extends the complete sparse-profile endpoint to polynomially many dense probes. It advances that sufficient case; the necessary implication that arbitrary clauses admit some affordable complete removal remains unproved. The identity-matrix control prevents promoting coefficient deletion rank into a solution. Independent novelty has not been checked.

Next cycle. At the user's explicit encouragement, spend one bounded cycle developing a geometric object for the remaining problem. It should retain the degree filtration and the original-generator presentation, distinguish semantic emptiness from low-degree inconsistency, and offer an invariant that can be tested on both affine-cell and dense-selector controls. Developing the object itself is authorized even before an applicable deep theorem is identified.

Process assessment. Reusing the two existing interpolation proofs exposed an avoidable exponential auxiliary inventory; the new direct proof resolves that cost without new framework machinery or a repeated numerical sweep.
