A marked fat point records proof degree; relative torsion, not ordinary flatness, is the geometric target

21 September 2026 · Working algebraic reformulation, exact controls and bounded geometry exploration · No new unrestricted lower bound. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, high reasoning).

Assignment and stopping point. At the user's encouragement, spend one cycle developing a nontrivial geometric object even without a preselected applicable theorem. The missing implication remains complete-source removal for arbitrary dense selector tuples. Stop after an exact bridge from the object to original-generator NS and PC degree, controls against misleading invariants, and a specific useful or obstructed geometric direction. This is not a claim that naming the obstruction solves it.

The object: retain the homogeneous presentation and mark the unit direction

For a finite list \(\mathcal F\subset k[y_1,\ldots,y_v]\), omit zero generators and let \(S=k[z,y_1,\ldots,y_v]\). Homogenize each supplied generator to its actual degree, set \[ J=(f^{\mathrm h}:f\in\mathcal F),\qquad A=S/J, \] and retain the marked class \(z\in A_1\). Do not saturate \(J\), and do not replace it by the homogenization of the final affine ideal. The linear isomorphism \(h_D:k[y]_{\le D}\to S_D\), \(h_D(f)=z^{D-\deg f}f^{\mathrm h}\), identifies the original-generator NS space \(I_D\) with \(J_D\). Indeed homogenize each cofactor product to degree \(D\); conversely dehomogenize a homogeneous representation. Hence \(1\in I_D\) precisely when \(z^D=0\) in \(A_D\). This is a reuse of historical homogenized separation, now including every old and fresh source variable.

For the full unsatisfiable Boolean PHP source, \(A\) is a finite graded local algebra supported at the origin, provided no constant contradiction is already a generator. Some power \(z^L\) lies in the homogeneous old ideal: the Boolean quotient is a product of field copies indexed by cube assignments, and the unsatisfiable old equations generate its unit ideal. Boolean homogenizations give \(y_i^2=zy_i\) for old cells. Fresh coefficient fields give \(r^p=z^{p-1}r\). Consequently every variable is nilpotent once \(z\) is, and \(A\) is finite-dimensional with radical the homogeneous maximal ideal. Thus \(\operatorname{Spec}A\) is a potentially large nonreduced point, while \(\operatorname{Proj}A\) and the \(z\ne0\) chart are empty. Projectivizing or saturating would erase the degree information. This presentation algebra is not the Rees algebra of the zero final affine quotient.

The cyclic module \(k[z]\cdot1\) has length equal to the first NS refutation degree. More generally the graded \(k[z]\)-module records the lengths and starting degrees of multiplication-by-\(z\) strings. This is the Jordan degree type studied by Altafi, Iarrobino and Macias Marques; their Definition 2.8 and Proposition 2.19 relate it to ranks of graded multiplication maps. We use that vocabulary and elementary linear algebra, not a Lefschetz theorem or a characteristic-zero result. The earlier dismissal of schemes based only on finite reduced Boolean solution sets misses this nonreduced presentation object.

Exact PC closure and a socle criterion

Working proposition. For any field, supplied polynomial system and \(D\ge1\), define subspaces of \(S_D\) by \[ W_0=J_D,\qquad W_{j+1}=W_j+\sum_{i=1}^v y_i(W_j:z),\qquad (W_j:z)=\{u\in S_{D-1}:zu\in W_j\}. \] This increasing sequence stabilizes after at most \(\dim S_D\) strict enlargements, and \[ W_\infty=h_D(C_D). \] Here \(C_D\) is the ordinary syntactic PC consequence space at ceiling \(D\), with all variables and original generators retained.

Proof. Every bounded NS representation can be derived by PC using its original generators and monomial multipliers, so \(J_D\subseteq h_D(C_D)\). A polynomial \(f\) of degree at most \(D\) has actual degree at most \(D-1\) exactly when \(h_D(f)\) is divisible by \(z\). For such \(f\), \(h_D(y_if)=y_i h_D(f)/z\). Thus the displayed operation is exactly a legal multiplication step, including multiplication after a cancellation has lowered actual degree. Addition is linear span. Induction gives both inclusions, and finite-dimensional stabilization proves the assertion. In particular a PC refutation exists at ceiling \(D\) precisely when \(z^D\in W_\infty\). No ordinary ideal saturation is substituted for this fixed-degree operation.

Let \[ T_D=\ker(z:A_{D-1}\longrightarrow A_D). \] Then \[ C_D=I_D \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad y_iT_D=0\text{ in }A_D\text{ for every }i \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad T_D\subseteq\operatorname{Soc}(A)_{D-1}. \] Here \(\operatorname{Soc}(A)\) means elements annihilated by every positive-degree generator; the statement also makes sense when \(A\) is not Artinian. To prove it, \(C_D=I_D\) is equivalent to \(W_0\) already being closed. The quotient of \((J_D:z)\) by \(J_{D-1}\) is exactly \(T_D\), and closure asks that every \(y_i\) annihilate this quotient in degree \(D\). Multiplication by \(z\) already vanishes there. This proves the equivalences.

The familiar no-fall criterion is the stronger sufficient condition \(T_D=0\); the exact condition permits torsion that all variables annihilate. For example \(\mathcal F=\{x^2-x,x^2\}\) has \(J=(x^2,xz)\). At \(D=2\), \(T_2=kx\ne0\), but \(xT_2=zT_2=0\), so PC and NS spaces agree despite a degree fall. This is an exact algebraic reformulation of truncated closure, not an independent lower-bound theorem.

A flat family with constant Hilbert function still has different marked NS degrees

Over any field take \[ \mathcal F_\lambda=\{x^2-x,\ x^2+(\lambda-1)x-1\},\qquad \lambda\ne1. \] Both supplied generators have degree two for every parameter. Their homogeneous ideal is \(J_\lambda=(x^2-xz,\lambda xz-z^2)\). The quotient has basis \(1,x,z,xz\), with \(x^2=xz,\ z^2=\lambda xz\), and all cubics zero. Indeed \(x^2z=xz^2=\lambda x^2z\), and \(1-\lambda\) is invertible; the remaining cubic monomials then vanish. The independent two degree-two relations leave \(xz\ne0\). These reductions work over \(k[\lambda,(1-\lambda)^{-1}]\), where the four displayed classes are a free basis. This is a flat parameter family with Hilbert function \((1,2,1)\).

At \(\lambda=0\), \(z^2=0\), so the NS refutation degree is two. At \(\lambda\ne0,1\), \(z^2=\lambda xz\ne0\) but \(z^3=0\), so it is three. Multiplication by \(z\) has Jordan type \((2,2)\) in the first case and \((3,1)\) in the second. Hilbert function alone misses even this difference. Nevertheless the PC refutation degree is two throughout: let \(q=f_2-f_1=\lambda x-1\); derive \(xq-\lambda f_1=(\lambda-1)x\), then \[ \frac{\lambda}{\lambda-1}(xq-\lambda f_1)-q=1. \] Every line has degree at most two. In the homogeneous algebra, \(\lambda x-z\in T_2\) and its product with \(x\) equals \((\lambda-1)xz\ne0\), exactly the active torsion detected by the proposition. Thus a long marked \(z\)-string alone is an NS statement, not a PC lower bound. At the excluded parameter \(\lambda=1\), \(x=1\) is a model; this is a satisfiable negative control.

The PHP-relative algebra and its tensor factors

Let \(A_0\) be the old homogenized PHP algebra. Adjoining only the fresh fields gives \[ B=A_0[r_1,\ldots,r_R]/(r_j^p-z^{p-1}r_j)_j. \] Monic division makes this a free \(A_0\)-module with basis \(r^\alpha\), \(0\le\alpha_j<p\), with graded shifts \(|\alpha|\). Therefore the old stable-filtration range gives injectivity of \(z:B_{d-1}\to B_d\) through the same ceiling: each coefficient is an old degree-shifted piece. Let \(K\) be the ideal of homogeneous companion images, so the complete source algebra is \(A=B/K\). In that range, \[ \ker(z:A_{D-1}\to A_D) \simeq\frac{K_D\cap zB_{D-1}}{zK_{D-1}}. \] The map sends a class of \(u\), for which \(zu\in K_D\), to \(zu\bmod zK_{D-1}\). Surjectivity is immediate; injectivity uses the stated injectivity of \(z\) on \(B_{D-1}\). This locates the newly created torsion in the companion ideal and keeps every coefficient field.

For a block with input degrees \(\delta_i\), maximum \(\delta\), and accuracy \(h\), its actual homogeneous companions are \(g_i^{\mathrm h}H\), where \[ H=\prod_{\ell=1}^h\left(z^{\delta+1} -\sum_jr_{j\ell}z^{\delta-\delta_j}g_j^{\mathrm h}\right). \] These formulas preserve the original joint degrees. With one-level old inputs and disjoint coefficient variables, the whole algebra is exactly the tensor product over \(A_0\) of the individual complete block algebras. Thus interactions of nonflat tensor factors are a real algebraic object here, not merely an analogy with a family of solution sets.

Ordinary flatness is unavailable for nontrivial positive-degree blocks

Working observation. Suppose a block has only positive-degree old inputs (zero and unit inputs have been cleaned up). Write its field-only extension as \(B_a\) and its companion ideal as \(K_a\), and suppose \(K_a\ne0\) in \(B_a\). With \(\mathfrak m=(A_0)_{>0}\), every homogeneous companion contains \(g_i^{\mathrm h}\in\mathfrak m\), so \(K_a\subseteq\mathfrak mB_a\). The algebra \(B_a/K_a\) is not flat over \(A_0\).

Proof. \(B_a\) is a finite free module over the finite local algebra \(A_0\). If \(B_a/K_a\) were flat, tensoring \(0\to K_a\to B_a\to B_a/K_a\to0\) with \(k=A_0/\mathfrak m\) would leave an injection \(K_a/\mathfrak mK_a\to B_a/\mathfrak mB_a\). This map is zero because \(K_a\subseteq\mathfrak mB_a\). Hence \(K_a=\mathfrak mK_a\); iterating the nilpotent maximal ideal gives \(K_a=0\), a contradiction. Equivalently the usual Tor exact sequence gives \[ \operatorname{Tor}^{A_0}_1(k,B_a/K_a)\simeq K_a/\mathfrak mK_a\ne0. \] This uses only the elementary exactness meaning of flatness and the nilpotent case of Nakayama's lemma; no deep flatness theorem is imported. The qualification \(K_a\ne0\) is essential.

This rules out a tempting sufficient endpoint—prove that all block extensions are flat and tensor them—before investing in it. Derived tensor products and graded Tor remain possible tools for organizing the failures, but merely observing nonzero Tor proves neither hardness nor easiness. The relevant issue is its degree and how multiplication reaches the old unit.

A complete affine ENS block already has harmless active torsion

Over \(\mathbb F_3\), on the satisfiable Boolean base \(x^2-x\), take the complete accuracy-one block with input \(g=x\), companion \(c=x(1-rx)\) and field \(r^3-r\). Put \(b=x^2-x\). Then \(q=c+rb=x(1-r)\in I_3\), and PC derives \(rq=rx-r^2x\) within degree three. But \(rq\notin I_3\): the entire original degree-three NS space is spanned by \(b,xb,rb,r^3-r,c\), and none contains the monomial \(r^2x\). In homogeneous form \(z\,x(z-r)\in J_3\), while \(r\,x(z-r)\notin J_3\). Hence the socle criterion fails for even this elementary complete block.

Nevertheless \(r=1\) is a degree-preserving retraction to the old Boolean system: the companion maps to \(-b\) and its field maps to zero. Thus no new old consequences are created at the same PC ceiling, despite the strict increase of the joint-variable PC space. This is not a PHP counterexample. It refutes the stronger proposed requirement of no active torsion for all complete blocks. A useful geometric endpoint must protect the old subspace or just \(z^D\), allowing harmless fresh-variable relations.

Evidence, route decision and next test

Exact modular elimination checked \(\mathcal F_\lambda\) for every parameter over \(\mathbb F_3,\mathbb F_5,\mathbb F_7\), including the satisfiable parameter, at degrees zero through five, plus the benign-torsion example in each field. A second ten-monomial check on the complete ENS example gives NS rank five, PC rank six, and old-variable intersection dimension two for both, with no unit in PC. All repaired checks passed. The first build had a C++ loop syntax error and the following attempted run had no binary; both tool failures are retained, not mathematical failures. family checker, full family ranks, ENS checker, ENS ranks. Compile each with g++ -std=c++20 -O2 and run with --out PATH, through the protected launcher.

Literature scope. The Jordan survey supplies terminology and multiplication-rank interpretation; no generic Lefschetz hypothesis is assumed for our distinguished \(z\) in fixed small characteristic. Caminata–Gorla, Theorem 3.1, distinguishes solving degree, last fall degree and final Gröbner-basis degree; our fixed-degree PC correspondence above is proved directly instead of equating those algorithmic measures. The historical homogenization lemma and existing no-fall criterion are acknowledged predecessors. The new value here is their precise relative-source packaging and its controls, with no novelty claim for the underlying commutative algebra.

Assessment of this line. The exploration did find a nontrivial scheme/module object: a marked graded fat point built as a tensor product of block algebras over the old PHP algebra. It also rules out unqualified Hilbert-function, full-source no-fall and ordinary-flatness approaches. It supplies no quantitative bound on propagation into the old unit, so the unrestricted goal is not closer by a proved general implication. The next bounded test is whether the tensor-factor presentation yields a degree-local relative torsion bound for several complete selector blocks, checked first against the existing ordering-source obstruction and the harmless affine block. A general tensor-product conservation principle cannot be assumed.

Process assessment. Building the object before searching for a theorem exposed the incorrect flatness and no-active-torsion endpoints early; the exact small controls distinguish the invariants without a large Gröbner computation. Future compile/run batches should stop immediately on compilation failure; no framework change is needed.

Normalization separates the column states but erases the old PHP degree thickness

21 September 2026 · Working conductor calculation and geometric obstruction with exact finite controls · Spin stops after this checkpoint. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, high reasoning).

Question and stopping point. The preceding marked-algebra cycle left a relative tensor-factor problem: can blockwise algebraic information protect the old unit at a degree cost independent of inventory? Before proposing such a theorem, test normalization and sections on the positive-dimensional old domain cone, before imposing the inconsistent PHP row equations. The inherited ordering control already prohibits a generic PC-hardness-only tensor argument, while the one-block old-target theorem pays an additive degree cost. Stop after an exact normalization/conductor calculation and its effect on the old PHP target. The user requested stopping Spin after this cycle.

Column cone, normalization and exact conductor

Let \(m=n+1\), \(n\ge1\), and work over any field \(k\). Define the homogeneous column-exclusive Boolean cone \[ R=k[z,X_{ij}]/(X_{ij}^2-zX_{ij},\,X_{ij}X_{i'j}:i\ne i'). \] A state \(a\in\Omega=\{0,1,\ldots,m\}^n\) declares each column empty or occupied by one row. Evaluation along the associated line sends \(X_{ij}\) to \(z\) if \(a_j=i\), and to zero otherwise. The resulting embedding is \[ R\hookrightarrow\widetilde R=\prod_{a\in\Omega}k[z]. \] This is the normalization, and its conductor is exactly \[ (R:\widetilde R)=z^n\widetilde R. \] Moreover, for \(D\ge0\), \[ \dim_kR_D=\sum_{q=0}^{\min(D,n)}\binom nq m^q,\qquad \dim_k(\widetilde R/R)_D=(m+1)^n-\sum_{q=0}^{\min(D,n)}\binom nq m^q. \]

Proof of the embedding and normalization. Reduce powers in a column with \(X^2=zX\), and discard products using different rows in one column. The surviving monomials \(X_T\), choosing at most one occupied cell per column, span \(R\) freely over \(k[z]\). They are independent: evaluation on the states gives tensor products of the one-column function basis \(1,\mathbf1_{a_j=1},\ldots,\mathbf1_{a_j=m}\), with the nonzero factors \(z^{|T|}\). Independence of this function basis over \(k\), and then over \(k(z)\), proves injectivity and uniqueness of normal form. Homogeneous degree \(D\) has precisely the displayed number of basis terms \(z^{D-|T|}X_T\).

Let \(e_a\) be the idempotent selecting the component \(a\). The product algebra \(\widetilde R\) is generated as an \(R\)-module by the finitely many \(e_a\); they are integral, satisfying \(e_a^2=e_a\). Its component rings \(k[z]\) are integrally closed. Inverting \(z\) in \(R\) already recovers every \(e_a\) by the products of column-state indicators, so the total quotient ring is \(\prod_a k(z)\). Thus \(\widetilde R\) is the integral closure of \(R\) in that ring.

Proof of the conductor. For state \(a\), multiply its \(n\) homogeneous column indicators: use \(X_{a_jj}\) for an occupied column and \(z-\sum_iX_{ij}\) for an empty one. The result evaluates to \(z^ne_a\), so \(z^n\widetilde R\subseteq R\). No nonzero point indicator has degree below \(n\) in the column function basis: each one-column indicator has a nonzero degree-one component, and their tensor product has a nonzero term using all \(n\) columns. Therefore \(z^De_a\notin R\) for \(D<n\). If \(f=(f_a(z))_a\) is in the conductor, multiply by \(e_a\); each homogeneous term of \(f_a(z)e_a\) must lie in \(R\), forcing \(z^n\mid f_a(z)\). Conversely that divisibility suffices. This proves the exact conductor and the stated quotient dimensions.

This is a geometric repackaging of the inherited column normal form. On the full Boolean cone in \(v\) independent variables, the same argument gives normalization \(\prod_{\{0,1\}^v}k[z]\) and conductor \(z^v\widetilde R\). Column exclusion improves that ambient cost from the number of cells to the number of columns, but not below the linear PHP scale.

Imposing PHP after normalization destroys the marked degree information

Let \(L=(\ell_i)_i\subset R\), where \(\ell_i=\sum_jX_{ij}-z\). The old weak homogeneous PHP algebra is \(A_0=R/L\). In component \(a\), \(\ell_i\) evaluates to \((c_i(a)-1)z\), where \(c_i(a)\) is the number of columns occupied by row \(i\). Since \(m=n+1\), every state has an empty row, for which the coefficient is \(-1\), nonzero in every characteristic. Hence \[ L\widetilde R=z\widetilde R,\qquad \widetilde R/L\widetilde R\simeq k^{(m+1)^n}. \] In this normalized quotient \(z\) vanishes in degree one.

In contrast \(z\ne0\) in \((R/L)_1\): a linear combination of the row equations equal to \(z\) would have coefficient zero on every \(X_{ij}\), forcing every row coefficient to be zero. The old matching barrier gives survival much farther in its stated stable range, also for this weak base since adding functional row exclusions only strengthens it. Thus the map \(R/L\to\widetilde R/L\widetilde R\) loses precisely relevant degree information already before any ENS block is added. Normalization is injective before quotienting; it is not a safe injective operation after the row constraints.

The finite-length module \(Q=\widetilde R/R\), concentrated in degrees below \(n\), records the missing information at the common vertex. From \(0\to R\to\widetilde R\to Q\to0\), the standard tensor exact sequence shows that the kernel of \(R/L\to\widetilde R/L\widetilde R\) is the image of \(\operatorname{Tor}_1^R(Q,R/L)\). This statement does not assert that the preceding \(\operatorname{Tor}_1^R(\widetilde R,R/L)\) vanishes, nor that the displayed image is all of that Tor module. The old target's survival is therefore part of the nonexact behavior of this quotient operation, rather than something preserved by first separating the lines.

Sections exist after normalization, but the uniform descent cost is too high

Work now over \(\mathbb F_p\). Let \(C\) be the homogeneous algebra obtained by adding any finite family of complete one-level old-input ENS blocks and their fields to \(R\), still before adding the row equations. It is finite over \(R\), because every fresh field equation is monic in its coefficient variable. For each column state \(a\), set \(z=1\), evaluate all old inputs and choose coefficients separately in each block: select the inverse of one nonzero input in the first accuracy row, with all other coefficients zero, or use all zeros if every input is zero. Every companion and field then vanishes. This gives an \(R\)-algebra map \[ s:C\longrightarrow\widetilde R \] by sending a coefficient to the degree-one tuple \(z\) times its selected values. The map need not land in \(R\).

The conductor gives an \(R\)-linear homogeneous map \(E=z^ns:C\to R\) of degree \(n\), with \(E(1)=z^n\). It descends after adding any old homogeneous ideal, including the row equations. This is a uniform, inventory-independent module map, but its degree charge is \(n\), already beyond the old linear lower-bound window used in our route. It is not an algebra retraction: \(E(1)\ne1\), and \(E\) does not in general commute with multiplication by fresh variables. Thus it provides no automatic bounded-PC replay. For homogeneous NS certificates it gives the corresponding additive \(n\)-degree old-target transfer, with column normal-form reduction retaining original generator degrees.

The existence of some coefficient choices on every component therefore does not supply an affordable global profile. This is consistent with, and partly rediscovers, the historical independent-input barrier: a Boolean accuracy-one block on \(v\) independent coordinates requires coefficient degree at least \(v-1\) for pointwise removal. Its proof forces the product to be the unique degree-\(v\) point indicator. Likewise the weighted column-only barrier rules out an affordable universal packing repair without row equations, while its following corollary removes the same row blocks by constant coefficients once rows are used. The geometric calculation does not invalidate our PHP-relative profile endpoints; it explains why a domain-only normalization argument is stronger than what is needed.

Finite controls and assessment

The exact new check takes three rows, two columns and all sixteen column states, over \(\mathbb F_3\) and \(\mathbb F_5\). Evaluation ranks at degrees zero, one and two are \(1,7,16\); normalization ranks are \(16,16,16\), giving defect dimensions \(15,9,0\). Every point indicator fails membership in the degree-one space. Each normalized component has an empty-row equation killing \(z\). In the original row-quotient calculation, the degree-one row span has rank three and excludes the unit; the degree-two span modulo the column generators has rank fifteen in dimension sixteen and still excludes it. All checks passed. This small case illustrates the proved normalization failure; it is not evidence for a new asymptotic lower bound. C++20 checker; complete output. Compile with g++ -std=c++20 -O2 check.cpp -o checker and run checker --out checks.json, through the protected launcher.

Focused review. Checked the free column basis, all-characteristic point-indicator degree, exact conductor rather than only an upper bound, the empty-row argument, and the distinction between a weighted module map and a PC-compatible substitution. No normalization theorem about the final empty projective scheme is being used. The Tor statement is the exact-sequence image assertion, not an unjustified flatness claim. The preserved full row equations are essential in any stronger continuation.

Contribution and remaining gap. The geometry now isolates a concrete object to study: the graded normalization defect and its interaction with PHP rows and complete companion ideals. Generic normalization and pointwise sections cannot discharge the main obligation; they erase old target thickness or pay an unaffordable conductor cost. No degree-local bound for the coupled source has been proved. If this direction is resumed, the next useful test is a PHP-relative annihilator or pairing on the defect module that survives the row quotient and couples the blocks without summing one cost per block. It must beat the conductor scale and respect the marked-PC closure from the preceding entry. This is an exploratory target, not an established promising theorem.

Process assessment. Reading the historical pointwise barriers before attempting tensor descent prevented repackaging an already excluded mechanism as progress; the normalization calculation gives a sharper explanation and leaves the unresolved quantitative task explicit. No framework change was needed. Spin stops after the local checkpoint at the user's request.

Topology proposals and a test of an augmented moment-extension complex

21 September 2026 · Working conditional lifting lemma, finite checks and route review · Ideas recorded before exploration; no full-source vanishing theorem. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, high reasoning).

Ideas retained from the supplied conversation and discussion

Purpose. The unrestricted odd-prime unary-PHP goal still needs a uniform way to handle complete selector blocks and their mixed coefficient moments. The private external conversation suggests topology, especially an obstruction complex modeled on the successful chessboard argument. These are proposals and literature leads, not imported theorems about the augmented source. The user explicitly requested recording all of them before pursuing the complex idea. Spin remains paused outside this single assigned cycle.

  1. The existing chessboard template. On a prescribed row set, compatible lower matching moments prescribe an augmented boundary; extending to the next row degree requires a filling. The recorded marginal-cycle interface and all-field matching argument explain why this topology is relevant. The range \(N\ge2s-1\) is a sufficient filling range used by that proof, not a claim that every feature of the exact PC threshold is classified topologically.
  2. An obstruction complex for complete extensions. Seek a chain complex, or a system of vector spaces over the matching faces, whose obstruction class measures failure to extend compatible low-degree moments after adding the actual companions and coefficient fields. It should reduce to the known chessboard mechanism when the blocks are absent. The sought gain is a structural filling theorem across many blocks, replacing or complementing weighted substitution. Merely writing a constraint matrix as a two-term complex is not such a gain.
  3. Cohomological local-to-global consistency. Organize local moment spaces and restriction maps as a presheaf or coefficient system, with compatibility on overlaps. Ó Conghaile's cohomological consistency work is a concrete lead involving presheaves, Čech obstructions and equations over finite rings. Chan–Ng's later hierarchy lower bounds offer failure controls as well as techniques. Their results do not automatically translate to bounded PC or to our source grammar. Our recorded common-root control already shows that many local extensions of the same old marginal need not yield a globally compatible family.
  4. Tseitin as a homological comparison. For an oriented incidence system over a field, solvability of prescribed charges is an image-of-boundary question; componentwise total charge is the zeroth-homology obstruction. This helps explain the accessibility of directly presented linear systems to matching-characteristic algebraic reasoning. It is a heuristic comparison, not a theorem that every CNF encoding of a homological inconsistency is easy for every linear proof system; encoding, degree and field must be specified.
  5. Hyperplane-arrangement complements. The full-field false sets are affine arrangement complements; complex arrangement topology and finite-field point counts suggest cohomological invariants. Lifting an arrangement with the same combinatorics and passing to its intersection with the Boolean cube are separate issues. No bridge from those invariants to our filtered image and original-generator certificate budgets is established. The earlier geometry audit retains the relevant limitations.
  6. Integral torsion and characteristic. Shareshian–Wachs provides a genuine lead on \(3\)-torsion in bottom homology of matching and chessboard complexes. Such torsion could matter near or outside vanishing ranges and when changing coefficient fields. It does not, by itself, explain the odd-prime extension obstruction or alter the all-field filling range already used. Threshold behavior is an optional later investigation, not the main task here.
  7. Fundamental groups and nonabelian topology. These are lower-priority candidates because our immediate unknowns and compatibility constraints are linear over a field. This is a task-specific assessment, not a general impossibility of using nonabelian invariants in proof complexity. Homology with an appropriate coefficient system is the first object to try.

Tests for the chosen idea. Keep every original companion, coefficient field and mixed-block moment; preserve the actual degree ceiling. Distinguish a normalized NS functional from a PC annihilator that also respects reuse after degree drops. Recover the unaugmented matching picture, detect the existing local-to-global control, and avoid demanding pointwise coefficient selections that the historical packing barriers already rule out. At the end, assess whether a structural filling mechanism—not just a relabeling of the equations—has emerged and warrants another bounded cycle.

Exploration: use the existing matching complex, but change the differential

The claim search found an important predecessor: the main notebook already has a projective-family lifting lemma for commuting square-zero operators in characteristic two, and a wide-row matching application. Its later relative refinements remain characteristic two and require supplied compatible data. We therefore do not introduce a new abstract complex merely by renaming the moment matrix. The present construction extends one part of that mechanism to characteristic \(p\), while leaving the complete-source interface explicit.

Let \(A_0\) be the homogeneous functional matching algebra on \(m\) rows and \(N\) labels, with original row equations \(\sum_jX_{ij}-z\), Boolean homogenizations, and row/column collisions. It need not be an unsatisfiable board for the following construction. Put \[ G=A_0/(z) =k[X]/(X_{ij}^2,\ X_{ij}X_{ij'},\ X_{ij}X_{i'j},\ \sum_jX_{ij}),\qquad K_s=G_s^*. \] The space \(K_s\) is the top moment-correction space: it is the kernel of the restriction \(A_{0,s}^*\to A_{0,s-1}^*\) dual to multiplication by \(z\). In row multidegree \(S\), its elements are injection arrays with zero marginal when any row is deleted. With the usual row orientation, these are top cycles of the fixed-row chessboard complex. Thus the underlying matching spaces are the ones already supplied by the chessboard argument; no new geometric model of the old PHP base is assumed.

For a homogeneous linear form \(\ell\), let \(D_\ell:K_s\to K_{s-1}\) be dual to multiplication by \(\ell:G_{s-1}\to G_s\). These operators commute. In characteristic \(p\), \[ \ell^p=\sum_{ij}a_{ij}^{\,p}X_{ij}^{\,p}=0,\qquad D_\ell^p=0. \] This remains true after any field extension. But \(D_\ell^2\) need not vanish for odd \(p\): two independent square-zero coordinates already give \((x+y)^2=2xy\). The unsigned contraction produced by polynomial multiplication cannot simply be identified with an oriented simplicial boundary in odd characteristic.

Instead use the \(p\)-complex and its alternating ordinary complex \[ \cdots\longrightarrow K_{d+p} \xrightarrow{D_\ell}K_{d+p-1} \xrightarrow{D_\ell^{p-1}}K_d \xrightarrow{D_\ell}K_{d-1}\longrightarrow\cdots . \] Consecutive compositions vanish because \(D_\ell^p=0\). The first obstruction group is \[ H^{(1)}_d(\ell)= \ker(D_\ell:K_d\to K_{d-1})/ \operatorname{im}(D_\ell^{p-1}:K_{d+p-1}\to K_d); \] the adjacent group replaces the kernel by \(\ker D_\ell^{p-1}\) at \(K_{d+p-1}\) and the image by \(D_\ell K_{d+p}\). These are standard kinds of amplitude homology for a \(p\)-nilpotent operator; Qi's hopfological-algebra work is a literature lead, not an imported exactness theorem for our spaces.

This choice is tied to the actual odd-prime source: the leading operator for a selector \(q=1-L^{p-1}\), whose linear part is \(\ell\), is \(-D_\ell^{p-1}\). Thus the complex is sensitive to the degree and shape of the selector. It is a candidate layer of an augmented obstruction complex on chessboard moment corrections; it is not yet a complex proved equivalent to full augmented PC feasibility.

Working lemma: two-parameter common lifting in characteristic \(p\)

Let \(C_s\) be finite-dimensional graded spaces over a field \(k\) of characteristic \(p\), with commuting degree-minus-one operators \(D_1,D_2\) satisfying \(D_1^p=D_2^p=0\). Put \(\delta=p-1\). Suppose that for every nonzero pair \((u,v)\) over an algebraic closure, the operator \(\Theta=uD_1+vD_2\) satisfies \[ \ker(\Theta:C_d\to C_{d-1}) =\operatorname{im}(\Theta^\delta:C_{d+\delta}\to C_d), \] \[ \ker(\Theta^\delta:C_{d+\delta}\to C_d) =\operatorname{im}(\Theta:C_{d+p}\to C_{d+\delta}). \tag{TP-exact} \] Then every tuple \(z_0,\ldots,z_\delta\in C_d\) satisfying \[ D_1z_0=0,\quad D_1z_j+D_2z_{j-1}=0\ (1\le j\le\delta), \quad D_2z_\delta=0 \tag{TP-closed} \] has one \(w\in C_{d+\delta}\) with \[ \binom{\delta}{j}D_1^{\delta-j}D_2^jw=z_j\quad(0\le j\le\delta). \tag{TP-lift} \] Conversely every such image satisfies (TP-closed), without the exactness hypothesis. A linear right inverse on the compatible tuple space exists under (TP-exact).

Proof. Commutation and characteristic \(p\) give \(\Theta^p=0\). Work over the algebraic closure on \(\mathbf P^1\). Use the bundle complex \[ E_2=C_{d+p}\otimes\mathcal O(-1) \xrightarrow{\Theta} E_1=C_{d+\delta}\otimes\mathcal O \xrightarrow{\Theta^\delta} E_0=C_d\otimes\mathcal O(\delta) \xrightarrow{\Theta} E_{-1}=C_{d-1}\otimes\mathcal O(p). \] Hypothesis (TP-exact) gives exactness on every fiber at \(E_0,E_1\). The two adjacent ranks at each exact position sum to a constant dimension and each is at most its generic rank, so those ranks are constant. Hence the kernel sheaves and images give exact sequences \(0\to Z_1\to E_1\to Z_0\to0\) and \(0\to Z_2\to E_2\to Z_1\to0\), where \(Z_j\) is the kernel of the outgoing map.

On \(\mathbf P^1\), \(H^1(\mathcal O(-1))=0\), and \(H^2\) of a coherent sheaf vanishes. These are the standard two-chart calculation and cohomological dimension used in the predecessor; see Stacks Project, cohomology of projective space. The second exact sequence gives \(H^1(Z_1)=0\), so the first gives surjectivity \(H^0(E_1)\to H^0(Z_0)\). The polynomial section \(z(u,v)=\sum_{j=0}^{\delta}u^{\delta-j}v^jz_j\) lies in \(H^0(Z_0)\) exactly when (TP-closed) holds. Its preimage is a constant \(w\), since \(E_1\) has twist zero. Comparing coefficients in \(\Theta^\delta w=z(u,v)\) proves (TP-lift). Solvability descends to \(k\) because the ranks of a finite linear system and its augmented matrix do not change under field extension. Choosing a basis of the image gives the right inverse. Necessity follows by multiplying by \(\Theta\). This proves the lemma.

For \(p=2\) this recovers the two-operator instance of the existing projective lemma. For \(p=3\), the target is a triple and the lift is \((D_1^2w,\,2D_1D_2w,\,D_2^2w)\). The middle component is required data, not something that can be dropped when only the two endpoint selectors are written. The output adds \(p-1\) old degrees; the sufficient fiber test also needs the next old degree \(d+p\). No polynomial-block-inventory bound is obtained from this two-parameter statement.

A direct arbitrary-family port of the binary projective proof is not automatic. The alternating twists begin \(0,-1,-p,-p-1,\ldots\). Already on \(\mathbf P^2\) in characteristic three, \(\mathcal O(-3)\) has nonzero second cohomology, so the old negative-twist vanishing argument cannot simply be reused unchanged. This flags a proof obligation, not a counterexample to every possible many-operator lifting theorem.

Exact positive and negative controls

Abstract positive model. Take \(C\) to be the graded dual of \(k[t_1,t_2]/(t_1^p,t_2^p)\), with \(D_i\) dual to multiplication by \(t_i\). Every nonzero linear combination remains a truncated coordinate after a homogeneous invertible linear change, even over the algebraic closure. The module is free over that one-variable truncated algebra, so its length-\(p\) strings make both alternating maps exact in every degree. Exact modular checks at \(p=3,5\), \(d=0,1,2\), find equality of the full compatible target space and the joint image. The respective ranks are \(3,2,1\) and \(5,4,3\).

Individual exactness is insufficient. On the dual of \(k[t_1,t_2]/(t_1^p,t_2^2)\), take \(D_1\) dual to \(t_1\) and \(D_2\) dual to \(t_1+t_2\). For odd \(p\), each listed operator individually has only length-\(p\) strings: the algebra is free of rank two over either corresponding truncated coordinate algebra. But \(D_2-D_1\) comes from \(t_2\), whose strings have length two. At \(d=0\), all \(p\) scalar target coefficients satisfy (TP-closed), whereas the source degree \(p-1\) has dimension two and its image has rank two. Thus compatible tuples need not have a common lift if the whole pencil is not tested. The checker saves an explicit coordinate target outside the image at \(p=3,5\). This is a counterexample to the weakened hypothesis, not to the lemma.

Actual matching-space control. Use \(m=4\) and \(m=5\) rows, \(N=9\) labels identified with \(\mathbb F_3^2\), and the two forms \(\ell_1=\sum_{i,(a,b)}aX_{i,(a,b)}\), \(\ell_2=\sum_{i,(a,b)}bX_{i,(a,b)}\). These are satisfiable functional matching boards, not balanced unsatisfiable PHP instances. The forms are weighted column occupancies, a known easy source class under the weak-base freezing route; this tests the proposed operator exactness, not a new hard query family. In each row-pair component, the degree-two quotient has dimension \(72-17=55\); each row-triple component has dimension \(504-190=314\). The checker constructs the row-sum relation spaces exactly and reduces the multiplication maps modulo them.

In both cases \(\ell_1^2:G_0\to G_2\) has rank one, so the other exactness test at \(K_0\) holds. Every nonzero \(\mathbb F_3\)-pencil direction is equivalent to \(\ell_1\) by a \(\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb F_3)\) permutation of the nine labels. This proves the same finite-field rank statements for all those directions, but says nothing yet about every algebraic-closure direction. The actual joint polarized map to the three-dimensional target at \(K_0\) has rank three in both boards. Thus the four-row fiber defect does not prevent this particular joint lift: the theorem's exactness hypotheses are sufficient, not necessary.

These tests are capable of rejecting the proposed hypotheses and distinguish a boundary defect from a successful joint image. They do not prove a general row/label range, do not establish exactness in positive target degree on matching spaces, and do not verify complete-source feasibility. The abstract checker and all abstract ranks and target indices, plus the matching checker and complete matching results, are retained. Compile with g++ -std=c++20 -O2 (the matching run used -O3) and run with --out PATH, through the protected launcher. All checks passed; no dependency installation or large exhaustive source enumeration was used.

What is still missing from an augmented obstruction complex

For an accuracy-one complete block \(P=1-\sum_jr_jq_j\), a degree-\(D\) NS functional must satisfy, for every legal original cofactor, \[ \Lambda(r^\alpha m q_i) -\sum_j\Lambda(r^{\alpha+e_j}m q_iq_j)=0,\qquad |\alpha|+\deg m\le D-e_i, \] where \(e_i\) is the original degree of \(q_iP\). The exponent support \(\alpha\) ranges across all blocks. Fresh exponents reduce by \(r^p=r\), with the degree of the unreduced original cofactor still paid. Higher accuracies expand the actual product and retain all its terms. The existing binary density coordinates make this coupling explicit, and the prime-field pairing supplies a possible coordinate interface with its larger density-degree cost.

The new complex captures a leading old-variable contraction layer of these equations. It does not supply their lower-degree terms, choose the intermediate polarized targets, or guarantee compatibility between different blocks. The common-root ordering control still refutes inferring global compatibility from separately available local extensions. Even a full normalized NS functional would not finish the PC task: the marked fixed-degree closure records the additional reuse after degree drops. Those conditions have not been identified with a vanishing theorem for this candidate complex.

The cohomological-consistency literature remains relevant, but the interface is not automatic. Ó Conghaile's construction uses compatible integral linear combinations of local sections and detects a particular obstruction to extending a local section (Appendix C); our unknowns are field-valued moments under a degree ceiling, not necessarily distributions on bounded-support satisfying assignments. Wide linear forms can mention the whole board at degree one. A support-size cover can therefore lose the polynomial-degree problem unless a translation is proved. The newer hierarchy lower bounds were screened at abstract level only; no theorem from them is used in the working lemma.

Assessment: one focused follow-up is deserved, a long commitment is not yet justified

What changed. There is now a natural odd-prime complex on the existing chessboard correction spaces, and a proved conditional two-parameter filling statement using projective cohomology. Exact controls show both a real matching-space obstruction and a nearby case where the proposed fiber obstruction vanishes. This goes beyond turning a linear system into a two-term complex. It also pinpoints the characteristic-two shortcut that cannot be copied: square-zero contraction becomes \(p\)-nilpotent contraction.

What did not change. The missing unrestricted implication—simultaneous complete-source removal with an affordable PC degree budget—remains open. The lowest-degree matching test is a preliminary control, not evidence that all mixed moments will extend. This is a working proof and route experiment, with no Lean verification or independent novelty claim.

Recommended next bounded cycle, if authorized. Prove or refute a quantitative amplitude-exactness bound for \(D_\ell,D_\ell^{p-1}\) on matching correction spaces at \(d\ge1\), uniformly over the algebraic closure, using collective row support and robust row-value evaluation hypotheses. Compare it with the existing coded-trade proof before inventing a new mechanism. If the fiber condition is unnecessarily strong, test a directly costed joint-lift criterion instead. A useful outcome must give a general range or a structural counterexample, not just more small ranks. Only after that should a further cycle try to derive the polarized compatibility data from complete source equations. No additional cycle is started here.

Focused correctness and process review. Checked the exact graded algebra, unsigned-versus-oriented distinction, \(p\)-nilpotence over field extensions, both projective exactness positions, the \(\mathcal O(-1)\) cohomology step, descent to the base field, binomial coefficients, and the finite checks' satisfiable-board scope. The prior claim search prevented rediscovering the binary projective argument without attribution. One actual matching-map test and an abstract bad-pencil control were more informative than a broad numerical sweep; no framework change is warranted.

Coded rows give a general odd-prime amplitude-exactness range

21 September 2026 · Working all-prime coded-row induction and joint-lift corollary · Positive-degree exact finite controls. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, high reasoning).

Missing implication and stopping point. The previous projective-line lemma required exactness of every pencil direction at two old moment degrees. Its matching tests covered only target degree zero. This cycle tests whether the inherited coded-row mechanism proves a general positive-degree range, rather than collecting more finite ranks. Stop at a complete amplitude theorem, its two-form consequence, original degree/label costs and below-threshold controls. The full coupled source and PC closure remain separate obligations.

Statement

Work over any field \(k\) of prime characteristic \(p\). On a functional matching board with arbitrary finite row set and label set \(\Omega\), let \(K_r\) be the top moment-correction space: the direct sum over \(r\)-row sets of injection arrays with every ordinary row marginal zero, with \(K_0=k\). For each row \(i\) in an active set \(I\), give a row function \(f_i:\Omega\to k\). Its weighted marginal is \(\delta_i\); put \(D_I=\sum_{i\in I}\delta_i\). These maps commute, \(\delta_i^2=0\), and \(D_I^p=0\).

Fix \(d\ge0\) and \(1\le a\le p-1\), and put \(b=p-a\). Suppose \[ |I|\ge 2d+b,\qquad Q=3d+2b, \tag{PA-room} \] and the evaluation matrix with rows \(1,f_i\) retains rank two after deletion of any at most \(Q\) labels, for every \(i\in I\). Then \[ \ker(D_I^a:K_d\to K_{d-a}) =\operatorname{im}(D_I^b:K_{d+b}\to K_d). \tag{PA-exact} \] Spaces of negative degree are zero. The lift can be chosen linearly. The theorem also holds with forced coded rows as below. It is a working extension of the characteristic-two forced-row proof; when \(p=2\), the predecessor's sharper row threshold \(t>d\) remains available.

Controlled insertion of one coded row

Fix a set \(F\) of forced rows, disjoint from an active set \(J\). Let \(V_r^F\) consist of arrays on row sets containing \(F\), with zero ordinary marginals and also zero \(f_i\)-weighted marginal for each \(i\in F\). All active contractions preserve these constraints. We prove the stronger version of (PA-exact) on \(V_d^F\), under \[ |J|\ge2(d-|F|)+b, \] with the same deletion allowance \(Q=3d+2b\) for all rows in \(F\cup J\).

Choose \(i\in J\). Let \(W_r\) denote the part of \(V_r^F\) avoiding \(i\), and \(U_r\) its part using \(i\). The map \(\delta_i:U_{r+1}\to W_r\) is onto whenever the needed deletion allowance is available. Indeed, the coded-disjoint-trade theorem expands every fixed-row component of a target into tensors whose forced rows have evaluation rank two and all other rows have rank one. Each tensor uses at most \(2r+|F|\) labels, and that theorem needs robustness through \(2r+|F|-1\) deletions. Outside those labels, choose a row-\(i\) vector \(c\) with ordinary sum zero and \(f_i\)-weighted sum one, supported on at most two labels. Its tensor product with the target tensor gives the insertion. The kernel of \(\delta_i\) is exactly the additional forced-row condition. No claim that this insertion commutes with the other contractions is needed.

Put \(A=D_{J\setminus\{i\}}\) and \(\delta=\delta_i\), so \(D_J=A+\delta\). Since \(A\delta=\delta A\) and \(\delta^2=0\), for every \(q\ge1\), \[ (A+\delta)^q=A^q+qA^{q-1}\delta. \tag{PA-binomial} \] Both terms have their literal integer coefficients reduced in \(k\).

Proof by induction on the number of active rows

Write \(e=d-|F|\). If \(e=0\), a target is supported on \(F\) alone. Expand it by coded trades, using at most \(3d\) labels per tensor. Choose \(b\) active rows and, successively outside the used labels, insert ordinary-zero, weighted-one factors. There are at most \(3d+2(b-1)\) forbidden labels at any insertion, within \(Q\). Divide the resulting tensor by \(b!\), which is invertible because \(b<p\). Its \(D_J^b\)-image is the target: precisely the \(b!\) orders deleting the inserted rows contribute. This proves the base case, including \(F=\varnothing,d=0\).

Now \(e>0\). Induct simultaneously for all amplitudes, using the strictly smaller active set \(J\setminus\{i\}\). Let \(z=w+u\in V_d^F\) be a \(D_J^a\)-cycle, with \(w\) avoiding \(i\) and \(u\) using it. The two row-support parts of \(D_J^az=0\) give \[ A^aw+aA^{a-1}\delta u=0,\qquad A^au=0. \] Applying \(A\) to the first equation yields \(A^{a+1}w=0\).

First correction: remove the part avoiding \(i\). If \(a<p-1\), induction at target degree \(d\) and amplitude \(a+1\) gives \(w=A^{b-1}h\), with \(h\in W_{d+b-1}\). The active threshold is \(|J|-1\ge2e+b-1\), exactly what that call needs. If \(a=p-1\), then \(b=1\) and simply set \(h=w\). Insert row \(i\) to choose \(v_1\in U_{d+b}\) with \(\delta v_1=h/b\). Formula (PA-binomial) shows that the part of \(D_J^bv_1\) avoiding \(i\) is \(w\). Therefore \(z'=z-D_J^bv_1\) uses row \(i\) only and remains a \(D_J^a\)-cycle. Its two cycle equations are \[ A^az'=0,\qquad A^{a-1}\delta z'=0, \] since \(a\ne0\) in \(k\).

Second correction: make row \(i\) forced. If \(a=1\), the second equation already says \(\delta z'=0\). If \(a>1\), put \(\eta=\delta z'\in W_{d-1}\). Induction at degree \(d-1\) and amplitude \(a-1\) gives \[ \eta=A^{b+1}h',\qquad h'\in W_{d+b}. \] Here the required active count is \(2(e-1)+(b+1)=2e+b-1\), again available. Insert \(i\) to choose \(u'\in U_{d+b+1}\) with \(\delta u'=h'\), and set \[ v_2=Au'-b h'\in V_{d+b}^F. \] Its first term uses \(i\); its second avoids \(i\). Consequently the avoiding part of \(D_J^bv_2\) is \(-bA^bh'+bA^{b-1}\delta Au'=0\), while \(\delta D_J^bv_2=A^{b+1}h'=\eta\). Thus \(z''=z'-D_J^bv_2\) uses row \(i\), has \(\delta z''=0\), and remains a \(D_J^a\)-cycle. For \(a=1\), take \(v_2=0,z''=z'\).

We now have \(z''\in V_d^{F\cup\{i\}}\) and \(A^az''=0\). Induction with the new forced set gives \(v_3\in V_{d+b}^{F\cup\{i\}}\) with \(A^bv_3=z''\): it needs only \(|J|-1\ge2(e-1)+b\). Because \(\delta v_3=0\), this is also \(D_J^bv_3=z''\). Hence \(v_1+v_2+v_3\) is the required lift. The reverse inclusion in (PA-exact) follows from \(D_J^{a+b}=D_J^p=0\).

Uniform labels and degrees. Every recursive target degree \(d'\) is at most the starting \(d\), and \(d'+b'\le d+b\). Forced rows number at most \(d'\). The largest inserted target \(h'\) therefore requires at most \(2(d'+b')+|F'|\le3d+2b=Q\) labels for its coded tensor. All smaller induction calls have no larger robustness requirement: changing \((d,a)\) to \((d,a+1)\) lowers \(Q\) by two, and changing it to \((d-1,a-1)\) lowers \(Q\) by one. The constant-only evaluation rows retain rank because the active rank-two hypothesis implies \(|\Omega|>Q\). Thus every coded-trade use and insertion is licensed. The output has degree \(d+b\); the construction can use auxiliary arrays through \(d+b+1\), which are contracted before forming the output. There is no iteration-dependent output degree or factor depending on the number of active rows. Fixed bases and insertion right inverses make the construction linear.

Corollary: the two-parameter matching lift has explicit room bounds

For two row-linear forms \(\ell_1,\ell_2\), suppose every nonzero algebraic-closure combination has at least \[ t\ge2d+2p-1 \] active rows and its active constant/predicate evaluation pairs remain rank two after any \[ Q_*=3d+3p-1 \] label deletions. Apply (PA-exact) with \(a=1\) at degree \(d\), and with \(a=p-1\) at degree \(d+p-1\). These are exactly the two fiber conditions of the projective-line lemma. Thus every compatible polarized target tuple has one lift in \(K_{d+p-1}\). The exactness construction may inspect arrays through \(d+p+1\), but the common-lift output still adds only \(p-1\) old degrees.

A concrete all-field way to satisfy the pencil hypotheses uses labels \(\mathbb F_p^\ell\), \(N=p^\ell\), and row predicates that are affine-linear functions of those labels over \(\mathbb F_p\). Remove rowwise constants on top spaces. Let \(A_i\) be the \(\ell\)-by-two matrix of coefficients on row \(i\), and require \[ \tau=\min_{0\ne c\in\mathbb F_p^2}|\{i:A_ic\ne0\}| \ge2d+2p-1,\qquad N>\frac{p}{p-1}(3d+3p-1). \] The minimum row support does not decrease over an algebraic closure: a nonzero kernel vector for the stacked coefficient matrices outside a given row set implies one over the base field. On an active row, an extension-field linear combination defines a nonzero \(\mathbb F_p\)-linear map from the labels to the extension field. Every value fiber has size at most \(N/p\); therefore any nonzero affine combination of \(1,f_i\) is supported on at least \((1-1/p)N\) labels. The displayed strict inequality gives the required rank robustness uniformly over the algebraic closure. Constant offsets do not affect the top operators.

This discharges the missing matching-exactness premise in a collectively wide two-form regime, including positive target degree. It does not assign the intermediate polarized targets from a complete source, combine an arbitrary number of forms, or establish augmented PC closure. Low-rank source tuples already have other normalization routes; the purpose here is a correction tool that can preserve supplied coupled data, not a new unrestricted proof-line bound.

Positive-degree checks and structural negative controls

A new exact \(\mathbb F_3\) computation uses twelve labels, with each of the three predicate values repeated four times on every active row. The evaluation pair survives deletion of any seven labels: at least two predicate values remain. This meets the theorem's allowances \(Q=7\) for \(a=1,d=1\) and \(Q=5\) for \(a=2,d=1\). The checker uses full degree-one targets, reduces multiplication by \(\ell\) and \(\ell^2\) modulo all top row relations, and verifies that every row-sum relation maps to zero.

The two zero-defect thresholds are precisely the predicted \(t\ge3\) and \(t\ge4\) in this degree. These are satisfiable functional matching boards, not PHP refutation instances. The failures below threshold have short algebraic witnesses. With two active rows in characteristic three, write \(\ell=\ell_1+\ell_2\). Since a row-linear top form squares to zero, \(\ell(\ell_1-\ell_2)=0\), although \(\ell_1-\ell_2\ne0\). Thus the dual map \(D:K_2\to K_1\) cannot be onto. With three active rows, \(\ell^2(\ell_1-\ell_2)=0\) and \(\ell_1-\ell_2\notin k\ell\). Choose a degree-one top moment taking values \(1,-1,0\) on the three \(\ell_i\)'s, by using ordinary-zero row differences between two predicate values. It lies in \(\ker D\) but evaluates to two on \(\ell_1-\ell_2\), whereas every \(D^2\)-image annihilates that polynomial. This proves the amplitude-one failure without relying on rank counts. Inactive rows may be added without repairing these witnesses. These examples show that label abundance alone does not replace a row-width condition.

Review, significance and next obligation

The induction was checked for both boundary amplitudes, the \(d=0\) base case, the two distinct corrections, forced-row preservation, all factorial/division coefficients, the global deletion budget and field extension. Its signed second correction \(Au'-bh'\) is what replaces the simpler characteristic-two step; assuming a contraction-equivariant insertion would have been unjustified. The new finite controls verify positive target degree and falsify weaker width conditions.

A bounded literature search located Bell–Siemons–Jones, On modular homology in the Boolean algebra, II, as relevant background on unsigned incidence homology. No theorem from that paper is imported into this proof. The actual dependencies are the previously proved all-field coded-trade spanning theorem and the projective-line lemma. Independent novelty and correctness review of the resulting matching theorem remain worthwhile.

Contribution. This proves a general positive-degree exactness range, the explicit stopping point of the previous cycle. It advances the topological correction mechanism beyond finite degree-zero controls. The unrestricted odd-prime PHP goal remains open because complete-source equations still have to supply compatible polarized targets, uniformly across the entire family and with PC degree-drop closure preserved.

Next cycle. Test that source interface directly. For two genuine odd-prime selectors sharing a moment component, derive the required polarized compatibility equations from original companions and retained domains, or exhibit a complete-source obstruction. Keep the intermediate mixed components and original costs. Do not spend the next cycle sharpening the sufficient row/label constants before addressing this higher-risk compatibility step.

Process assessment. Reusing the forced-row proof exposed a two-correction induction; one targeted positive-degree matrix test plus explicit thin-row witnesses checked the mechanism more effectively than expanding the case list. No framework change is needed.

Wide field predicates have stable conditioned moments; two selectors still need polarized completion

21 September 2026 · Working conditioned-filtration theorem and exact source endpoint identities · Joint completion remains open above the first window. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, high reasoning).

Missing implication and stopping point. The amplitude theorem fills compatible top targets, but a source must supply those targets and preserve shared moments. Test the actual companion/field identities, not another improvement of the row threshold. This cycle proves a one-predicate prescribed-moment extension, derives the two-selector endpoint conditions, and identifies the unproved polarized-completion problem. It does not infer a joint source functional from independently extended product-value components.

Stable filtration after a wide allowed-value predicate

Let \(\Gamma\) be the functional matching system on \(m\) rows and \(N\) labels over \(\mathbb F_p\). Let \(L=c+\sum_i\sum_j f_i(j)X_{ij}\) be an old affine form. Fix a nonempty proper set \(A\subset\mathbb F_p\), put \(a=|A|\), and retain the literal degree-\(a\) equation \[ q_A(L)=0,\qquad q_A(T)=\prod_{\alpha\in A}(T-\alpha). \] Choose \(B\ge a\). Suppose \(t\ge2B-a\) active rows have their constant/predicate evaluation pair robust under deletion of any \(3B-a\) labels. Then every ordinary NS functional for \(\Gamma+q_A(L)\) through degree \(k\le B\) extends through degree \(B\). Consequently, with \(I_s^A\) the original-generator NS space, \[ I_B^A\cap\mathcal P_{\le k}=I_k^A,\qquad C_B(\Gamma+q_A(L))=I_B^A, \] and there is no degree-\(B\) PC refutation. The statement concerns one old field predicate, not a complete fresh ENS layer.

The assumptions imply \(m\ge B\) and \(N>3B-a\ge2B\), so the old matching extension theorem is available throughout. Rank robustness, rather than just a syntactically large row support, is required. For \(A=\mathbb F_p^\times\), the equation is \(L^{p-1}-1=0\), precisely the old constraint imposed on a block-product component by the selector companion \((1-L^{p-1})P=0\).

The one-step extension and its original-degree check

Extend one step, from degree \(s-1\) to \(s\le B\). If \(s<a\), the new predicate is inactive and the old extension suffices. Otherwise put \(d=s-a\), and let \(\mu\) annihilate \(I_{s-1}^A\). Choose any old-system extension \(E\mu\) through degree \(s\). Define the defect on \(\mathcal P_{\le d}\) by \[ z(f)=-(E\mu)(q_A(L)f). \] It annihilates old \(I_d\), because multiplication by \(q_A(L)\) fits old degree \(s\), and it vanishes below degree \(d\), because the previously supplied predicate equations are valid through \(s-1\). Thus \(z\in K_d\).

Let \(b=p-a\). Since \(q_A\) divides \(T^p-T\), write \[ \frac{T^p-T}{q_A(T)}=T^b+r_0(T),\qquad \deg r_0\le b-1. \] For \(\deg f\le d-b\), \[ (D_L^bz)(f)=-(E\mu)((L^p-L)f)+(E\mu)(q_A(L)r_0(L)f)=0. \] The first term has an old Boolean certificate through \(p+\deg f\le s\): Frobenius reduces an affine \(L^p-L\) to the individual Boolean field consequences, with original degree at most \(p\). The second has degree at most \(s-1\), and its predicate cofactor has degree at most \(d-1\), so it vanishes by the given \(\mu\). If \(d<b\), the cycle condition is vacuous. No semantic replacement of an original generator is used.

Apply the amplitude theorem with kernel exponent \(b\). It gives \(v\in K_{d+a}=K_s\) with \(D_L^av=z\), under the sufficient bounds \(t\ge2d+a=2s-a\) and robustness through \(3d+2a=3s-a\). The assumptions at \(B\) imply all these bounds. Since the leading term of \(q_A(L)\) is \(\ell^a\), the functional \(\widetilde\mu=E\mu+v\) annihilates every new degree-\(s\) predicate multiple and preserves \(\mu\). Fixed old sections and amplitude right inverses make this linear.

Iterating proves extension for every functional, not just one normalized design. Finite-dimensional duality yields filtration stability. A degree-\(B\) PC predecessor that can be multiplied by a variable has actual degree at most \(B-1\); stability puts it in \(I_{B-1}^A\), so that multiplication stays in \(I_B^A\). Hence \(C_B=I_B^A\). Starting with the normalized degree-zero functional proves nonrefutation. This argument preserves the actual degree \(a\) of the supplied predicate and does not replace it by a lower-degree normal form for free.

Comparison with existing conditioning. The old affine-conditioning gap can already give strong refutation lower bounds after a low-degree survival check. The same weighting argument works for \(\chi_A(L)=\sum_{\alpha\in A}(1-(L-\alpha)^{p-1})\), of degree at most \(p-1\): \(1-\chi_A\) is a multiple of \(q_A\) through that degree, and \(\chi_Aq_A\) has a Boolean certificate. Applying the new theorem only through \(p-1\) rules out an old degree-\((p-1)\) certificate for \(\chi_A\) under constant-prime row/label bounds; old stability and weighted PC replay then give the inherited long-window hardness conclusion. Thus the distinctive interface proved here is extension of arbitrary prescribed conditioned moments and intrinsic \(C_B=I_B^A\), not a claim that mere conditioned hardness first became available.

Two genuine selectors supply endpoint relations

Let \(\delta=p-1\), \(q_i=1-L_i^\delta\), and suppose \(\mu\) is an old functional through \(s-1\) annihilating the legal multiples of both \(q_1,q_2\). Put \(d=s-\delta\ge0\), choose an old extension \(E\mu\) through \(s\), and define \[ z_i(f)=\mu(f)-(E\mu)(L_i^\delta f),\qquad \deg f\le d. \] Each \(z_i\) lies in \(K_d\). The literal field identity \(L_i^p-L_i\) gives \[ D_i z_i=0. \] Indeed its evaluation on \(f\) of degree at most \(d-1\) is \(\mu(L_if)-(E\mu)(L_i^pf)=0\), within old degree \(s=d+p-1\). Also \[ D_1^\delta z_2=D_2^\delta z_1. \tag{EP-cross} \] For a test polynomial of degree at most \(d-\delta\), the two \(E\mu\)-terms cancel by commutativity, leaving \(\mu((L_1^\delta-L_2^\delta)f)=0\), supplied by the lower predicate equations. If that test degree is negative, the condition is vacuous. These are genuine original-degree consequences, not independently imposed compatibility assumptions.

To use the projective common lift, take \(z_0=z_1\) and \(z_\delta=z_2\) as endpoint data (indices \(0,\delta\) now refer to polarization). One still needs intermediate elements \(z_j\in K_d\) satisfying \[ D_1z_j+D_2z_{j-1}=0\quad(1\le j\le\delta). \tag{EP-completion} \] For \(p=3\) the single unknown middle element must solve \[ D_1z_{\mathrm{mid}}=-D_2z_0,\qquad D_2z_{\mathrm{mid}}=-D_1z_2. \] The self and cross relations imply the natural annihilator and commutation conditions for these right-hand sides. They do not by themselves invoke the earlier theorem: that theorem fills an already completed polarized tuple. Sufficiency of these endpoint conditions for general positive \(d\) on our matching module is not established in this cycle.

The first two target degrees do complete. At \(d=0\), all maps to \(K_{-1}\) vanish, so set the intermediate targets to zero. At \(d=1\) and odd \(p\), assume \(\ell_1,\ell_2\) are independent in \(G_1\), as guaranteed by positive collective row distance. Then \((D_1,D_2):K_1\to K_0^2=k^2\) is onto. Choose the two scalar evaluations of each intermediate \(z_j\) to satisfy the neighboring chain equations; for the last intermediate component choose its \(D_2\)-value to match the fixed endpoint. Every prescribed pair is realizable, so (EP-completion) holds. Under the collectively wide two-form hypotheses, this yields a common correction in both degrees. In particular, using the hypotheses at \(d=1\), the system with the two old nonzero-value predicates has stable filtration and PC=NS through old degree \(p\): below \(p-1\) both predicates are inactive, and the remaining two extensions are exactly \(d=0,1\). This is a small initial window, not the compiled source's full degree range.

What the actual product component supplies—and what it does not

For a genuine complete block with whole product \(P\), retain its original degree \(w\). A source functional \(\Lambda\) at cutoff \(D\) gives the component \(\mu_P(f)=\Lambda(Pf)\) through old degree \(D-w\). Multiplying any old original-generator certificate by \(P\) shows that this component is an old NS functional in that window. The companion \(q_iP\), of original degree \(w+\delta\), supplies its predicate equations through cofactor degree \(D-w-\delta\). Thus the endpoint identities above apply at their stated windows to this actual component. For a singleton input, the one-predicate theorem provides a compatible old extension of that component.

However \(P=1-\sum r_iq_i\) at accuracy one, or its actual higher-accuracy product, already couples \(\mu_P\) to raw coefficient moments. Those moments also enter other blocks through mixed cofactors, and every \(r^p-r\) remains an original equation. Choosing a new \(\mu_P\) does not establish that it comes from one extension of \(\Lambda\) preserving all these equations. Nor does a normalized NS functional automatically annihilate bounded PC closure. These are precisely why this result is a source-interface step, not a complete-block or whole-family lifting theorem.

Exact conditioned-system control

A new \(\mathbb F_3\) check uses a satisfiable three-row, nine-label functional matching board and \(L=\sum_{ij}(j\bmod3)X_{ij}\). For each \(\alpha=0,1,2\), add the literal affine equation \(L-\alpha\). At \(B=2,a=1\), the theorem requires three active rows and rank robustness after five label deletions, both satisfied: each value occurs on three labels. The checker works in all 244 matching normal-form monomials through degree two, with degree-preserving original Boolean/collision reductions. It builds the complete NS space and iterates legal PC multiplications only from polynomials of actual normal-form degree at most one.

For every value, the degree-one NS rank is four, the degree-two NS and PC ranks are both 79, their degree-one intersection has dimension four, and the unit is absent. A negative control replaces the wide form by one cell and imposes \(X_{00}=2\): degree-two NS already contains the unit, PC closure fills all 244 coordinates, and the low-degree intersection has dimension fourteen. Thus dropping the width/robustness hypotheses fails sharply. The checks concern the conditioned old system, not a full ENS source. C++20 checker; complete ranks. Compile with g++ -std=c++20 -O2 and run with --out PATH, through the protected launcher. All checks passed.

Assessment and next step

The source does supply the one-predicate obstruction equation and the two-selector endpoint self/cross relations; these are no longer merely external assumptions. The new conditioned-filtration proof also treats every allowed nonempty proper field-value set with the original predicate degree charged. The first polarized completion window is understood. The main gap remains joint completion at higher degrees and then coherent preservation of raw mixed coefficient equations across all blocks.

The next cycle should test the higher-degree endpoint-completion implication directly, rather than prove more variants of one-predicate conditioning. A useful outcome is a matching-module joint-lift theorem from the endpoint relations, or a structural counterexample showing what further source data are necessary. Even success there will still need a separate full-source and PC accounting argument. The unrestricted proof-line goal remains open.

Process assessment. Comparing with the older affine-conditioning gap prevented overstating conditioned hardness as the novelty; the new content is prescribed-moment extension and the exact boundary of automatic source compatibility. One complete degree-two conditioned-system check and an impossible-value control verified the relevant distinction; no framework change is needed.

Two ternary selector endpoints can be completed at every moment degree

21 September 2026 · Working ternary joint-lift theorem and conditioned-filtration corollary · Full matching-image and thin-combination controls. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, high reasoning).

Missing implication and stopping point. The preceding source audit derives endpoint self/cross relations but completes the polarized tuple only at degrees zero and one. Test higher-degree completion directly. Stop at a theorem from those actual endpoint conditions, or a counterexample under the collective-width hypotheses. Do not replace the target with more one-predicate cases. This cycle proves the ternary implication using the existing polarized lift at adjacent degrees; many-form and raw coefficient compatibility remain open.

Abstract completion from polarized lifting

Work over characteristic three, with nonnegatively graded spaces \(C_r\), zero in negative degrees, and commuting degree-minus-one maps \(D_1,D_2\) satisfying \(D_1^3=D_2^3=0\). Write \(\mathsf P(r)\) for the following property: every triple \((a,b,c)\in C_r^3\) satisfying \[ D_1a=0,\quad D_1b+D_2a=0,\quad D_1c+D_2b=0,\quad D_2c=0 \] has a common lift \(h\in C_{r+2}\) with \[ D_1^2h=a,\qquad 2D_1D_2h=b,\qquad D_2^2h=c. \] This is exactly the property supplied by the ternary projective-line lemma, not a new unproved operator assumption in the matching application.

Joint linear lift. If \(\mathsf P(r)\) holds, and also \(\mathsf P(r-1)\) when \(r\ge1\), then \[ D_1w=u,\quad D_2w=v,\quad w\in C_{r+1} \] is solvable exactly for pairs \(u,v\in C_r\) satisfying \[ D_1^2u=0,\qquad D_2^2v=0,\qquad D_2u=D_1v. \tag{JL} \] Necessity follows from cubical nilpotence and commutation.

Proof of sufficiency. For \(r\ge1\), the triple \[ (D_1u,\ 2D_2u,\ D_2v)\in C_{r-1}^3 \] satisfies the four polarized closure equations. The middle equations are multiples of three, using \(D_2u=D_1v\); the endpoints use the two square-annihilator conditions. Property \(\mathsf P(r-1)\) gives \(w_0\in C_{r+1}\) with these second derivatives. The residuals \(u'=u-D_1w_0,\ v'=v-D_2w_0\) are each killed by both \(D_1,D_2\). When \(r=0\), take \(w_0=0\); the same assertion follows from the negative-degree spaces being zero.

Apply \(\mathsf P(r)\) separately to \((u',0,0)\) and \((0,0,v')\), obtaining \(h_1,h_2\in C_{r+2}\). Their mixed derivatives vanish. Therefore \[ w=w_0+D_1h_1+D_2h_2 \] has \(D_1w=u\) and \(D_2w=v\). This proves (JL), with output degree \(r+1\); the temporary polarized lifts have degree \(r+2\). Linear choices of the earlier right inverses give a linear joint right inverse.

Working endpoint theorem. Assume \(\mathsf P(r)\) for all nonnegative \(r\le d\) (only \(d,d-1,d-2\), when nonnegative, are used). Then \[ D_1^2w=z_1,\qquad D_2^2w=z_2,\qquad w\in C_{d+2}, \] is solvable exactly when \[ D_1z_1=0,\qquad D_2z_2=0,\qquad D_1^2z_2=D_2^2z_1. \tag{TE} \] Again necessity is automatic. For sufficiency at \(d=0\), choose the middle polarized target zero. For \(d\ge1\), put \(u=-D_2z_1,\ v=-D_1z_2\in C_{d-1}\). The endpoint conditions imply (JL) for this pair: the square-annihilator equations follow from endpoint self-annihilation, and its cross equation is the last condition in (TE). The joint linear lift produces \(b\in C_d\) with \(D_1b=-D_2z_1,\ D_2b=-D_1z_2\). Thus \((z_1,b,z_2)\) is a closed polarized triple. Apply \(\mathsf P(d)\) to obtain \(w\). This supplies the previously missing intermediate component rather than assuming it.

Matching hypotheses and degree ledger

Take \(C_r=K_r=(A_0/(z))_r^*\), the functional matching correction spaces, and \(D_i=D_{\ell_i}\). The coded-row/projective corollary supplies all required \(\mathsf P(r)\), \(r\le d\), if every nonzero algebraic-closure combination of \(\ell_1,\ell_2\) has at least \[ 2d+5 \] active rows and each active constant/predicate evaluation pair survives deletion of any \[ 3d+8 \] labels. The larger-degree hypotheses imply the smaller ones. In the full-label model \(\Omega=\mathbb F_3^\ell\), with row predicates affine-linear in the label coordinates over \(\mathbb F_3\), it suffices to check collective row distance over \(\mathbb F_3\) and \(N>\tfrac32(3d+8)\), by the recorded algebraic-closure argument.

The final correction has old degree \(d+2\). The middle-target construction uses no degree above that output. The inherited amplitude proofs may inspect auxiliary matching arrays through \(d+4\); they are contracted before forming the output and are not assumed to be extra prescribed source moments. No factor proportional to the number of rows or to the size of a chosen basis occurs. This theorem is ternary; no identical higher-prime endpoint-completion statement is inferred from it.

Corollary: two collectively wide nonzero-value constraints have stable moments

Let \(L_1,L_2\) be old affine forms over \(\mathbb F_3\) on the functional matching base \(\Gamma\), and retain the original degree-two equations \(q_i=1-L_i^2=0\). Fix \(B\ge2\). Suppose every nonzero algebraic-closure combination has at least \(2B+1\) active rows, with the active evaluation pairs robust under deletion of any \(3B+2\) labels. Then every NS functional for \(\Gamma+q_1+q_2\) through degree \(k\le B\) extends through \(B\), and \[ I_B^{12}\cap\mathcal P_{\le k}=I_k^{12},\qquad C_B(\Gamma+q_1+q_2)=I_B^{12}. \] A normalized design exists, so there is no degree-\(B\) PC refutation. The nonrefutation conclusion also holds for weak balanced PHP with these predicates, since adding functionality only strengthens it; the filtration assertion is for the functional base. This is not an unconditional ENS-family lower bound.

Proof. Below degree two the predicates are inactive. At each later extension from \(s-1\) to \(s\le B\), choose an old extension \(E\mu\), put \(d=s-2\), and form the two defects exactly as in the source endpoint lemma. Its original-degree calculation gives all three conditions (TE). The present theorem supplies one \(v\in K_s\) correcting both defects simultaneously. It fixes every lower old moment, so this step can be iterated. The displayed width and deletion bounds are the maxima of \(2d+5\) and \(3d+8\) over \(d\le B-2\). They imply the old matching extension room. Duality gives filtration stability, and the ordinary degree-bounded multiplication argument gives PC=NS. Degree-zero normalization is preserved at every step.

The distinction from the previous one-predicate result is that a single correction now preserves both constraints at all degrees. For an actual complete block whose product component obeys these two companions, it supplies the corresponding simultaneous old component extension within the carried product-degree window. It still does not reconstruct a global functional on all raw coefficient variables or show compatibility with additional blocks.

Full-image check at the first higher-degree bottleneck

The new exact \(\mathbb F_3\) check uses nine rows and \(27\) labels \((a,b,c)\in\mathbb F_3^3\), with \(\ell_1=\sum_{ij}a(j)X_{ij}\) and \(\ell_2=\sum_{ij}b(j)X_{ij}\). Collective row distance is nine, and any active predicate has at least eighteen nonzero evaluations after adding any constant, so rank robustness through fourteen deletions holds. These meet the theorem's conditions at endpoint target degree \(d=2\). The board is satisfiable, not a balanced PHP refutation instance.

Polarized completion at \(d=2\) requires the joint linear map \(K_2\to K_1^2\). Its dual is \((f,g)\mapsto\ell_1f+\ell_2g\) from \(G_1^2\) to \(G_2\). Here \(\dim G_1=234\), \(\dim G_2=23{,}364\). The self square-annihilator conditions on \(K_1\) are vacuous, and the cross condition has rank one, so the compatible target dimension is \(467\). Exact quotient reduction gives joint image rank \(467\). Every input row-sum relation and the Koszul relation \((\ell_2,-\ell_1)\) map to zero, as independently checked by the program.

For a negative control replace the second row predicate by \(a+b\) on row zero and by \(a\) elsewhere. Both listed forms remain individually wide, but their difference is confined to one row. The compatible target dimension stays \(467\), while the joint image rank drops to \(441\). The loss has an explicit reason: for every row-zero form \(f\), the pair \((f,-f)\) is a syzygy since \((\ell_1-\ell_2)f=0\) in \(G_2\). These give 26 independent relations in addition to the ordinary Koszul relation.

An explicit target outside the joint image is \(u\) supported on row zero with values \(+1\) at label \((1,2,0)\) and \(-1\) at \((0,0,0)\), and \(v=0\). Its ordinary row sum is zero, \(D_2u=0=D_1v\), and both square-annihilator conditions are vacuous. The syzygy \((f,-f)\) for the first-coordinate row-zero predicate pairs to one with \((u,v)\), so no common lift exists. This falsifies replacing collective width by individual widths; it does not violate the theorem's hypotheses.

C++20 checker and full dimensions and ranks are retained. Compile with g++ -std=c++20 -O3 and run with --out PATH, through the protected launcher. The largest map has 23,364 quotient coordinates and 486 raw columns, with echelon rows below 30 MB. All checks passed. This verifies the relevant higher-degree matching bottleneck, not every degree or a full source certificate; the general assertion is supported by the proof above.

Contribution, review and next risk

The exact two-selector compatibility relations supplied by the source are now sufficient in the collectively wide ternary regime at every target degree, rather than only the first two. The proof was reviewed for the coefficient two, the characteristic-three cancellations, both boundary target degrees, use of adjacent polarized lifts, and preservation of lower moments. The negative control checks the collective hypothesis actually used.

The main unrestricted goal still needs arbitrary input families, arbitrary primes, and coherent raw mixed coefficient moments with original field equations and PC closure. Two-predicate stable filtration is stronger than mere conditioned hardness, but it is not a simultaneous complete-source construction. The next checkpoint should review family scaling: identify the first genuine three-selector compatibility obstruction and whether the pair construction preserves already-corrected components. A pairwise filling theorem alone cannot be promoted to a family theorem, in view of the recorded common-root counterexample. Do not declare the full obstruction complex exact on the basis of these pair results.

Process assessment. Reducing endpoint completion to a joint linear lift made the supplied source conditions usable without inventing extra moments; adjacent applications of the existing theorem resolved that lift. One complete image test and an explicit thin-combination syzygy checked the new interface. No framework change is needed.
